- 2026-10-15 (owner request, job #45: "don't show this week's past events, only start from today", and a first try at the calendar that said "isn't connected yet" with no clear way to sign in). Recommended options, per the owner rule: the Week view is today and the next six days, not Monday to Sunday; ‹ / › move seven days, ‹ still shows the seven days before, and Today and opening the panel come back to today; the Month view is unchanged (its grid still starts weeks on Monday). My calls: the week heading names the weekdays ("Thu 15 – Wed 21 Oct 2026", "Wed 28 Oct – Tue 3 Nov 2026"), since the seven days no longer start on a Monday, and keeps the year as before; switching from Month to Week starts the seven days on the month's selected day (today, a day clicked, or after ‹ / › the same date in the month shown), as the view already worked. When Google's library says its window was blocked or closed (`popup_failed_to_open`, `popup_closed`) the panel says "Google’s window didn’t open or was closed. Click Connect Google Calendar to try again."; when access was refused (`access_denied`) or calendar access unticked, "Calendar access wasn’t given. Click Connect Google Calendar to allow it."; both offer a solid (amber, like Save) **Connect Google Calendar** button in place of Try again, doing the same as Try again from the click. Every other failure (no client ID, offline, a Google error) keeps "Google Calendar isn’t connected yet. Try again later." with Try again. Locally `&demo-calendar=blocked` or `=refused` (with `?demo-user=`) makes the first ask fail that way, so it can be tried and tested.
- 2026-10-15 (owner approval, job #46: "yes that makes sense, please go ahead"): the crew coordinator works the same from the owner's Windows computer or a Claude Code cloud session. The crew log moved from `.claude/crew-log.md` to the **Crew Board**, a private claude.ai page with a small database (jobs, Needs you items, and who the coordinator is), because the repo is public. Whichever coordinator chat the owner talks to takes the wheel: it names itself on the Crew Board, then checks each job in progress on GitHub and restarts or re-queues any whose worker isn't running, asking the owner first if the other coordinator may still have live workers. Rule 9's "main session" is now "the crew coordinator": only it pushes straight to `build/v1` or `main`, and it publishes when the owner says "publish", from either place. On the Windows computer the coordinator also keeps `.claude/crew-log.md` as a mirror so the local Needs me strip stays right; a cloud coordinator skips it. Needs you holds only what waits on the owner right now (owner, 2026-10-07). CLAUDE.md, the crew, finish-step and check-pr skills say so.
- 2026-10-15 (owner request, job #47: "for the calendar's monthly view I'd like to see some top level activities and their names, similar to … Google Calendar's actual monthly view"). Recommended options, per the owner rule: each day cell in the Month view shows up to 3 events, one line each, cut with …: all-day events first as small filled chips (amber tint), then timed events as "10:00 Dentist" with a small dot, in the Week view's order; when more than fit, the last line says "+2 more". The grid fills the panel width and cells grow taller to fit; a cell under 70px wide keeps up to 2 lines. Clicking a day still lists all its events under the grid; clicking an event name opens it (edit form for simple events, read-only view for others). Events spanning several days show on each day. Week view, editing and colors elsewhere unchanged. My calls: (1) in cells under 70px (the 300px desktop panel gives about 40px, a 390px phone about 53px) the time and dot are left out, since "10:00" alone fills such a cell and leaves no room for the name; the time is in the hover text and the day list. (2) A timed event shows its start time only on the day it starts, not on the days it runs over into. (3) "+n more" is a button that lists the day (the same as clicking the day), so it works from the keyboard; the date number is the day's button, since a cell holding event buttons can't itself be a button. (4) Read-only events look as they do in the lists: a gray dot, and a gray all-day chip instead of amber. (5) Cells keep a minimum height for two (or three) lines so the month's rows stay even. (Spotted while building) in the 300px desktop panel only the first 3–4 letters of a name fit; a wider panel in the Month view would show them properly, left for the owner to decide.
- 2026-10-15 (owner decision, job #48): while the Google Calendar panel shows the Month view, the side panel is 560px wide (about 75px per day, so timed events show "10:00 Dentist"); the Week view and the other side panels (Version history, Due, Focus timer) stay 300px, and the phone layout stays full screen. My calls: (1) in a window too narrow for 560px the panel shrinks to keep 300px of board beside it, and never goes below the usual 300px (so the narrow two-line, names-only cells come back under about 810px wide); (2) the width changes at once, as the panel has no transition; (3) the zoom control and the Find bar move aside with the wider panel, as they already do for the 300px one.
- 2026-10-15 (owner request, job #53: "there's no way to navigate back to the main BusyAnts webpage from a board itself"). Recommended options, per the owner rule: the BusyAnts ant at the toolbar's far left becomes a link to the website's About page (`about/` beside the app, so it works locally and on GitHub Pages), opening in a new tab so the board stays open, like the existing links. Tooltip and accessible name "BusyAnts website". It keeps its look, with a subtle hover like the toolbar buttons and a keyboard focus ring (rule 6). On a phone the top bar's ant does the same; ⋯ → About BusyAnts and the shortcuts panel links stay. Nothing else changes.
- 2026-10-15 (owner request, job #49: "can you add some new colors that are more vibrant and more differentiated?"; owner's decisions: keep the 16 pastels, add a third row of 8 bold colors Tomato, Tangerine, Sunflower, Grass, Emerald, Ocean, Violet, Magenta; Auto-color hands out the bold ones first, then the pastel order, repeating only beyond 24). Recommended options, per the owner rule: each bold color has a background, edge and text like the others (values in SPEC.md's color table; dark shades in `DARK_PALETTE`); the column title keeps the usual ink (at least 7:1 on every bold color, light and dark), the color's own text at least 7:1, a card title band (70% edge mixed with background) at least 4.5:1 with the ink; the Color menu is three rows of the same 32px chips, also in the phone ⋯ panel. My calls: (1) Auto-color's bold order is Ocean, Tomato, Emerald, Violet, Sunflower, Magenta, Grass, Tangerine (neighbors far apart in hue, alternating cool and warm). (2) On a bold column, the count, hints, icons and the "Column title" placeholder use the color's own text color instead of the soft grays (which drop to about 2:1 on the bold colors); the placeholder is that color at 80%, and an empty column's dashed "Drop cards here" outline is that color mixed 45% with the background (the usual dot color almost vanished on them; found in code review). (3) On a bold title band (a card colored bold, or a card with no band color of its own inside a bold column) the header icons and the title placeholder use the color's own text color too, at full strength, since the deep band leaves no room for a paler placeholder at 4.5:1; pastel bands are unchanged. (4) A "clearly colored" test checks each bold background against white at 1.3:1 or more (the pastels sit near 1.1 to 1.2; Sunflower, a light yellow, is about 1.4). Known limit: an older copy of the app reads an unknown color as its default (`isColorKey` in `src/model/persist.ts`), and if that older copy saves the board, the bold color is replaced by the default. The Claude connector, rebuilt (`npm run mcp:build`) from this version, accepts the new colors; a connector built before it says "Safe to edit: no" for a board using them (reading still works, nothing is changed) until it is rebuilt, as its README already says for updates.
- 2026-10-15 (owner question, job #52: does offline work on web and on phones?). The offline browser tests (the three tests with "offline" or "without internet" in their names, in `e2e/published.spec.ts` and `e2e/website-published.spec.ts`) now also run on a phone (Playwright project `published-phone`, Pixel 7 in Chromium) and in Safari's engine (`published-iphone`, iPhone 14 in WebKit). Recommended options, per the owner rule: only the offline tests get the extra phone and WebKit runs, not the whole suite, so runs stay fast. GitHub's "All tests" installs WebKit too. On a computer, `npx playwright install chromium webkit` is needed once, and the 4-browser cap stays. Finding: in WebKit the offline copy installs, saves the app's files and answers from them (checked by hand: a file that exists only in the saved copy loads from it), and a new version clears the old copy (that test passes). But Playwright's WebKit can't fake being offline for a page with a service worker. Its offline switch blocks requests before the worker sees them, so the page can't open. That is a limit of the test tool, not an app bug. So in WebKit the two open-without-internet tests stop just before going offline (skipped there, with that reason). They run in full on the phone and on desktop Chrome. Opening offline on a real iPhone is still worth checking by hand.
- 2026-10-16 (owner request, job #56: "the calendar should be able to be resized … to give more real estate. In monthly view, clicking on +more does nothing. The default view should give more room to showcase event headers"). Replaces the fixed 560px Month width of job #48. Decisions: (1) a thin drag strip on the calendar panel's left edge (`role="separator"`, "Resize calendar", focusable; ← / → change the width by 20px; double-click restores the usual width); it is a separate fixed element beside the panel, since the panel scrolls and would clip it; (2) range 300px to window width minus 300px, re-clamped in CSS (`clamp()`) when the window shrinks; (3) the width is remembered separately for Week and Month on this device only (`note-board:calendar-width`), not board data, undo or sync, and bad values fall back to the defaults; (4) defaults Week 420px (was 300) and Month 720px (was 560); (5) in the Week view a name may wrap to a second line (cut after two); (6) phone: full screen, no strip; other side panels stay 300px with no strip; (7) the zoom control and Find bar keep moving aside with the panel width. My call: the strip shows a `col-resize` cursor on itself only (rule 6 keeps the arrow on the canvas and blocks; the strip is neither). Root cause of "+n more does nothing": it did pick the day, but the day's list renders under the month grid, below the visible part of the scrolling panel, so nothing seemed to happen; picking a day in the Month grid now scrolls the list into view (`scrollIntoView({ block: 'nearest' })`).

- 2026-10-15 (owner request, job #54: "for the due functionality, it should show a list of all things due and their date, not just the current date"): the Due panel lists every unticked item with a due date on every board, grouped Overdue (oldest first, each with its date), Today, Tomorrow, then one heading per later day soonest first, written like the chips' full dates ("Mon 20 Oct", year only when not this year). Recommended options, per the owner rule: the red count on the Due button is unchanged (unticked items due today or earlier) and its label stays "Due today and overdue"; the panel title stays "Due"; no cap on how far ahead; long lists scroll inside the panel (it already did). My calls: (1) a day 2–6 days ahead gets the full date as its heading ("Wed 7 Oct"), not the chip's short weekday, so every heading names its date; (2) the empty note reads "No open item has a due date" (ticked items with dates are left out, so "nothing has a due date" could be untrue). From the code review: (3) the panel groups by comparing each date with today when it is shown, so a panel left open over midnight still files items under the right day; (4) a typed date that is not a real YYYY-MM-DD day (for example a 5-digit year) is not saved, since the panel would now try to show it.

(4) Code names (such as `ColourControl`, `colourMenuOpen`, `autoColour`, CSS class names), code comments, the prototype reference file, brand names and saved data keys keep their spelling; `mcp/README.md` (a risky path) is left for a later change. American English is now the house spelling for anything new.
- 2026-10-16 (owner decision, job #59: "a bolder look for the website"; owner approved the About direction after seeing screenshots, with three fixes). Decisions: all four pages share one bolder look, About is the showcase and FAQ, Terms and Privacy use the same system but stay calm; every word, link and American spelling kept; brand kept (ant icon, honey and cream family); real app pictures instead of drawings; a new Google display font is allowed (chosen: Bricolage Grotesque 700/800, headlines only); no tracking or extra scripts; dark mode, phone width, accessibility, fast loading and reduced motion are kept. The owner's three fixes: (1) the giant ant stays fully in frame at every desktop width (tested at 1280, 1440, 1920 and 390); (2) the phone nav stays on one line at 375px with every link (the ant icon stands for the name there, the name stays in the page for screen readers, text is 13px with tight gaps); (3) the dark slab is a lighter warm brown (#5A3C14) against the #1D1B17 page, with honey text at about 6.8:1. My calls: (a) the dark footer and band keep #2A2416; (b) FAQ, Terms and Privacy put their title in a full-width honey slab under the nav (one `.page-head` wrapper inside `main`), no other change to their text; (c) scroll-in motion on About only, behind `@supports (animation-timeline: view())` and `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`; (d) `about/board.png` and the new `about/board-dark.png` are Playwright captures of this app's own demo board (a seeded "Weekend in Lisbon" board), light and dark, so they show the real app; retake them when the app's look changes much. The tests' slab-versus-page check is 1.3:1 in light mode and 1.5:1 in dark mode, and heading and nav text must be 4.5:1 or better.
- 2026-10-16 (owner decision 2026-10-15, job #51: "the Completed card" becomes a panel). Owner's decisions: the Completed record stays in the board data unchanged (no migration, saved format unchanged) but is not drawn on the board; a round button by the zoom control, right of Due (on a phone ⋯ → Completed), opens it in the shared right side panel spot (one panel at a time, full screen on a phone); per board, grouped by date newest first, with list names and sub-items; unticking sends an item back with the same rules as before, one undo step each; Find and `read_board` still see it; Clean up is unchanged except that it no longer makes or places a card. My calls (recommended option, per the owner rule): (1) a board saved with a Completed card keeps it in its data, hidden and taking no space; nothing else on the board moves. In a column it is left out of the card count, the "Drop cards here" hint, the drop places and the "Delete column" count. (2) Clean up makes a new record loose at 0,0 with no settling, selects nothing and does not open the panel. (3) Unticking an item whose list is gone makes the new list at the free spot nearest the middle of the screen (there is no card to sit next to). (4) Select all, box select and Fit to screen pass over the record; the empty-board hint shows when the record is the only thing on the board. (5) Find: matches in the record come last; with the panel shut, the current one rings the Completed button and the count adds "in Completed", without opening the panel (the simplest option); with it open, the match is marked and scrolled into view. (6) Day headings keep their "2 Oct 2026" style.
- 2026-10-16 (owner decisions, job #60: a guided tour for new users). Starts by itself the first time the board is shown on a device (not on the sign-in or join screens); finishing or dismissing it means it never starts again there; remembered per device in localStorage (`note-board:tour-done`), like the dark-mode choice (not synced, not board data, not undoable; a blocked store never crashes it). Existing users see it once per device. Five short steps: add a note or checklist, drag cards into columns, the Boards menu, Share, press ? for shortcuts. Style: spotlight with a soft amber outline plus a card with "Step N of M", Back, Next / Done, Skip tour and ×; Escape closes. Replay: "Show the tour again" in the ? panel and the phone ⋯ menu; no new toolbar button. No new package. My calls (recommended options): (1) a step whose control isn't showing is skipped and the count adjusts (local dev has no Share, so "of 4"); (2) on a phone the tour points at +, the Boards button and ⋯ (the Share step becomes ⋯, which holds Share), and skips the move and ? steps; (3) the dimmed area and the lit control are covered by a click shield, so the lit control is not clickable during the tour (Next moves on); (4) a reload mid-tour shows it again, since only finishing or dismissing is remembered; (5) the Escape key is caught before the board's own handler so it doesn't also clear the selection.
- 2026-10-16 (owner request, job #62: a shorter, punchier About page). Owner's decisions: remove the big ant drawing and put a small sample board (the existing "Weekend in Lisbon" screenshot) in the hero; remove the big board picture further down; put the story and the three highlights side by side; replace the "Also in BusyAnts" list with a comparison table against Milanote, Notion, Miro and Trello, compared on free plans and dated October 2026, no logos, and once checked it merges itself. My calls: (1) `about/board.png` and `board-dark.png` are now 960px wide, 128-color copies of the old captures (about 37 KB each instead of 210 KB), since they only show small. (2) The table has the rows we could back with a rival's own help or pricing page: free limits (Milanote 100 cards, Notion 1,000 blocks once a workspace has two or more members, Miro 3 editable boards, Trello 10 boards per Workspace: all "Partly"), live sharing by link (Milanote and Notion yes; Miro "Partly" as free-plan links are view-only; Trello a dash), offline (Milanote no; Notion "Partly", apps only; Trello "Partly", phone app only; Miro a dash), version history (Notion "Partly", 7 days; Miro "Paid"; Milanote and Trello dashes) and checklist item due dates (Trello "Paid", via advanced checklists). (3) Rows we could not confirm for all four rivals were dropped rather than guessed: boards inside boards, dark mode and phone layout, imports, Google Calendar beside the board, the focus timer, and sub-items. The old "Also in BusyAnts" items that were not rows (Google Calendar, focus timer, imports with its FAQ link) are named in one line under the table; "24 column colors", "find anything and shortcuts" and "colors with Auto-color" are no longer on the page. (4) Where a rival cell is a dash it means "couldn't confirm"; the note under the table says so. (5) The rival facts come from search results quoting each company's help and pricing pages, because the build machine cannot open those sites directly; they should be rechecked by hand when the date goes stale.
- 2026-10-16 (owner request, job #61: tour tweaks): the welcome tour order is now Add a note or checklist, Move cards, Share, Your boards, Keyboard shortcuts (Boards second-to-last). The Note step says a Note is a blank card to jot down anything; the column step drops "New column makes one" and says a column is a container that holds notes and checklists. Call: on a phone the order stays +, Boards, ⋯ (⋯ last, since it mentions showing the tour again); because Share and ⋯ used to be one step, the ⋯ step is now its own phone-only step and Share is desktop-only. Steps whose control is missing are still skipped. Title of the column step unchanged.
- 2026-10-16 (coordinator, job #62): the owner is renaming "column" to "container" in everything people read. On the About page the highlights and the board picture description already say "container"; a separate job renames the rest.
- 2026-10-16 (owner choice, job #66): cards and containers can be resized up to 1280px wide (was 640px); the minimums (200 and 240) and the 700px height limit are unchanged. `CARD_MAX_W` and `COLUMN_MAX_W` in `src/model/constants.ts` are the only limits: dragging, the arrow keys on a resize handle and "Same width" read them. Loading a saved or imported board (`src/model/persist.ts`) keeps any positive width and never clamps to these constants, so existing boards stay unchanged and no saving, sync, undo or deleting file was touched (low-risk).
- 2026-10-16 (owner decision, job #67: "column" becomes "container" in everything people read; follows job #62's note). Only reader-visible words change: the New container button and its tooltip, container title placeholder and screen-reader names (Container: Week, Untitled container, Container title, Resize container, Expand / Collapse container), the delete question and its Delete / Keep buttons, the Color, Collapse all, Same width, Select and zoom tooltips, the empty-board hint, the shortcuts panel, the tour, the phone + menu (Container), the Version history line ("3 containers"), the text copy ("Untitled container"), the website (FAQ, Privacy) and SPEC.md. Code names (types, functions, variables, CSS classes, test ids) and the saved board format keep "column", so no saved board is touched and nothing needs migrating. My calls (recommended options): (1) the Claude connector (`mcp/`) keeps "column" in its tool descriptions, its `column` setting and its board outline, since renaming a setting would break connectors already set up and its answers are read by Claude rather than shown in the app; say if you want its descriptions reworded too. (2) Old CHANGELOG entries and decisions keep the words they were written with; new text uses "container". (3) Test names and code comments keep "column"; the new `e2e/container-wording.spec.ts` fails if "column" shows anywhere a person can read it.
- 2026-10-16 (owner request, job #65: Ctrl+arrow keys switch boards). Ctrl+→ / Ctrl+← go to the next / previous board in the Boards menu order, sub-boards included; Ctrl+↑ goes up to the parent board; Ctrl+↓ goes into the first sub-board on the open board; at the ends nothing happens (no wrap); not while typing in a text field. A short note names the board switched to, and the keys are listed in the ? shortcuts panel. Calls: (1) on a Mac, Control+arrows are usually taken by the system (Spaces / Mission Control), so they may not reach the browser; as decided, no workaround, and ⌘+arrows are not used (they are the browser's Back / Forward). (2) There was no separate toast, so the existing notice banner is reused and cleared after about 2.5 seconds. (3) A board with no board above it (a top board) has nothing for Ctrl+↑.
- 2026-10-16 (owner decisions, job #63: "Delete your account and data", required by Google Play for apps with sign-in). Owner: (1) boards the person shared are deleted for everyone, by the same path as deleting a shared board (its member records go with it); boards shared with them they leave (their member record goes), the others keep them; the confirmation says how many of each. (2) A panel explains what goes, offers "Download a backup first" (the usual full backup), needs DELETE typed, then a fresh Google sign-in, then deletes; afterwards this device is cleared, the person is signed out and lands on the sign-in screen with "Your account and data were deleted." (3) Firestore first, the Firebase Auth user last, every step safe to run again; on any failure "That didn't work. Check your connection and try again." and nothing deleted on the device; offline the button is off with a note. (4) A "Delete your account" web page beside Privacy, linked from Privacy and the footer, and the Privacy wording updated. (5) Not offered locally without an account. **Everything the app writes for a person** (read from `src/sync`, `src/store`, `firestore.rules` and SPEC), and what deleting does with it — online: `boards/{uid}` (their boards), `boards/{uid}/versions/*` and `boards/{uid}/versionData/*` (version history; listed apart, so a board left without its list entry still goes), `boards/{uid}/shared/*` (their list of shares), `shared/{id}` with `shared/{id}/members/*` for each share they own (deleted for everyone), `shared/{id}/members/{uid}` on each share of someone else's (left), and the Firebase Auth user (name, email, photo, account number; deleted last). There is no other profile or people document. On the device (localStorage; a demo person's keys start with `demo-name:`): `note-board:v1` (boards), `note-board:open`, `note-board:view:v1`, `note-board:versions:v1` and `note-board:version:*` (versions kept on the device), `note-board:shares:{uid}`, `note-board:own-boards:{uid}`, `note-board:share-base:{id}` for each of their shares (from the server's list and the device's kept list); all cleared, plus Firestore's offline copy (IndexedDB, cleared when no other tab has it open). Kept, as the device's settings rather than the account's data: `note-board:theme`, `note-board:font`, `note-board:tour-done`, `note-board:pomodoro`, `note-board:calendar-width`, and another account's keys. Google Calendar's permission is in memory only and goes with the reload. The Claude connector (`mcp/`) writes only `boards/{uid}` and versions, so nothing more; its sign-in file on the owner's computer stops working once the account is gone. My calls (recommended option, per the owner rule): (a) the item is "Delete account…" at the end of the File menu, in red, on a computer and (inside ⋯) on a phone, since there is no account menu and File already holds the backup; (b) the wording of the panel and the pages, including a line in the Privacy policy and the page that copies in the service's own safety backups (the managed exports and daily backups of the database move, job #40), if any, go as those expire, within 90 days, since the app can't reach them; an emailed request (for someone who can't sign in) is still done within 30 days; (c) DELETE must be typed in capitals (the box suggests capitals on a phone); (d) syncing stops before deleting (waiting changes are sent first) so nothing is sent back meanwhile, and starts again quietly, without the "Opening your board…" screen, if deleting fails; (e) the Google re-sign-in comes first, before anything is deleted, with the signed-in address suggested to Google; (f) the shared-board counts wait for the shared boards to load (the button stays off until then); (g) demo people (`?demo-user=`) have it, so it can be tried and tested locally; (h) the page reloads after deleting, so nothing of the person stays in memory. Nothing needs changing in the Firebase console: the rules already let a person delete all of it, and Firebase lets a signed-in user delete their own account (if the project has been upgraded to Identity Platform, Authentication → Settings → User actions → "Enable delete" must stay on, which is the default). After the high code review (my calls): (i) a failure after something was already deleted online says so ("That didn’t finish, and some of your data is already deleted…") instead of the plain message, since boards already deleted for everyone can't come back; (ii) another device still signed in to the deleted account stops syncing when its online boards disappear (a server-confirmed "none" after a copy was seen) and signs out with the note, so it can't write them back while its sign-in is still valid, and leaves its own copy alone in case that was ever wrong; (iii) the note shows only until the next sign-in, and nothing flashes up between deleting the sign-in and the reload. Left as they are: the version boards are each read once to find them (reading only the list would miss a board left without its list entry); the empty board's pan and zoom may be saved again as the page reloads (no data of the person's); a demo person's shares are taken from those loaded on screen.
- 2026-10-16 (owner decisions, job #69: short-form feedback). Owner: feedback goes to a new Firestore `feedback` collection, read by the owner in the Firebase console (no reading in the app); only verified, signed-in people can send it (signed-out people don't see it); each note holds only text, uid, email and the server's time; a box capped at about 1000 characters (in the app and the rules), Send, "Thanks — sent", a plain error that keeps the text; rules allow create only, as the signed-in person with their own email, only those keys, text 1..1000, time == request.time, and no read, update or delete for anyone; the Privacy policy says feedback is stored with the email so the owner can reply, how long it is kept and how to ask for removal. My calls (recommended options, per the owner rule): (1) the entry point is File → "Send feedback…", just before Delete account… (on a phone ⋯ → File), since File already holds the account items; (2) the time field is named `sentAt`; (3) Send is off while offline, because Firestore's offline cache would hold the note and leave "Sending…" until the connection returns (a time-out could send the note twice); (4) what was typed is kept when the panel is closed, until the page reloads; (5) a "n / 1000" count is shown; the box counts UTF-16 units, which is never more than the rules' character count, so anything the box allows the rules accept; (6) the note is trimmed, and only spaces can't be sent; (7) retention, which wasn't settled: kept until the owner deletes it, deleted within 30 days of an emailed request, and not deleted with the account, since the owner asked for no delete right on feedback; the Privacy policy, the Delete your account page, the FAQ and the delete panel say so; (8) the Privacy policy's effective date moves to October 16, 2026, as the page promises when what is stored changes; (9) demo people (`?demo-user=`) can send feedback, to a dev-server endpoint that drops it, so it can be tried and tested locally; (10) the repo has no Firestore emulator, so the rules are checked by the existing string tests (`src/components/signIn.test.ts`), not run against Firebase. The owner must paste the new `firestore.rules` into the Firebase console (Firestore Database → Rules → Publish) before sending works on the live site; until then a send fails with the plain error and the text is kept. After the high code review (my calls): (11) the panel can be closed while sending, since Firestore holds a note until the server confirms it and a poor connection that the browser thinks is online would otherwise trap the board under "Sending…"; the sending state lives outside the panel, so opening it again meanwhile shows "Sending…" and the note can't be sent twice; (12) board keys (V, H, Delete, arrows, Ctrl+D…) no longer act on the board from inside a panel that covers it (Send feedback and Delete account; found on Delete account too); (13) when the account changes (signing out, perhaps in another tab, then another person signing in on the same page), the panel closes and the unsent text is forgotten. Left as they are: no rate limit in the rules (the owner set the rule's exact shape; a per-person limit needs an extra document and is worth a later job if spam appears); the small panel store and the reused delete-account styles follow the existing account panel's pattern.

- 2026-10-16 (job #69, fixes after the coordinator's /check-pr of PR #68; owner said "fix"). (14) Board keys stop whenever a panel covering the board (Send feedback, Delete account) is open, checked on the page rather than on where the key was pressed, since focus can be on the page itself (after a click on the panel's text, or after a failed send or delete turns a field back on); undo, redo and zoom still work, as everywhere. After a failed send the typing goes back to the feedback box. (15) A change of account also drops a note still on its way: the next person sees no "Sending…", "Thanks — sent" or error for it (the note itself may still arrive). (16) An emailed request to delete an account (for someone who can't sign in) deletes their feedback too (my call, the simplest promise: the owner is deleting by hand anyway); deleting the account in the app still doesn't, since the app can't delete feedback. (17) Every place that said deleting the account removes "everything" (the Privacy policy's choices, the Delete your account page, the FAQ, the delete panel, SPEC) now says "everything except feedback you sent", or that an emailed request includes it. After the high code review of these fixes (my calls): (18) While a covering panel is open, undo and redo never act on the board, replacing "still work" in (14): the board's undo would change it unseen behind the panel. Inside the panel's own box the browser's undo works on its typing; elsewhere they do nothing. Zoom keys still work. CLAUDE.md rule 7 ("undo/redo always do") is about the board's text fields; a panel covering the board is outside it. (19) Everything beside a covering panel is made `inert` while it is open (the browser's own way to shut off what's behind a modal, rule 11b), so Tab, clicks and Enter can't reach the board even when focus is on the page itself; this also keeps Chromium's page-wide undo from reaching the board's text boxes. Instead of a hand-written Tab trap. (20) Ctrl+F does nothing behind a covering panel (neither the board's search nor the browser's), and the Version history panel's, the Due panel's and a side panel's Escape don't act while one is open. (21) How a send went stays until the panel has shown it and is closed (it used to be cleared on opening), so a note that finishes, or fails, while the panel is closed shows its result when it is opened again. (22) After a failed delete, typing goes back to the DELETE box. (23) The Terms of use say deleting your account and data leaves feedback you sent (deleted when you ask by email), so both legal pages are effective October 16, 2026; the Play Console data-safety draft lists feedback (email address, account number and the note's text, purpose marked OWNER CHECK) and its deletion exception.

- 2026-10-16 (owner request, job #72: "remove all functionality and references regarding due, due dates, etc."). Owner: (1) due dates already saved are **erased**, not hidden: `parseItems` in `src/model/persist.ts`, the one read path every load goes through (this browser, the online copy, backup files, Version history, shared boards and the AI connector), keeps only the fields it names, so an old `due` is dropped on loading and the next save no longer has it; everything else on the board loads as before; (2) Google Calendar (the side panel) stays, only due dates go. Removed: the item calendar button and its menu, date chips, the Due button with its red count, the Due panel, Due in the phone ⋯ menu, and the About page's "Due dates on checklist items" row; the item hover strip is back to grip and trash (40px) and the text keeps its place. My calls: (a) the AI connector **ignores** a `due` sent to `add_items` (its input schema no longer has the field, and unknown fields are dropped), the simplest option; `edit_items` now needs `text`, its only change; `read_board` no longer shows due dates; (b) the connector's "data it doesn't know" check no longer counts an old item `due` as unknown, so boards saved with due dates can still be edited through it (the edit erases them, as the app does); (c) the Trello import still ticks a card whose Trello due date is marked complete (that is Trello's done flag, not a due date in BusyAnts); only the spec's wording changed; (d) old plan notes in `docs/plans/` are left as history.
- 2026-10-16 (owner request, job #73: "please remove the arrow connecting feature between cards and containers"; owner's decision: erase saved arrows). The Arrows feature is gone: no connection dots on hover or selection, no drawing, selecting or deleting arrows, no arrows layer. Arrows already saved are not read: `readBoardData` (`src/model/persist.ts`), the one path every load goes through (this device, the online copy, shared boards, backup files and imports, Version history restores and previews, combining someone else's changes, the Claude connector), leaves them out, so they never show and the next save erases them (from every board at once, since all of a person's boards are saved together). Damaged arrow data never stops a board loading, and nothing else on the board changes. "Arrow keys", the collapse arrow and the normal arrow cursor are other things and stay. My calls (recommended options, per the owner rule): (1) the Claude connector's "safe to edit" check treats an `arrows` field left in online data as known, so a board saved with arrows stays editable there (and its next save drops them) instead of being read as "from a newer version"; (2) an online copy keeps its arrows until the next change is uploaded, and Version history copies made before keep theirs until they are cleared out (a restore drops them), since opening it alone changes nothing to upload; (3) an older copy of the app still open somewhere shows no arrows once a newer copy has saved over them; (4) the old arrow browser tests were deleted, and new tests check that no arrow parts show and that saved arrows are dropped while everything else is kept.

Honey Workshop" look (a separate job); (2) Familjen Grotesk replaces Bricolage Grotesque for titles, IBM Plex Sans stays for body text; (3) the FAQ, Terms and Privacy use accordions, built on native `<details>`/`<summary>` so they stay accessible; (4) a second Start your board, free button at the end of the About page; (5) the big honey block stays at the top of every page; (6) the existing `about/board.png` and `board-dark.png` stay, shown larger and framed; retaking them is a follow-up after the app restyle lands. From the code review: (k) printing or saving a folded page as PDF shows every section (print style), so the legal text is never cut from a printout; (l) the section headings stay real `<h2>`s inside each `<summary>` (Chromium keeps them in the headings list; some screen readers may read them as the button's text instead), kept because the spec asks for native folds and the ids links use live on them; (m) the "Completed" label and the app names shown in the phone table are on-screen only, so screen readers don't hear them twice.

- 2026-10-16 (owner request, job #85: "I would like to be able to directly edit google calendar from within BusyAnts' google calendar connection"). Owner's answers: (1) repeating events can be changed and deleted; saving or deleting one of a series asks "Only this event" or "All events in the series" (no "this and following"); changing the repeat rule stays out; (2) on events the person organises, guests can be seen, added by email and removed; when an event has guests and is saved, deleted or its guest list changes, ask "Send update emails to guests?" with Send / Don't send (`sendUpdates=all` / `none`), and don't ask without guests; (3) events someone else organises stay read-only, and answering invites stays out; (4) events with their own reminders, Out of office and Google's other special kinds stay read-only; (5) the permission (`calendar.events`) and the Google Cloud setup are unchanged. My calls (recommended options, owner rule): (a) "All events" applies the title, times, length, all-day and guests to the series, each event keeping its own day; when the start date was moved only "Only this event" is offered, with a note, because moving a whole series means changing its repeat rule; (b) deleting the whole series also removes events of it changed on their own, as Google does; (c) the guest list is sent only when it changed, and guests already there are sent back as Google gave them so their answers stay; (d) the person themself isn't listed among the guests but is kept; (e) an event whose guest list Google left out (`attendeesOmitted`, very long lists) stays read-only, since saving it could drop guests; (f) new events can have guests too; (g) without guests no `sendUpdates` is sent at all (as before); (h) the email question's Send is the focused button (Google's own default), Don't send next to it; Escape on any question goes back to the form without saving; (i) the Privacy policy's Google Calendar section gains one sentence: guests' email addresses go to Google Calendar with the event, and Google emails guests about a change only when the person chooses Send. Calls after the code review: (j) "All events" also isn't offered when All day was switched, since that too would move the series' days; (k) a timed series Google keeps in another time zone than the device's can't be changed "All events" here (a note says to use Google Calendar), since its times would shift by the zone difference; (l) the form is locked while a question is open, so what is sent is what was asked about; (m) adding one's own email address as a guest is ignored, like a duplicate.
- 2026-10-17 (owner decisions, job #87, About page). Owner: (1) a silent looping demo video of about 10 to 15 seconds replaces the big board picture (tick an item so it moves to Completed, fold a container, drag a card with snapping, type a new item), with a visible keyboard-reachable Pause / Play button and the still picture instead of video under "reduce motion"; (2) new sections in this order: hero and demo, Try it, highlights, 3 steps, feature tiles, comparison, trust band, FAQ preview, closing; (3) every claim true. My calls: (a) "Try it" and "highlights" are one section, because since job #86 the three highlights are the Try it list; a second highlights block would repeat the tiles, so there is no separate one. (b) The trust band does not say "no account needed": the published site asks everyone to sign in with Google (Sign-in, sync and offline). Its four facts are Free (FAQ: no limits, no ads), Private by default (Privacy: only you, unless you share; no analytics or tracking), Works offline (FAQ) and Yours to keep or delete (backup; deleting the account removes everything saved online at once except feedback sent, as the Privacy policy says). (c) The steps and tiles claim only what is built: Share by link (changes reach everyone within a second or two, FAQ), offline, install (computer, iPhone, iPad, Android), Version history (no mention of shared boards, where it is still planned), Google Calendar (simple events), imports. No presence avatars, per-person undo or user counts. (d) The FAQ preview uses three FAQ questions (free, offline, sharing) and links to ../faq/. (e) Video: recorded from the real app by `scripts/about-demo.mjs` (Playwright, headless Chromium, the made-up Weekend in Lisbon board, a drawn pointer; no new package, ffmpeg cuts and shrinks it to 960x600). Each of light and dark ships as WebM (VP9, for Chrome, Edge and Firefox) and MP4 (H.264, for Safari and iPhone); a browser downloads one. A small script on the page picks the dark or light clip, because browsers do not reliably honour `media` on video sources. Under "reduce motion" the clip is never loaded (autoplay removed, preload none).
- 2026-10-17 (job #85, code-review finding 1 on pull request #79, fix approved by the owner). "All events in the series" sent the whole form to the series, so if one event had been moved on its own (10:00 in a 09:00 series), changing just its title for all events moved the whole series to 10:00. Fix: only the fields the person changed in the form (title; times and length; guests, as before) go to the series, and the rest is taken from the series itself (`seriesDraft` in `src/model/calendar.ts`). My call (recommended option): this rather than offering "All events" only when the event's time matches the series', because the panel would need to fetch the series before asking, it would not cover a title or length the event was given on its own, and it would take the choice away from a person who does want the whole series moved.
- 2026-10-17 (owner decision, job #85). Deleting a repeating event with "All events in the series" also deletes the days that were changed on their own, as Google Calendar does.
- 2026-10-17 (job #85, my calls from the high code review of pull request #79, recommended options). (1) Guests follow the same rule for "All events": only the guests added or removed in the form change the series' own list, so a guest one event was given or lost on its own isn't spread, and the series' other guests (with their answers) stay. (2) Start and length are taken apart: a new start time goes on the series with the form's length; a new end or length alone keeps the series' own start (in a 09:00–09:15 series, an event moved alone to 10:00–10:15 whose end becomes 10:30 makes the series 09:00–09:30). (3) An event made all-day or timed on its own, when its series isn't, can't be changed "All events" here (a note says to use Google Calendar), since its times don't fit the series. Left alone (Low): the email question for "All events" is decided from this event's guests, not the series' (a series whose guests were removed from this one event alone saves or deletes without asking and without emails); the series fetched isn't checked for a cut-short guest list (the app never asks Google to cut it); time zone names are compared exactly (an old alias of the same zone gets the time-zone note); the pretend calendar has its own email check; the day-difference sum is written out in three places.
- 2026-10-17 (job #85, the remaining Medium finding on pull request #79, fix approved by the owner). One event of a series already changed on its own (say moved to 10:00), renamed with "All events in the series": only the series was changed, and Google (like the pretend calendar) can leave an event changed on its own out of that, so the event the person had just edited could keep its old title. Fix: with "All events", the edited event itself also gets the fields changed in the form, but only the title and guests, never its time, and only when it was moved on its own (its start isn't where the series put it, Google's `originalStartTime`) or differs from the series on its day (title, start, end or guests), so an ordinary event of the series isn't turned into one changed on its own (`exceptionChange` in `src/model/calendar.ts`, a second change after the series'). My calls (recommended options): its own time stays even when the form's time changed, as the owner asked (the series' new time doesn't overwrite a time it was given on its own); the same "Send update emails" answer is used for both changes, so with Send its guests may get two emails; when the series' change reached Google but the event's own didn't, the panel shows the calendar as it now is and says the change didn't reach Google (high review). Also: a title that only gained or lost spaces isn't treated as a new title for the series, and an untitled event opens with an empty Title field (its "(No title)" placeholder), so "(No title)" is never saved as a real name.
- 2026-10-17 (job #85, high code review of the fix above; left alone as Low). Guest lists are compared in order (Google is not known to reorder them); a Google event really titled "(No title)" opens with an empty Title and would be saved untitled (looks the same); an event changed on its own only in a field the panel doesn't read (place, description) isn't seen as changed on its own; a change with only a start or only an end isn't cleared like a full one (nothing sends one); the trimmed title check is written in two places.
- 2026-10-17 (owner approved fixes from a mobile review; job: phone touch fixes). Seven phone fixes (SPEC.md, Phone layout): (1) text boxes at least 16px on a phone, without blocking page zoom; (2) `touch-action: none` on the top bar and the two bottom bars so a pinch on them cannot zoom the page; (3) the line being typed in stays above the keyboard as the text grows or the cursor moves; (4) tick boxes get a wider press area, Completed buttons 40px tall with 8px gaps; (5) the empty-board hint on a phone reads exactly "Tap the Add button (+) below to add a note, a checklist or a container." (the computer wording is unchanged); (6) 44px targets in the tour, History and Timer, Search, the Boards and File menus, the color menu and the ⋯ Arrange row; (7) transparent 44px press areas on the card and container controls. Nothing is saved or changed in boards; no saved board is affected. My calls (recommended options, listed for the owner): (a) 16px is a floor (`max(16px, …)`), so on a phone the smaller text size (A−) no longer makes these boxes smaller than 16px; the bigger sizes still do. (b) The phone hint drops "drag one onto the board" and "Press ? for keyboard shortcuts", which a phone has no way to do. (c) A tick box's area is 44px wide but only as tall as its row (30px for one line): the rows are 30px apart, so a 44px-tall area would overlap the next item's box and the wrong item could tick. (d) The six-per-row color swatches: eight 44px swatches (352px) do not fit in the 330px the ⋯ panel gives them, so there are as many per row as fit (six on a 390px phone, five at 320px); the first 16 are soft and the last 8 bold, so the soft / bold rows no longer line up with the grid rows. (e) Item 7 applies on touch screens (`pointer: coarse`, tablets included) rather than only under 600px, since a tablet with the desktop layout has the same small controls; it changes nothing that is drawn. A card's collapse and trash can reach up above the header, not down (the earlier design reached 2px into the text below, which the "never over another control" rule now forbids); between the two, collapse takes the 4px gap and the trash can grows outwards; on a collapsed card or container the trash can is kept clear of the width strip beside it, so there it is narrower than 44px. The resize corner and width strip grow outwards; the item grip grows right and up/down (38px wide inside a container). Left as they were (not in the list): the item trash can's area (it already grows left over its own item's text, a pre-existing choice) and the tick boxes inside the Completed panel. (f) The Search bar stays one row: the count takes no room until there is one, so the box is about 200px wide (about 120px beside "No matches"). (g) Because iOS Safari is not available in the test container, `touch-action` and the 16px rule were tested in Chromium only. Not built from the review: opening at 100% on a phone, bigger delete-confirm buttons, landscape phone layout, Fit / 100% tiles, undo toast changes, tour replay, More panel overflow, the Snap switch, new-card placement.
- 2026-10-17 (job #88, from a high-level review of pull request #79; fix approved by the owner). With "All events in the series", an event changed on its own got its own change (title, guests) after the series' change, using its old id. In Google an event of a series has an id made from the series' time, so moving the series' time can change or remove that id: the second change could fail, the panel said "That change didn’t reach Google Calendar" although the series had changed, and trying again kept failing until a reload. Fix (owner's choice): the event's own change goes first, then the series'. Everything else stays: only changed fields, PATCH, the same sendUpdates answer for both. Failures, my calls (recommended options): (1) the event's own change fails: nothing has changed, the series isn't sent, and it says "That change didn’t reach Google Calendar. Try again later."; (2) the event's change reaches Google but the series' doesn't: it says "Only this event changed: the rest of the series didn’t reach Google Calendar. Try again later.", and the calendar is loaded again so it shows that event as it now is. Nothing is lost: the form stays open with what was typed, and Google keeps what reached it. After the high code review: what was changed is now worked out from the event as the form opened it (the panel passes that event to the save), not from the calendar as last loaded. Otherwise, once the calendar was loaded again, a second try would see the new title (and guests) as already there and not send them to the series, so the series would silently miss them. So a second try sends both again: the event's change is the same as before (with Send, its guests may get one more email), then the series'. The pretend calendar now gives a series' events new ids when the series' time moves, and moves their `originalStartTime` with it (as Google does, so events that followed the series don't count as moved on their own); the unit tests' fake gives new ids on any series change with a start (simple: Google does only on a new time; the code must cope either way). Left alone (Low or not checkable here): whether Google, on a new series time, keeps an event's own changes or resets the event to the series is not known from here; if it resets it, the event's new title still comes from the series, but a time or guests it had on its own could go (as before this fix, for its time); if the series' change reaches Google but its answer is lost, the panel says only this event changed, and a second try fails until the form is closed and opened again (the event then has a new id); the event's own change failing (say it was deleted in Google meanwhile) now stops the series' change too, until the calendar is loaded again (another week or month, or reopening the panel) and the form opened again.
- 2026-10-17 (job #91, whole-app review finding H2). A shared board whose online data this app can't read (saved by a newer version, say) and that was never opened on this device (no agreed version kept here) used to wait for ever: the shares were never "known", so the person's own-board sync kept holding back every board made on the device after its online copy arrived, never uploading them, and the next reload dropped them in favour of the online copy. Fix (`received` in `src/store/sharing.ts`): such a share is refused, as one without its starting board already is, so it holds nothing, opening its link fails ("This share link doesn’t work…") and the person's own boards sync as usual. A share already opened here keeps its boards as they were until a version it can read arrives (unchanged). The share is left exactly as it is online, and a later version this app can read is still taken. After the reviews (high code review, then the coordinator's check of PR #85): opening the link of a share this app can't read never takes anyone off it, whoever opens it (its owner, a member, or someone new), because on a new device or after storage was cleared the page may not yet know the share is theirs (the shared-boards list can take a while), and taking the owner off would make their other devices remove the board. Such a share stays on their list, refused on this app, and an updated app opens it. A share whose starting board is missing is still taken off the list of whoever opens its link (security review fix), except its owner's, and except when it was already on this page's list (those two are new; before, the owner opening their own share's link would have left it). That check can't tell an existing member on a new device whose list hasn't loaded yet from someone new, so such a member opening it then is still taken off (as before this change; follow-up: only leave when the join itself added them). My calls (recommended options): no new message for the person (the board simply isn't shown, as before; a link opened says it doesn't work); someone opening such a link for the first time stays on the share, so the link works once their app is updated (the cost: until then it isn't in their Boards menu, so they can't leave it from there, and the owner sees them listed on it). Left alone (follow-ups for the coordinator): a share whose online watch fails with an error other than "not allowed" (no connection, say) also never counts as known, with the same effect on boards made meanwhile; and boards held back while the shares aren't known are kept only in memory, so a reload before they are known still gives way to the online copy (the first-load rule above). The live rules only accept text `data` and `root` on a share, so a share in another shape can't reach this page.
- 2026-10-17 (job #93, finding H3 of a whole-app review; my calls, recommended option per the owner rule; changed after the owner's check of PR #87). Restoring a version saves its safety version (every board as it is at the press) through `saveSafetyVersion`, as deleting a board, restoring a backup and losing a shared board do. Restore starts that helper before reading the chosen version (owner, second check of PR #87: just after the connection drops that read can take about 10 seconds, and the copy must be queued at the press; the read itself keeps no time limit, as before, and the restore needs it; if it fails, or the version is gone, the copy already started is still saved, an extra version and nothing lost, and a retry's repeat copy is removed again; a failed read after the person moved on drops the restore silently too). That helper now starts the save at once, before reading the version list (owner, check of PR #87: just after the connection drops, the list read alone can take over 3 seconds, and the restore must not go ahead before the safety version is queued on the device). The wait is at most 3 seconds (VERSION_SAVE_WAIT_MS, its timer cleared once done) and starts with the save already queued; offline, the save stays queued and is sent once back online. Only after the save answers is the list read, within the same 3 seconds (my call: the owner asked for the limit on the save only; the copy is queued at the press either way, which closes the gap, and finishing the tidy-up first keeps the version count settled when the action goes ahead): if the newest version saved no later than it has the same boards, the later of the two is removed again (so the sharing case that relies on "none is added" still ends with one copy, and two same copies answering together keep exactly one, never none), otherwise the oldest past the limit are dropped. If the page is closed before an offline save answers, that tidy-up doesn't run: a repeat can then stay in the history (and the list can be one over the limit until the next version trims it); nothing is lost. A save refused within the 3 seconds stops the restore with "Couldn't restore it", as before; so does one refused after the 3 seconds but before the restore happened (while the chosen version was still being read, or in the same moment the 3 seconds end: the refusal is remembered), since by then it is known the copy is lost; one refused after the restore went ahead shows the notice banner: "The boards from before your restore couldn't be saved in Version history." (no promise that Ctrl+Z still works: the refusal can come hours later; no note when the restore was dropped or waited for shared boards, since nothing was replaced). A save refused within the 3 seconds after the person already moved on drops the restore silently, like any other moved-on restore. Delete, backup and shared-board loss keep going ahead silently whatever the save does, as before. If by then the version on screen is not the one Restore was pressed on (Back to current, the history closed, another version picked, or even the same version picked again after going back) or another board is open (picked by the person, or boards arriving from another device moved it), the restore is dropped silently and changes nothing: the screen already shows the person moved on. Online versions now save their board and their list entry in one Firestore batch (`versionsRemote` save, `src/sync/firebase.ts`) instead of the list entry only after the board reached the server: offline, both are kept on the device together, so closing or reloading the page before the connection is back no longer leaves a version saved without its list entry (missing from the history). No browser test: the dev build keeps versions on the device, where saving never waits; unit tests cover it. Left alone (code review, high): while it waits, Restore on another version picked in the list is ignored (the button is still busy; the first restore is then dropped, so press again after it); a change to the open board arriving from another device during the wait is replaced by the restore (Ctrl+Z brings it back, as for any restore); the same wait in restoring a one-board backup (the open board could change meanwhile) is not guarded, a follow-up. Also left (round-2 review, high): on the device-only version store (dev build), a nearly full storage now refuses a safety copy that repeats the newest version (it is written before the check), stopping the restore with "Couldn't restore it" where it used to go ahead; online, a repeat safety copy costs a write and a delete instead of only a list read. Both come with saving first, as the owner asked.

Signed in and offline, with saves this device had not sent before the page was closed, the board never opened: the watch of `boards/{uid}` (`boardRemote` in `src/sync/firebase.ts`) skipped every snapshot holding unconfirmed saves, and offline the server never confirms them. Fix: until the board has opened, a snapshot with unconfirmed saves is used when it holds a board, marked `pending`; it is this device's newest copy, and the server ends up holding it once the saves arrive (most recent change wins, unchanged). Nothing extra is sent. The save note says "Saving…" until the server has that version, then "Saved". After opening, unconfirmed snapshots are still skipped, as before. A `pending` version never counts as "an online copy has been seen", so the server going back to "no board" after refusing those saves is not taken for an account deleted elsewhere (which signs out). An unsent delete (document missing) is never taken for "no online board", so a deletion is not undone by uploading this device's boards; reopened offline, that case still waits (only deleting the account makes one). Known limit, as before this fix: if the server refuses the saves the closed page left (too big, rules), Firestore puts the online copy back and it is shown. If there is no online copy, this page uploads its boards once, so a refusal shows the "Couldn't save online" banner as with any upload. Another open tab's later unsent saves are not seen until they reach the server (as before); if both tabs edit offline, the most recent change wins. Tried and dropped after the high review: sending the unsent copy again as the new page's own, so a refusal would show "Couldn't save online"; it could land after another open tab's newer save and replace it, and doubled the upload on every reload. (f) adding the board card on home is one change on home, exactly as Add a sub-board: Ctrl+Z on home removes that card, while the Journal board itself stays (listed on its own in the Boards menu) and the button keeps using it, so no second board is made; My calls: (1) a double tap is the second tap starting within 300ms of the first lifting and within 30px of it (nothing existed to reuse; `src/model/tapZoom.ts`, a `// simple:` fixed pair); (2) a long tap uses the existing hold time for picking up cards (`HOLD_MS`, 450ms) and zooms out **when the finger lifts**, not when the time is up, so resting and then swiping still pans (Hand tool) or draws the box (Select tool) exactly as before, with no zoom; a rest that never moves, with the Select tool, selects nothing and only clears the selection as a tap on the empty board always did, so there is no conflict with an existing gesture; (3) the browser's own double-tap page zoom was already off on the board (`touch-action: none` on `.canvas`), so nothing was added for it; (4) the long-press copy / paste menu is kept away on a long tap on the board (`onContextMenu` is cancelled while a finger is down on it); (5) mouse and trackpad are unchanged (touch input only). **Bug, same job (owner report):** on a phone, with several blocks selected, a long press then move dragged only the one under the finger. Fix: the hold keeps a selection the block is already part of; a touch on a text box inside a bigger selection leaves it alone until the finger lifts, and a quick tap on that text then narrows it to that block, as before. - 2026-10-18 (owner decision, job 98 follow-up: "Fix the gap too, then merge."). Before, someone already on a shared board who opened its link on a new device, before that device's list of shared boards had loaded, was taken off it when its data was damaged, in the old format or older, and so lost it on their other devices too. Now opening a link takes someone off a refused share only when that same link-open added them. Joining now says whether it added the person (`join` in `src/store/collab.ts`, `true` only when they weren't on it), and `join()` in `src/store/sharing.ts`, which every link-open goes through, leaves only then. Online (`src/sync/firebase.ts`) this is asked of the server just before joining: reading one's own member record works only for someone already on it, so "not allowed" means new; any other answer (no connection, say) counts as already on it, so when unsure nobody is taken off (a newcomer could then stay on a refused share, invisible, as before job 98; rare, and it never loses anyone's boards). No change to `firestore.rules`, to anything saved online or on a device. The unit tests cover an existing member on a new device keeping it, a newcomer still taken off, and the online answer in each case; the browser test now also checks the newcomer really was added before being taken off. After the high code review, left alone (as before this change, for the owner): someone already on it who opens the link on a new device still has their member record written again, which moves them to the end of the people list, and an old or turned-off copy of the link fails for them there ("This share link doesn’t work…"; the share still comes through their list of shared boards); skipping that write would fix both but would let members open turned-off links, so it needs the owner. Also: the read and the write are two steps, so a join from another tab in between isn't seen (`// simple:` comment, with the unsure case); each link-open now waits for one extra server read.

(4) Back to "This board" limits matches to the open board again; Escape or × closes the bar and resets the scope. Small calls made while building (in the same direction, nothing else changed): the order is fixed (`ui.find.from`) when the words or the switch last changed, so stepping does not reshuffle the list when a match opens another board; the first match on another board is shown in the count and not opened until Enter; clicking the switch leaves the cursor in the box so Enter still steps; board names are not searched (the rules are as before); the Journal board opens like any board when a match is on it. Known limit, same as the Boards menu: leaving a new, empty, unnamed board that no card opens discards it, so a search that opens another board from such a board does the same.

- 2026-10-19 (job 101, owner request "include Emil Kowalski's design skill and incorporate it throughout", choice "Rule + apply all fixes"; the polish pass, pull request `design-polish`). Applied the audit in `docs/design-polish-review.md` to the whole app, phone layout and website included. SPEC.md "Look and feel" (Motion) and "Phone layout" (Phone-native feel) describe it as built. Owner colours, sizes and decisions were kept; what would have conflicted is listed there as held. My calls (recommended options): (1) **Keyboard means no motion.** `main.tsx` sets `html[data-input='keys']` on a real key press (a lone Shift / Ctrl / Alt / Cmd doesn't count) and clears it on any pointer press; all new motion, the block glide and the collapse arrow are off while it is set, and `keyboardInitiated()` (in `src/store/env.ts`, used by `toggleItem`) makes Ctrl+Enter and a keyboard tick on the Done box tick at once instead of fading out for 280ms. A menu opened by clicking its button is a pointer action and animates; opened with Enter it doesn't. (2) All new motion is only for people who allow motion (inside `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`), including the press shrink, so with reduced motion there is nothing to turn off; a bar button's pressed tint shows in both cases. (3) Opening motion is 150ms ease-out from 96% scale with `transform-origin` at the opening button; the Color menu fades only, because `Toolbar.tsx` pans the board by that menu's box at the moment it opens; closing stays instant (React removes the element); the format bar has no motion (it appears on every selection). (4) Held, not changed: the 280ms and 450ms of the tick animation (decision 2026-10-02, and in the store), the glide staying on `left` / `top` / `width` (blocks are placed that way), `viewport-fit=cover` and `interactive-widget` (need a real phone), animating the website's folds (printing and native `<details>`). (5) Hover colours do not fade (hover is seen tens of times a day); they now apply only under `@media (hover: hover)`, so a tap on a phone leaves nothing tinted. The checklist row buttons keep their old hover rules, hidden on touch by the 2026-10-04 decision. (6) Impeccable's critique was not run: its launcher downloads a program on first use, and this job was told to install and fetch nothing; a by-hand look at the main screens found no look changes to make (three observations are in the review file for the owner). (7) The website tests were added right after the CSS, not before; the app and phone tests were written first and seen to fail.
- 2026-10-19 (owner request, job "press-always"): the press shrink (`scale(0.97)` on `:active`, 100ms; the website's Start and Open buttons 120ms) plays even when the computer is set to reduce motion; the rest still respects it (menu, panel and phone-sheet entrance, the fold and collapse arrows' turn, the tick fade, the block glide). Reason: the shrink is a tiny scale in place, not movement across the screen, so it is not what reduce-motion settings are for, and without it a button gives no sign it heard the press (the owner has "Animation effects" off in Windows and saw no press feedback). The 100ms transition on the press stays for everyone too. A held Space on a focused button already shrinks it, and that is accepted. Changes `src/styles.css` and `src/site.css` (the press rules moved out of the no-preference block); SPEC.md Motion wording updated.
- 2026-10-19 (owner choice, job 103: Google Calendar to "stay connected after being connected once"; option "fix what's wrong only"; high-risk pull request #98, trade-off approved by the owner). Before, the calendar access (about an hour) was kept in memory only, so every reload showed Google's quick window. Now it is also kept in this tab's `sessionStorage` (key `note-board:calendar-access`, never `localStorage`) with its expiry time and the signed-in account's id (`src/model/calendar.ts` `keepAccess` / `readAccess` / `dropAccess`, used by `src/calendar/google.ts` and the local demo calendar). It is used only for the same person with over a minute left; it is dropped on sign-out and when another person signs in (the always-loaded sign-in watcher, `src/sync/firebase.ts` `watchUser`), when Google answers 401 (the existing `forget`), and when it runs out or is damaged (on read). Every storage access is in try/catch, and anything bad or missing falls back to the usual ask. Limit (`// simple:` comment): it covers reloads in the same tab within the hour; a new tab, a new day or another device still shows Google's quick window, though browsers copy a tab's `sessionStorage` when it is duplicated and restore it when a closed tab or the whole browser is reopened, so the access can carry over then until it runs out. Trade-off, stated plainly: anything that could run script in the page (a cross-site scripting bug, a bad browser extension), or another page on the same address (`ezhang43.github.io`, shared by every site published there) opened in the same tab, could read the token from the tab's storage for up to an hour and use it on the person's calendar; before, it would have had to catch it in memory while the page was open, which such script could largely do too. The token only reaches Google Calendar (`calendar.events`), and the person can take the permission back in their Google account. My calls (recommended options): a token that arrives after a different person signed in is not kept; the Privacy policy's calendar sentence now says the permission is kept only in this browser tab, and its effective date moves to October 19, 2026 (the Terms stay October 16). Sign-in, Firebase, scopes and board saving are unchanged. Code review (high) fixes: a Google answer that arrives after another person signed in no longer blocks that person's own ask (the pending ask is per person); an odd expiry from Google counts as run out rather than never; the wording about closing the tab was corrected (see above); and the demo's `&demo-calendar=blocked` note says it needs a new tab once connected. Left alone: a 401 for an older token can drop a newer one (as before, in memory; the next call just asks again), and with nobody signed in nothing is kept (the panel only shows when signed in). Owner's check of PR #98 (2026-10-19): the owner explicitly approved the trade-off (keeping the access in `sessionStorage` for up to an hour) and asked for four fixes, now made: (1) the tab's copy is dropped on sign-out and on a change of person by the sign-in watcher that is always loaded (`src/sync/firebase.ts` `watchUser`), not only once the calendar module has loaded, so a sign-out before the calendar ever showed (on a phone, before the ⋯ sheet opens; an account deleted elsewhere; a lost session) still drops it; (2) SPEC's summary of the legal pages matches the corrected Privacy wording; (3) a stored expiry that isn't a finite number (Infinity included) is ignored, and an odd one from Google is not kept; (4) in the demo, `&demo-calendar=blocked` / `=refused` acts only on the tab's first ask, so in a tab already connected it has no effect, not later either, as SPEC says. Sign-out is covered by unit tests of `watchUser` and `google.ts` with a pretend Firebase sign-in, since the local demo has no sign-out; reload, another person, expiry, damaged data and 401 are covered by browser tests with the demo calendar.
- 2026-10-19 (owner request, job 112: color themes for people who want to customise). Owner's choices: (1) each theme changes the accent color and the board's background tint; cards stay white (in dark mode, as dark mode has them); (2) six curated themes, Honey (today's look, the default), Ocean, Forest, Lavender, Rose, Slate, each with a light and a dark version, with readable contrast (WCAG AA); (3) kept on this device only, the same way dark mode is (`note-board:color-theme`, `view.colorTheme`; no sync change, nothing in board data); (4) picked from an Appearance menu that replaces the moon button in the toolbar and holds the light / dark choice and the theme swatches; on a phone it is a row in ⋯ More. Built on the existing dark-mode mechanism: `<html data-color-theme>` beside `data-theme`, with the colors as CSS variables defined once in `styles.css` (Honey is the plain `:root` look with no rules of its own; every other theme has a light block and a dark block that set the same variables). The browser's title-bar color is now read from the page's `--toolbar-bg`, so it follows the theme. My calls (recommended options, per the owner rule): (a) "board background tint" covers the canvas and its grid dots and also the toolbar and panel surfaces and their lines, so a warm cream toolbar doesn't sit on a blue board (cards, text, tick boxes, container colors and the BusyAnts logo are untouched; an uncolored container keeps its warm default); (b) "accent" means the accent text and focus rings, the pressed tool and Share ("honey"), pressed switches and chips, the New container button and the drop highlight; (c) the Dark mode control inside the menu is a switch (the same as the phone's), keeping its name "Dark mode" and its pressed state; the menu stays open while it is used, so the result is seen at once; (d) on a phone the Appearance row replaces the Dark mode row in ⋯ More and opens inside the panel, with chips 44px tall; (e) the themes' contrast is proved by unit tests that read the stylesheet itself (no second copy of the colors); the dot shown in the menu is the one value kept in TypeScript, and a test checks it equals the CSS; (f) the saved value is read in `src/store/store.ts` and not in the saving code (`core.ts`), so no saving, sync, undo or deleting file changed; `note-board:color-theme` is a device setting, so deleting an account keeps it, like `note-board:theme` (the unit test list of kept keys has it). Nothing is added to the board data, so boards already saved are unaffected.
