- 2026-10-19 (owner choice, job 107: Google Calendar "Remember for the hour"; high-risk pull request). After PR #98 the owner still saw Google's quick window when closing and reopening the site, because the access lived in the tab's `sessionStorage`, which the browser forgets when the tab closes. Offered (a) a server-held refresh key (never flashes; big), (b) "Remember for the hour", (c) leave it; the owner chose (b). Now the access (same key `note-board:calendar-access`, same checks: same account id, over a minute left, anything odd or damaged dropped, every storage access in try/catch) is kept in `localStorage`, so a reload, another tab or reopening the browser within the hour opens the calendar without Google's window. Google's window still shows about once an hour of use, when the access runs out, and on another device. It is dropped on sign-out and when another person signs in (`src/sync/firebase.ts` `watchUser`; Firebase tells every tab of the browser, so this covers another tab too, and `readAccess`'s account check means no one else's access is ever used), when Google answers 401 (`forget`), when it runs out, and when the account is deleted (`src/store/account.ts` `accountKeys` now includes the key; it isn't a setting; for demo people it has no per-person prefix, so deleting one demo person removes whichever demo person's access is kept, who then just connects again). Backups and exports hold board data only (`serializeWorkspace`), never this key. Trade-off, stated plainly: `localStorage` keeps the token readable by anything that can run script on the page (a cross-site scripting bug, a bad browser extension) or by another page on the same address (`ezhang43.github.io`, shared by every site published there) until it runs out, up to about an hour, and now also after the tab is closed; the token only reaches the person's calendar events (`calendar.events`), and the person can take the permission back in their Google account. My calls (recommended options): a copy kept for the tab by the earlier version is moved to `localStorage` by the sign-in watcher when it passes the same checks (same person, over a minute left), and removed otherwise (a `// simple:` line that can go after 2026-11-19; added after the high-level code review, so nobody connected is asked again); no `storage` event listener (the sign-in watcher already runs in every tab); the Privacy policy's calendar sentence now says the permission is kept in this browser on this device until it runs out (effective date unchanged, October 19, 2026); in the demo, `&demo-calendar=blocked` now needs a private window once connected. Checked, no change needed: before Firebase restores the signed-in person on load, nothing reads the kept access with no one signed in: Firebase calls `watchUser` only once the saved sign-in has loaded, and the calendar button shows only once the board is ready for a signed-in person, so the reload flash was the tab's storage being forgotten, not a wrong drop. People already connected: a tab still open keeps its access (moved to the device on the next load), so nobody is asked again because of this change; after that, Google's window shows about once an hour of use. Left alone after the review: a 401 in one tab can drop a newer access another tab just kept (costs one extra Google window, errs on the safe side); while a tab still runs the old version, its sign-out clears only its tab copy, but the account check means no one else's access is ever used. Board data and saving are unchanged.

No saving, sync, undo or deleting file changed (the choice rides in the view state like the color theme, loaded in `src/store/store.ts`), so this is a normal low-risk pull request. - 2026-10-19 (owner request, job 119: rich text in Journal notes only). Lists (Enter continues, Tab / Shift+Tab indent), bold / italic / underline / strikethrough on selected words, headings and text size, fonts, and a highlight color, in the Journal panel only; every other block stays plain. The highlight overrides the "No colors" rule for the Journal only (owner's request). Calls made while building, on the safest option (rule 4): (1) save format: the note's `text` stays the plain truth (date line, blank line, plain body) and an optional `rich` field beside it holds the formatting as small typed blocks (paragraph, heading 1 / 2, bulleted or numbered item with indent 1 to 4) and runs (b, i, u, s, size small / large, font serif / rounded / hand / mono, highlight yellow / green / blue / pink); no HTML is ever stored or drawn (the editor builds the page with createElement and textContent only); (2) the formatting is used only while its plain text still equals the note's body, so a per-field sync merge, an older copy of the app, an edit on the board or an MCP edit that changes `text` simply shows that plain text, never mixed-up formatting; (3) no `BOARD_VERSION` bump and `firestore.rules` unchanged (they only check the board's size); a plain note keeps exactly the old saved shape (no `rich` key), so boards already saved load unchanged; (4) every load path (this device, sync, versions, backups, import, sharing) goes through `parseCard`, which keeps only the known blocks and looks above and caps a note at 20,000 lines of 2,000 runs (a note bigger than that is saved as plain text straight away, so its formatting never vanishes on a reload); (5) the plain text of a list item is "• " or "1. " after two spaces per level; numbers restart after a plain line, after a bulleted item at the same level, and for each new run of deeper items; headings are their words; (6) Shift+Enter is the same as Enter (no line break inside a line); Enter on an empty item ends the list; Enter at the end of a heading starts a plain line; (7) Backspace at the start of an item first outdents it, then makes it a plain line, then joins it to the line above; (8) Tab indents only in a list, elsewhere it moves on (no keyboard trap); (9) copy gives plain text without list markers; paste and drop take only plain text; (10) typing in the note on the Journal board (a plain note there) drops its formatting, undoable; (11) each formatting change is its own undo step, typing merges as usual; (12) shortcuts Ctrl+B / I / U, Ctrl+Shift+X strikethrough, Ctrl+Shift+8 / 7 bulleted / numbered list (as in Google Docs); (13) the highlight colors are soft light tints, with darker versions in dark mode. From the high code review: (14) plain typing inside a line, and any edit a phone keyboard won't let the app stop, is left to the browser and read back, so spelling, autocorrect and suggestions keep working; (15) dragging words inside a note is not offered (it would have copied them); (16) the Style, Text size, Font and Highlight menus show List or Mixed when that is what is selected, so any choice acts; (17) left as before: typing in the panel and on the Journal board's card within a moment of each other is one undo step, as before this job. This touches saving, so it is a high-risk pull request for the owner to merge.

A click on a header button now counts only when the press began on that button (keyboard clicks still do), checked in `Canvas.tsx`. (g) Resize handles, the checklist item buttons and the item bar are not part of this (job 115 owns the item bar and the row trash). Tablets and wider windows keep their always-visible buttons. Not checkable without a real phone: whether iPhone Safari keeps the keyboard up while the cursor jumps to another box (it is the same hand-off Enter and Backspace already use).
- 2026-10-19 (owner OK, "audit job D" second half, job 114; low-risk, phone layout only). On the phone layout (under 600px, by finger), pressing and holding a checklist item for about half a second lifts it (a shadow, the other rows slide aside, its label follows the finger); moving drags it, and the drop uses the existing item-drag rules (`dropOnRow`, `moveItems`), so saving and undo are unchanged. Calls made while building (recommended options, per the owner rule): (1) resting on an item (its text included) now lifts the item instead of the whole card, on the phone layout only; resting on the title, the blank card space, a note or a link still picks up the card, and tablets and wider windows keep the card pickup (this narrows the 2026-10-18 "hold anywhere on a card" rule, which stays for everything but a checklist item's row). (2) The grip still drags at once. (3) The press-and-hold timing and the "move first = pan the board" rule are the existing ones (`holdOrPan`, 450ms), so a tap types, a swipe pans and a short rest does nothing. (4) The floating label is as wide as the item, kept on the screen, on the finger's line just above it; it moves by `translate`. A grip drag on a computer keeps its small label, now also placed by `translate` (same look). (5) The rows slide by the lifted rows' measured height (taken once when lifted); the lifted item's own place is hidden, and the rows below it slide up to close it, so nothing overlaps. Into another list, that list opens the gap and this one closes up (the other card does not grow while held, so rows pushed past its bottom edge show over the board until the drop). (6) Where a hint is worked out, the rows are read as laid out without the slide, or the gap would move the hint and back. (7) Motion: a 200ms `--ease-out` transform transition on the rows and a 140ms fade and grow on the label, both off with reduced motion (then the rows jump at once, which is state, not movement). Not checkable without a real phone: whether holding a row with the keyboard up (it closes, as for a card hold) shifts the board under the finger before the lift; how the hold feels on iPhone Safari (the text-selection loupe is kept away by the same code the card hold uses).
- 2026-10-19 (owner OK, "audit job B", job 116; phone layout only, the desktop layout is unchanged): the line being typed in is always kept in view on a phone. The code that kept it above the keyboard (`keepCaretClear` in `src/components/ItemBar.tsx`) only looked at the bottom edge and only reacted to focus, typing and the cursor moving; it now (1) also keeps the line below the top bar and, when the phone has scrolled the page up (iPhone Safari moves the visual viewport down), below the part of the screen that is out of sight, (2) also keeps the cursor between the left and right edges (a card wider than the screen), (3) looks again after every item button (Tick, Delete, Undo, Move up / down, Indent, Outdent) and whenever the visual viewport resizes or scrolls, not only on focus and typing. The sum is one small pure function, `panToShow` in `src/model/view.ts` (unit-tested), used for all four edges; `caretBottom` became `caretLine` (top, bottom and the cursor's x). Notes, list and container titles and items all go through the same check. Nothing is saved or changed in boards; no saved board is affected. My calls (recommended option, per the owner rule): (a) **The held viewport settings H4, H5 and H8 of `docs/design-polish-review.md` are all left off**, because this task does not need them. **H5 `interactive-widget=resizes-content`**: the app already follows the keyboard through `visualViewport`, which works with and without the setting; with it on, Chrome on Android would shrink the layout and the phone bar would float up beside the item buttons, and iPhone Safari ignores it, so it would change what Android users see for no gain. **H4 `viewport-fit=cover`**: it is about the notch and the home-indicator area, not about keeping the cursor in view, and it changes what the owner sees on a notched phone. **H8** (paint under the status bar): the theme colour follows the dark-mode button by the 2026-10-16 decision, and it has nothing to do with the keyboard. All three stay held. (b) The board jumps to the new place at once, with no glide: a browser scrolls a focused box into view at once too, it would run on every few letters typed, and a keyboard-driven move is not animated (decision 2026-10-19, job 101). (c) A margin of 12px, as before, is kept on all four sides. (d) The bottom edge is the item buttons' top while they show, else the top of the visible screen above the keyboard, and never lower than the board area. Not checkable without a real phone: how iPhone Safari and Android Chrome really move the page when the keyboard opens (tested here with a stand-in visual viewport that shrinks and moves down). Reasoned, not seen on a phone: with motion allowed a tapped Tick closes the list up 280ms later, which only moves the items below it up into the ticked item's place (already in view), so the one look a frame after the tap is enough; the tests run with reduced motion, so they cannot see it, and it is in the owner's click-through. A page the person has pinched in (visual viewport scale above 1) is left alone, so the board does not fight a deliberate look around. A title box wider than the screen is kept by its left edge, not its cursor (a one-line box has no cursor position to measure). The mobile-native and emil-design-eng skills were applied: no glide for keyboard-driven moves, the viewport settings left alone, real-hardware check listed.
- 2026-10-19 (owner request, job 117: privacy-friendly usage counts; the owner signed up for GoatCounter, site code `busyants`). Built as the owner's snippet but added by a tiny loader in each page's `<head>` (a build-only Vite plugin, `scripts/goatcounter.ts`) so it runs only on the published address `ezhang43.github.io`: the browser tests serve the build on localhost, and the job said no request to gc.zgo.at there. Explicit `https://gc.zgo.at/count.js` source. Every HTML page in the build gets it (app, About, FAQ, Terms, Privacy, Delete your account). No Content-Security-Policy exists, so none was added. Calls made: (1) the Privacy page no longer says "no analytics"; it has a "Counting visits" section and links https://www.goatcounter.com because goatcounter.com's privacy-page address could not be checked from this build environment (the site was unreachable); the "no cookies, no personal tracking" wording is the owner's own description of GoatCounter, not checked against its docs here; (2) the About trust band line and the Play Store listing and Data safety notes that said "no analytics/tracking" were reworded to match, with an OWNER CHECK on whether Google's Data safety form needs an answer for the counter; (3) if the site moves to its own domain, the address in `scripts/goatcounter.ts` must change or counting silently stops. (4) Code review caught that count.js would send the address with its query string, and the app only removes a share link's `?join=<id>.<key>` after load; the loader now gives GoatCounter the page path only (`data-goatcounter-settings`), tested in a real browser, and it runs only under `ezhang43.github.io/note-board/`, so other projects on that address are not counted. The Privacy page says what is sent (page address without the query, IP address, browser details, the page you came from).
- 2026-10-19 (owner decision: "Spring back + row shift", replacing the 2026-10 "dragged block takes priority" rule that sent every block in the way to the nearest free spot). Dragging a block across a row scattered the others, some jumping above or below the row, and widening a card pushed its neighbour down below it. Now: (1) room is made only for where the dragged block is now, worked out from the drag's start, so blocks spring back to their own spots once it has passed; (2) blocks in the same row slide sideways and keep their left-to-right order; (3) resizing by hand does the same when it is let go; (4) no overlap (10px gap) still holds after release, and the live preview stays. My calls: "same row" means the two blocks overlap by at least half the shorter one's height; a block goes left if its middle is left of the dragged block's middle, otherwise right, and on a tie the way the dragged block came from (so dropping a block exactly onto its neighbour swaps them); shifted blocks push the row-mates beyond them along, and anything the shift still runs into moves to the nearest free spot; a single dragged card still never pushes a container, so containers in its row are left out of the shift; several blocks dragged together keep the old nearest-free-spot rule; resizing has no live preview of neighbours moving (as before), so shrinking a block back before letting go leaves them where they were. After the code review: after a resize every row-mate goes right (a block only grows to the right, so widening past a neighbour's middle never flips it to the left); a vertical-only drag's tie goes right; only row-mates level with the blocks being pushed are pushed along, so blocks stacked beside the dragged one move over together instead of spreading out; when a dragged card would cover a container (so it will take the nearest free spot itself), its row is not shifted; shifted blocks don't take priority over other blocks afterwards (a card's shift can't push a container).
- 2026-10-19 (owner request, job 109: "a new feature for habit tracking"), with the owner's choices: a side panel opened by a Habits button next to Journal (on a phone a ⋯ tile), reusing the Journal pattern; daily habits only, at most one tick a day; each habit shows this week's seven day boxes (tick or untick any of them) and its current streak, where today not being ticked yet doesn't break a streak that ran through yesterday; synced like boards and the Journal, working on the device when signed out; add, rename and a safe delete; reorder only if trivial. My calls (recommended options, rule 4): **Where it is kept:** a hidden board named "Habits", like the Journal board: each habit a checklist card (title = name), each ticked day a done item whose text is the day ("YYYY-MM-DD", local). So saving, sync, undo, Version history and backups work as for any board, `firestore.rules` and the save format are unchanged, and saved boards load as before. The board is made only when the first habit is added (opening the panel saves nothing). An existing board the person named exactly "Habits" is used as it is (the first one in Boards menu order; never one held by a share); it stays hidden only while it holds nothing but habits and no card opens it. **A habit never ticked** comes back after a reload with one blank item (the loader gives every empty checklist one, unchanged), so a blank, not-done item still counts as a habit and is not a tick. **The week** runs Monday to Sunday. Any of this week's days can be ticked, a day ahead too (the owner said "any of those days"), but a day ahead doesn't count toward the streak until it comes. The streak counts back past this week as far as the ticks run. **Delete** asks first (a confirm naming the habit) and can be undone with Ctrl+Z in the panel or from Version history. **Order:** the order habits were added; no reorder (not trivial to do well with the keyboard and touch, so left out). **Undo:** the Habits board has its own undo history, as the Journal does; Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y while the keyboard is in the panel act on it only, except in the New habit box, where they undo the typing (it isn't saved yet). Typing a new name is one undo step. The toolbar Undo button acts on the open board, as with the Journal. **Two devices** changing habits while offline: the person's own boards sync whole, so the last save wins (corrected after the check of PR #107); only on a board in a share are the two merged, and then a day ticked on both counts once; unticking removes every copy. **Clean up** on an opened Habits board would move the ticks into a Completed card, as for any checklist; the board is hidden, so this only happens if the person opens it on purpose. **"Today"** is read when the panel draws; left open past midnight it moves on at the next change (a `// simple:` note in the code). Code review (high) fixes: a day item unticked on the Habits board itself no longer counts as ticked; a new habit keeps the blank item every new checklist has, so it is the same before and after a reload; the panel and its actions find the Habits board afresh each time instead of keeping the one found when the panel opened, so one that arrives from another device while it is open is used, and a board that becomes shared is never written to; Ctrl+Z in the New habit box undoes the typing; the delete question names Version history first (on a phone there is no Ctrl+Z); an unused colour transition was removed. Left alone: Clean up on an opened Habits board moves the ticks into a Completed card (undoable, and the board is hidden unless opened on purpose); the list follows the board's order, which only changes if the board is opened and its cards moved (SPEC says so); new habits on an open Habits board are placed with guessed heights (a browser test with four habits found no overlap); the Journal and Habits helpers stay as two small copies rather than a shared layer.
- 2026-10-19 (owner decision, job 109, check of PR #107): restoring a Version history version rolls the Habits board back to how it was then, the same way as the Journal (already so; a unit test checks it); a version without a Habits board leaves it as it is. Fixes from the check: on a phone or touch screen the New habit box no longer takes the cursor when the panel opens (the keyboard would cover the day boxes); each day box is named with the habit and the day for screen readers ("Read, Mon 19 Oct"); ticking a day that was unticked on the Habits board itself ticks that same item instead of adding a second; and the note on two devices offline is corrected: own boards sync whole, so the last save wins.

- 2026-10-19 (job 110, owner request: the Claude connector can create and safely delete; see SPEC.md, Claude connector). New tools `add_checklist`, `add_column`, `create_board`, `preview_delete` and `confirm_delete`. Calls made while building (recommended options): (1) the "Let Claude delete" setting is a `settings.json` file beside the connector's saved sign-in, on that computer only, off by default (missing or damaged reads as off), switched only by the owner with `npm run mcp:delete-on` / `mcp:delete-off`, and read again on every delete call; it is not in the boards and not synced, so saving and sync are unchanged; (2) no `add_link` tool, following the 2026-10-05 decision that there is no Add Link anywhere; (3) previews are kept in the connector's memory only, used once within 10 minutes, and forgotten on a restart (a confirm refused because deleting is off or the board can't be read leaves the preview for a retry); confirm deletes only if everything it would remove is exactly as it was at the preview (compared in full, not just the listed lines, so an edited second line of a note or a column's swapped card stops it), while changes elsewhere, such as renaming the board, don't; (4) "at most 20 things" means 20 ids per call: a column or a board inside counts as one, with what is inside it; (5) "top-level board" means the home board or a board no card opens (`pathTo` of length 1); these are never deleted; (6) "container" is called "column" in the connector, as its other tools already do; (7) as the owner's request asked ("a backup version before every delete"), the backup version before a delete is always saved (it ignores the 10-minute version rule) unless the newest version already holds exactly these boards, and is saved again on each retry when the app saved in between; a delete the app saves back over is reported, never made again; (8) deletes use the app's own functions (`deleteItems`, `deleteBlocks`, `withoutBoard`), so a column's Completed card stays loose, a board's inner boards stand alone, and an emptied checklist keeps one blank item. `firestore.rules` are unchanged.

- 2026-10-19 (owner choice, job #108 follow-up; Eric chose to fix three medium findings of the /check-pr of PR #104 before merging). (1) While a delete is on its way to Google, clicks elsewhere in the calendar panel (another event, another week, the panel's ×) wait, so the card stays and Google's refusal is still shown; the board itself and clicks inside the card are not blocked. (2) My call (recommended option; the spec was silent on this case): when there is no room for the card left of the panel (a narrow window with a wide panel), it sits inside the panel, 8px from the window's right edge and never left of the panel's edge plus 8px (it narrows to the panel's width minus 16px when needed), instead of being pushed over the panel's edge, where the panel's resize strip covered part of it (`cardPosition` in `src/model/eventCard.ts`). The card stays inside the panel's layer (no portal), so the find bar and other layers keep their order. (3) The card is placed again whenever the panel's width or the window's size changes, so it follows the panel's edge (its height stays where the event was when it opened); pressing on the resize strip no longer closes the card, so it can follow a drag. Focus after a delete (my call, matches the code): the event's button is gone, so when the keyboard is left on nothing it goes to the calendar panel itself (Tab then continues inside the panel); after × or Escape it goes back to the event's button. A refused delete leaves the card open with its note; the keyboard is not moved (Escape and Delete still act on the card from anywhere).

- 2026-10-19 (job 119, review finding on PR #112, owner chose to fix it first). The finding: combining two edits of one note in sync (`mergeBoards`) picks the note's text and its formatting separately, so it could pair one side's words with the other side's formatting, which then shows plain and is saved plain by the next keystroke. Fixed there: when the two sides' words differ, the formatting comes from the side whose words are kept (none if that side has none), so both devices end on the same note; when the words are the same, the formatting is merged as before. Formatting made on words the other side changed at the same time is dropped (re-fitting it needs a three-way merge of the formatting). Found while fixing: the Journal panel only edits the person's own boards, which sync whole with the latest save winning (`sync.ts`), so a Journal note's words and formatting already travel together and the merge only runs for shared boards. Two of the person's own devices editing at once lose the earlier save, words and formatting together, as on every board; changing that is a change to how sync works, left to the owner. No change to the saved format or the online rules.

- 2026-10-19 (owner request, job 124: "add the functionality of users being able to add in images, videos, urls by dragging them in, or by copy pasting them in? no need to specifically have a new item for it like checklist or container"). Owner chose option B on 2026-10-19: online media only, nothing uploaded, no new block type. A pasted or dropped web address becomes an ordinary link card; the card shows a picture, video or YouTube / Vimeo player from its address, decided when it is drawn, so no field is added to saved data and nothing about saving, sync or undo changes. Only http(s) addresses are ever used as a source. A file dropped or pasted from the computer only shows a note. Calls made while building (recommended options): (1) a click on a video or player on a card that is not selected selects the card (a clear cover over it), and the player works once the card is selected; (2) pictures use no referrer and YouTube players use youtube-nocookie.com in a sandbox that allows scripts, same-origin and presentation only; (3) the newest copy wins (changed 2026-10-19, fixing a stale clipboard found in review of PR #121): copying blocks in the app also leaves a marker (`application/x-note-board-blocks`) on the computer's clipboard, written by the browser's own copy; Ctrl+V is now left to the browser's paste event and pastes the blocks only while that marker is still there, so a web address copied afterwards in another tab or app becomes a link card, and blocks copied after an address paste as blocks. First version (dropped): blocks always won until the page was reloaded. If the browser refuses to write the marker, Ctrl+V pastes the copied blocks as before. Known limit: a Paste key the person re-mapped in the shortcuts panel can't see the clipboard, so it always pastes the copied blocks (Ctrl+V still handles addresses); copied checklist items still win while items are selected; (4) because the card decides from its address, a link card already on a board (shared, imported or older) with a picture or video address also loads it now, which the privacy page says (the owner can ask for a click-to-load step instead); (5) pasting with a container selected puts the card in it, as Add does, while a drop goes where the pointer is, or to the end of the container it lands on; a paste does nothing while the Journal or Habits panel is open or an old version is shown. See SPEC.md, Pictures, videos and web addresses; the privacy page says the website holding a picture or video can see it was viewed.
- 2026-10-19 (job 111, owner request: the Claude connector installable by others in one step; see SPEC.md, Claude connector). The connector is packed as an MCP Bundle (`.mcpb`, the current Claude Desktop one-file format; MANIFEST.md 0.3 in github.com/modelcontextprotocol/mcpb, and claude.com/docs/connectors/building/mcpb), and two tools, `sign_in` and `sign_out`, let a person sign in from inside Claude. Calls made while building (recommended options): (1) `sign_in` reuses the existing sign-in page (`mcp/login/`), opens it in the default browser and answers at once with its address instead of waiting up to 5 minutes, since Claude stops waiting for a tool long before; a new `sign_in` closes the previous page; (2) `sign_out` is a tool too, because people who install the bundle have no `npm run mcp:logout`; neither tool changes boards, so both are marked not read-only and not destructive; (3) no new API key: the app's web key is not restricted to the site's address (checked: Google's token service answers it from outside the site), so everyone signs in with it; a sign-in made with `--key` keeps using that key; (4) the bundle asks nothing at install (no `user_config`): there is no key to type, and deleting stays off for bundle installs, switched only from the project's code as before (whether to add an install-time "Let Claude delete" switch is left to the owner); (5) the manifest names the site's existing privacy page (the MCPB spec asks for one when a connector reaches an online service with people's data); (6) the `.mcpb` is built by `npm run mcp:pack` into `mcp/dist/` (not in the repository) and shared as a GitHub Release file, not on the website; (7) the built server is an ES module, so the bundle carries a one-line `server/package.json` saying so; (8) `mcp/README.md` is rewritten for anyone, Mac and Windows, with no one's own folders or email (a unit test checks it and the manifest); the stale "invite list" line is gone, since any Google account may use its own boards. `firestore.rules`, saving and the token file's place are unchanged.

- 2026-10-19 (job 111 follow-up, owner's answers to four questions about the connector's one-file install, PR #116; all the recommended answers; built as job 123). (1) Deleting stays switched off in the installed file for now. (2) The `.mcpb` file is shared from a GitHub Release. (3) The Privacy policy gets one short section about the connector: "The Claude connector (optional)", saying it is optional and runs on the person's own computer, signs in with their own Google account and keeps that sign-in in a file on that computer, reads or changes their boards only when they ask Claude to, has deleting switched off, sends what Claude reads to Claude under Anthropic's privacy policy (linked), and that BusyAnts stores nothing new; Anthropic is also listed under "Where it is kept, and who helps run it". Written from `mcp/` (checked: it talks only to Google's sign-in and Firestore, writes only the person's own boards and version history, and keeps its sign-in, `activity.log` and `settings.json` on the computer). My calls (recommended): the policy's "Effective from" date stays October 19, 2026, since that is today's date and it already carried it; the wording says deleting is off "in the connector you install", since it can be switched on from a copy of the project's code. (4) Claude Code is blocked from reading the Mac and Linux sign-in folder `~/.config/busyants-mcp` (deny rule in `.claude/settings.json`, as the Windows folder already was).

- 2026-10-19 (job 126, owner choice "search 60"). Owner 2026-10-19: Find zooms in only to 60%, not 100% ("it zooms in a bit too much"). When the board is zoomed out below 60%, moving to a match zooms in to exactly 60%, around the match; at 60% or above the zoom is unchanged, as before. The 60% is `FIND_MIN_ZOOM` in `src/model/constants.ts`; the rule is `zoomForFind` in `src/model/view.ts`. View only: nothing saved.

- 2026-10-19 (owner choices, job 120: collapse the Journal's entries). (1) The Journal panel's days are grouped Year > Month > Week, each group a button with `aria-expanded` that opens and shuts; a week spanning two months is split, each month listing its own days. (2) On opening, the current year, month and week are open and today is visible; older groups are shut. (3) What is open or shut is remembered on this device only (`localStorage` key `note-board:journal-open`, not in the board data: not synced, not undoable, not in versions or backups); bad saved data falls back to the defaults. (4) The Journal panel is as wide as the Calendar panel (the same `PANEL_DEFAULT.week` value, 420px, not a second number). Calls made while building (recommended options): weeks run Monday to Sunday, as the calendar already does (`weekStart`); only choices that differ from the default are stored, so groups that were never touched follow the date as it moves on; opening the panel opens today's groups again even if they were shut earlier (so today's note is always there for the cursor); the Journal does not follow the person's own dragged Calendar width, because it has no drag handle; groups are in plain date order, so a day written ahead sits in its own group above today's (before, it was listed just below today's note), while today's note still gets the cursor. Only the Journal panel, styles, the width line in `App.tsx` and pure functions in `src/model/journal.ts` changed: no saving, sync, undo or deleting file.

- 2026-10-20 (job 120 follow-up, owner chose "journal fix"). Before the Journal was grouped, today's note was listed first, above days written ahead. Grouping by plain date order had put a later date's year, month and week above today's. Decision: today's year, today's month within its year, today's week within its month and today's day within its week stay first; every other group and day keeps newest-first order (so days written ahead come right after today's groups). Done in `groupJournal` by passing today's day and sorting on it; no saving, sync, undo or deleting file changed, and saved boards are untouched.

- 2026-10-20 (owner request, job #127: "the side panel for habits, journal, etc. should be resizable by the user"; calls made by the coordinator under rule 4's exception). Every side panel (Version history, Completed, Focus timer, Journal, Habits) is resizable exactly like the calendar's: same strip, limits, 20px arrow steps, double-click reset and cursor; the strip's name is "Resize <panel name in lower case>" (the calendar's stays "Resize calendar"). Each panel remembers its own width on this device, in a new key `note-board:panel-widths` (the calendar's `note-board:calendar-width` and its saved values are unchanged); not board data, not undone, not synced, kept when an account is deleted (a device setting). Usual widths stay 300px, Journal 420px. A phone stays full screen with no strip. Code: one `createWidthStore` (`src/store/panelWidths.ts`) used by the calendar and the other panels, one `PanelResizeHandle` component, `.panel-resize` in the CSS (was `.cal-resize`).

- 2026-10-20 (owner request, job #128: "habit should also have a monthly and annual view" and "no need for habit to have a streak counter"; the owner picked mockup option A of three, B being one calendar with a habit picker and mini-month year blocks, C a per-card switch with year bars). The Habits panel gets a Week | Month | Year switch with ‹ › arrows and a Today button in a bar that stays at the top; Month is one tickable calendar per habit (two side by side from about 440px wide), Year is twelve rows of tiny squares per habit, view only, where clicking a month name opens that month. The streak is removed everywhere (panel, code, tests, SPEC). Calls made while building (recommended options, rule 4): (1) weeks start Monday, as before; (2) in Month any day, days ahead too, can be ticked, as in Week, through the same store action (one undoable change each); (3) Month day boxes are 32px tall on a computer and 44px on a phone (Week keeps 44px / 52px); (4) the chosen view and place are view state in the panel, not remembered: the panel opens on this week each time (remembering the view on this device was left out, as it is not trivial next to the panel-width store); (5) one switch and one place for every habit; (6) the year grid's squares have no individual labels for screen readers (they are `aria-hidden`); the month-name buttons carry the count ("October 2026, 10 ticked") instead; (7) no new saved data, no change to how ticks are stored, saved or synced, so boards already saved load and look the same, only the panel changed. Code: `moveHabitView`, `habitViewLabel`, `isHabitViewNow`, `habitYear` in `src/model/habits.ts` (the unused `thisWeek` and `streak` are deleted); the calendar's `monthGrid`, `addMonths` and `weekStart` are reused.

- 2026-10-21 (owner request, job #129: "in the habit tracker, I'd like to include a weight tracker as well"; the owner chose kg with one decimal, and a "Weight" card at the top of the Habits panel with a box for today's weight and a small line chart following the panel's Week / Month / Year view and ‹ › period, one entry per day, entering again replacing it). Calls made while building (recommended options, rule 4): (1) **Stored** on the same hidden Habits board, so saving, sync, undo, Version history and backups work as for habits: one checklist card titled "Weight" whose items are "YYYY-MM-DD 72.4" (local date), not-done, after the usual blank item; the save format, `firestore.rules` and sync are unchanged. (2) **Not a habit:** habits only accept day-only items, so the card is never in the habit list; a habit the person named "Weight" stays a habit. The "holds only habits" rule for hiding the Habits board now also allows this one Weight card (and nothing else, so a Weight card with a stray item keeps the board listed). (3) The card (and the Habits board, if none) is made only when the first weight is entered; removing the last weight deletes the card, since a Weight card with no weights would read as a habit named Weight. Both go through new store actions in `src/store/actions/weight.ts` (`saveWeight`, `removeWeight`), one undoable change each on the Habits board's own undo; the first save, as with the first habit, also makes the board without an undo step of its own. (4) **Input:** 20.0 to 400.0 kg, comma or dot, rounded to one decimal; anything else shows "Enter a weight from 20.0 to 400.0 kg." and saves nothing, keeping the text; a blank does nothing. No goal weight, BMI or pounds. Only today can be entered (there is no date picker, so a missed day can't be added later). (5) **Chart:** plain inline SVG, no new package; a dot for each day with an entry joined by a line, lowest and highest weight and the period's first and last day written on it; "No weight entered in this week/month/year." when empty. (6) **Delete:** a × beside "Latest: ..." (the period's last entry) removes it with no question, as it is one Ctrl+Z from coming back (ticking does not ask either); simplest version, so only the latest entry of the shown period can be removed, one at a time. (7) **Look:** under the New habit box, above the habits, in every view; on a phone the box is 44px tall with 16px text and Save and × are 44px. (8) A malformed or out-of-range item on the card is ignored, never a crash; a day found twice counts as its last item. (9) Ctrl+Z in the weight box just after Save (the box is empty) takes the saved weight back; while it holds text it undoes the typing. (10) Two Weight cards on one Habits board (two devices) keep the board listed in the Boards menu, because the panel reads only the first. (11) Risk: `node scripts/claude-hooks.mjs level` says no risky file changed (the new actions are in `src/store/actions/weight.ts`, not on the risky list, and `boards.ts` is untouched), but the code saves to and deletes from the owner's boards, so the pull request is labelled `high-risk` and left for the owner, as for `high` (code review ran at `high`). Whether `weight.ts` belongs on the risky list in `scripts/publish-rules.mjs` is left to the owner.

- 2026-10-21 (owner request, job #136: "for journal, please also add the functionality so that daily journal notes can be collapsed as well"; calls made by the coordinator under rule 4's exception). Each day's note in the Journal panel folds like the Year > Month > Week groups: its date line is a header button (same arrow, `aria-expanded`, keyboard behavior and look). - 2026-10-21 (owner request, job #135: "cards should be resizable from all 4 sides instead of only the bottom right"). Owner's picks, asked first: (1) loose cards, open or collapsed, resize from all 4 edges and all 4 corners; containers from their left and right edges plus all 4 corners, which set the width (an empty container's corners also set its minimum height as before; one with cards stays exactly as tall as its cards, so it has no top or bottom strip); cards inside a container still cannot be resized. (2) Resize cursors (left-right, up-down, diagonal) show only within the edge and corner grab areas, everywhere else on blocks stays the normal arrow: an owner exception to the cursor rule (CLAUDE.md rule 6; the wording changed in SPEC.md only), like the calendar panel's resize strip. (3) Making room mirrors the right edge: on release, dragging the left edge out slides blocks in its row that end at or left of it left, keeping their order; dragging the top edge up moves the blocks above it straight up (and those above them); nothing moves while still dragging; the left and top edges also move the block's x / y so the opposite edge stays put. Calls made while building (recommended options, rule 4 exception): grab areas are invisible 6px strips along each edge and 12x12px squares at each corner, inside the outline, stopping short of the bottom-right handle and clear of the item grips, trash can and tick boxes (checked by tests); they are pointer-only (the existing handles keep the arrow keys), drawn as hidden buttons (so a touch screen with a mouse treats them like the other handles); same size limits, snapping, size matching and one undo step per drag; a card's top edge stops at its content height (so its bottom edge stays put); on touch screens (pointer: coarse) the new grab areas are switched off and only the older handles remain, because a 6px strip is too thin for a finger and, being buttons, they stole presses meant for the board and for the buttons' 44px touch areas (the owner may want a different touch way to resize from the top and left later); where a header button and a grab area meet on a computer, the button wins. Saved boards are unchanged: positions and sizes are stored as before.
- 2026-10-21 (job #142, fixes to job #135's resize from all sides, from a code review; no owner question). (1) When a resize is let go, "in its row" and "above it" are judged from where the block was before the drag (kept by the store while the resize is shown), not from the grown rectangle: a block that started level with or below the old top edge never moves up, so a corner drag from the top only slides a neighbor beside it sideways, and a block under it is not a row mate. (2) Only an edge that actually moved outward slides the row: the right edge (e, ne, se) to the right, the left edge (w, nw, sw) to the left; dragging only the top or bottom edge slides nothing sideways, and growth is pushed straight down by the usual settle. (3) Size matching only accepts another block's size when it is inside the limits this block may have (cards 200-1280 wide, containers 240-1280, heights 100-700, a card's top edge no shorter than its content), so a match can no longer break a limit. Calls made while building (recommended options): "above" means starting higher than the old top edge (a block that only partly sticks up beside it counts as above); no data change, saved boards are unchanged.
- 2026-10-21 (job #145, finding H2 of review 137-A; owner said "go all", which covers changing the SPEC sentence "if there is none, this device's board is uploaded"; high-risk pull request, waiting for the owner). On a shared computer a brand-new account was given the previous person's boards, their private Journal included: Sign out leaves the boards in `note-board:v1`, and with no online copy yet sync uploaded "this device's board". The same happened to another person's shared board left on the device when the new account's share list was slow (`boardsInKeptShares` counted every kept share for someone with no list). Coordinator's call: the device remembers which account its boards belong to (`note-board:owner-uid`, written when sync starts, by `claimDevice` in `src/store/account.ts`, called first thing in `start` in `src/sync/session.ts`). When a different account signs in, the earlier account's boards are kept aside on the device (`note-board:parked:{uid}`, the saved text itself) with the last agreed version of each share on its list (`note-board:parked-bases:{uid}`), and never shown or uploaded; the new account opens on its own kept-aside boards (their shares' agreed versions put back too), or an empty board, then its online copy as usual; undo starts over, and an old version being looked at is put away. The earlier account gets its kept-aside boards back when it signs in here again, exactly as after a reload (its online copy wins when it arrives; saves Firestore still holds go out, since Firestore keeps queued writes per user). Boards with no owner kept yet (saved before this change) stay with the first account to sign in, which is then remembered. My calls (recommended option, owner rule): (1) keep the earlier account's boards aside rather than delete them on sign-out, as the coordinator asked ("if in doubt keep it"); (2) the steps go in an order that never leaves the device's boards under the wrong owner: keep aside, record the new owner, swap the boards; if the new boards can't be saved, the device's board copy is removed (the earlier account's stays kept aside); (3) if the earlier account's boards can't be kept aside (storage full), the owner can't be read (storage blocked), or the boards kept aside for the account signing in can't be read (saved by a newer version), nothing on the device changes and the board doesn't open, with the usual "Your board couldn't be loaded" message, rather than risk showing, emptying or losing them; on a device with no owner kept and full storage, the first account still signs in (the owner is just not remembered); (4) each open page remembers whose boards it holds, so a second tab still holding the earlier account's boards opens the new account's instead of uploading the earlier ones; (5) the shares' agreed versions go aside with the boards because they are one per device: without them, an account's restored copy of a board shared with both accounts would be combined against the other account's newer agreed version, and its old content sent as if just edited; (6) a share kept on the device that is on another account's list no longer counts as possibly the new account's (only for someone with no list of their own here; shares on no one's list, kept by much older versions, still count); (7) deleting an account clears its kept-aside boards and other device data but keeps the owner key, so whatever is left on the device (another tab writing back, say) is never taken for the next account's. Unit tests only, as for job 92: own-board sync runs only on the published site with a real Google sign-in, which browser tests can't do. Left as is (after the high code review): kept-aside boards stay on the device until that account signs in here again or is deleted here, so several accounts with very large boards could fill the browser's storage, after which switching accounts says "Your board couldn't be loaded" (the earlier account's unsent work is never dropped to make room); edits whose upload was refused are lost when the online copy arrives, as on any reload; the open board and pan/zoom kept on the device aren't per account (the open board falls back to home).
- 2026-10-21 (job #147, review 137 finding H3, step 1 only; owner: "go all"). When a change from another device replaces the person's own boards (the whole own-boards copy, as an old copy a phone saved offline and sent hours later does) and this page has had edits since it last did this, every board as it was just before is saved as a version marked "Before changes from another device". Step 2 (refusing a save based on an older copy, in the Firestore rules) is not done: it waits for its own owner decision. Calls made while building (recommended options): (1) at most once every 10 minutes since the last such version (ordinary versions don't count, as they hold the boards from before the edits), the same gap as ordinary versions, so two devices used at once don't fill the 100-version history and push out older versions (work done within 10 minutes after such a version can be lost to a second one); (2) only boards that were already here count, and not shared ones: a share's boards arriving, or someone else changing a shared board, doesn't save one (shared boards sync on their own); (3) the save starts straight away, so nothing (such as deleting a demo account, which then clears it with everything else) can clear the device first; restoring a backup of every board doesn't count (it saves its own safety version just before); if it fails (offline, or over the 900,000-character online limit) the incoming change still goes ahead and the failure is only logged in the console, not retried; (4) asked of the owner in the pull request, as it needs a Firebase console change (rule 4): the mark is a new optional `kind` field on a version's list entry, which the rules allow only as `'outside'` on `boards/{uid}/versions/{id}`; until the owner pastes the new `firestore.rules` into the Firebase console these versions are refused online (nothing else changes; the recommended order is to paste the rules first, then publish); (5) only a page that is open when the change arrives can keep the boards: one opened afterwards only sees the new copy (that needs step 2).

- 2026-10-22 (owner picks, jobs #166 and #167: "Habits feels very AI-generated and too basic ... not polished and structured"; "the Journal doesn't have that refinement and polish"). One designer did both panels with the same design language; the owner picked **Habits mockup A, the Ledger**, and **Journal mockup A, page and timeline**, and said the "3 of 7 this week" counts and the per-habit colors from the 8 bold swatches were fine; the weight card stays above the habits. Habits: the weekdays are written once above one white sheet, today's column has a soft accent band, each habit is a row with its count, a ⋯ menu (Rename, Color, Delete habit) and seven ticks in the habit's color; the New habit box is the sheet's last row; a line under the title says how today is going; month calendars use filled circles; the Weight card has a big latest number, its change and a chart with room around the line. Journal: today is a page with its date as the heading, "Week 43", a word count and "Saved"; the earlier days are a timeline of rows with a big date and two-line previews, opening in place; the four drop-down boxes are one Aa menu of labeled buttons, with a highlighter button beside it. Everything the owner had decided stays: Month option A, no streak counter, weight in kg above the habits, Year > Month > Week folding and day folding (remembered only on this device), rich text, panel widths (Habits 300px, Journal 420px). Calls made while building (recommended options, rule 4 exception), all listed in SPEC.md's Habits and Daily journal sections: (1) **Earlier days start folded** (their two-line preview is the folded day) and today's page starts open; before, every day opened. A day's fold is still view state, never saved. (2) **This year has no header** (a year other than this one keeps its header); today's note is on its page, not in the groups, so shutting its month or week doesn't hide it. (3) The Weight box stays visible in the card (the mockup's "Log today" button would have hidden it and changed how Ctrl+Z right after Save works), and the chart is always shown (the mockup showed it only in a wide panel). (4) The ⋯ menu's Delete keeps the browser's confirmation, as the owner's earlier decision (2026-10-19) has it. (5) A habit's color is its card's `titleColor` (already saved data), set by one new store action that follows the name's save, sync and undo path; only the 8 bold swatches (and none, the default green) are accepted, anything else counts as none. (6) New wording, for the owner to see: "Week 43", "48 words", "✓ Saved", "Write about today…", "All 6 done", "Add a habit", "Wednesday · 2 of 6 done", "3 of 7 this week", "18 of 21 days", "233 days", "−0.4 kg this month". "✓ Saved" is shown by the panel's own timing (the app's save delay and a moment after), because the store does not report a failed write (storage full or blocked is ignored, as before); making it report one would change the saving code, so it is left for the owner to decide. (7) The weekdays stay under the view bar while the habits scroll; the Journal's month headers are not sticky (a shortcut: the Formatting bar's height varies, most of all when it wraps on a phone). (8) The phone note text stays 16px (the mockup showed 17px) so the spec's rule and the page not zooming still hold. (9) The panels' default widths are unchanged; the Ledger lines names up beside the ticks from about 400px, so a default of 380px or more would show it that way without dragging (not done: the owner's call).
- 2026-10-22 (owner: "do the UX fixes"; job 165a, the fixes that don't change how screens look; calls made by the worker under rule 4's exception). (1) Keys in a side panel: every side panel is treated like the Journal, so a button, tick box or heading inside it never passes Delete, Backspace, Ctrl+A, Ctrl+D, the tool keys, arrows or Escape-clears-selection to the board behind it; undo, redo, zoom and Escape-closes-panel still work. Reason: with a note selected, Delete on the Focus timer's Reset button or Backspace on a Completed tick box deleted the note unseen. (2) Screen readers: tick boxes are named "Done: <item text>"; one persistent polite live region (data-testid "announcer") announces deletes, undo/redo, items moving to Completed, and a failed save online. It is the store that says things (`src/store/announce.ts`), so every way of deleting is covered. The existing save-failed banner keeps its own `role="status"` for now (it lives in `src/sync/`, a risky path), so a screen reader may hear that one twice. (3) Sign-in screen speed: the board's code (`App`) loads separately from the sign-in screen (`src/mainPublished.tsx` and `src/PublishedApp.tsx`; `npm run dev` and the tests keep starting from `src/main.tsx`, so they still draw the board before the page finishes loading, and `vite.config.ts` swaps the entry in index.html for the published build), and index.html asks only for IBM Plex Sans 400/500/600; the italics and the other typefaces are added by `src/appFonts.ts` when the board loads. The old `src/sync/SyncedApp.tsx` is no longer used; deleting it and splitting Firebase's database code (about 380 KB, loaded to draw one button) out of sign-in both touch `src/sync/`, so they wait for a separate high-risk pull request.

- 2026-10-22 (owner yes; job #164, the website messaging). New headline "Free, with no limits. Your notes stay safe."; the four points are the "Try it: tick one off" list; new sections Your boards are safe, Works well on a phone and No AI pushed on you replace "Everything on one board"; the FAQ gains "Does BusyAnts use AI?" and the Privacy connector section gets the anchor `privacy/#connector`. Calls made (rule 4 exception): (a) the comparison table now uses only what job 158 confirmed against each app's own pages: Notion's "1,000 blocks when shared" became "Limited once shared" (the number could not be confirmed), Milanote's cap reads "100 notes, images or links", Miro's "free links are view-only" and the Milanote "No" and Trello "phone app only" on offline were not confirmed, so Miro and Trello share-and-edit read Yes and the two offline cells read a dash; (b) SPEC.md's "no analytics or tracking" in the trust band description was wrong (GoatCounter counts visits, as the About page and Privacy policy say) and now matches; (c) the Quick answers swapped "Does it work offline?" and "How does sharing work?" for "How do I get a board back?" and "Does BusyAnts use AI?"; (d) the "How it works" steps stay, moved after the new sections. Nothing links to a connector download, because none is on the website yet.
- 2026-10-21 (job #146, review 137-A findings H4 and M2; owner: "go all"). H4: on a new device a share's data could hold a board with the id of one of the person's own boards and take it, since the clash check only looked at boards already on the device. Fix: the first own online copy each page load reads is handed to sharing (`ownCopy` in `src/store/sharing.ts`, called by `ownBoardsGuard`), which drops any board with one of those ids from every share's agreed data on this device; a share whose starting board clashes holds nothing there. Calls made (recommended options, rule 4): (1) the only exception is the person who shared it: the board they shared and the boards inside it, as their online copy shows them (a copy saved just before sharing), so the existing "the share takes over that device's old copy" rule still works; (2) it runs at every page load, not only on new devices, and a share's boards kept on the device don't count as allowed, so a board taken this way before (or by a page closed before the own boards arrived) is given back; the cost: a board someone else shared that somehow also sits in the person's own online copy (only through an old bug) stops being shared on that device and is theirs there; (3) the share's data online is left as it is (the other people keep their board). M2: a board in another share's agreed data, or in its boards last seen here (a sub-board just made, not yet online), stays with that share; it is never followed through a card pasted on another shared board. A board cut from one shared board and pasted on another stays in the first (its card opens nothing for the second's people), since moving boards between shares isn't in the spec. Left for a later job: the connector's twin (review F9, `mcp/server.ts` `sharedIds` counts every board id in every share as read-only, so a share holding `home` makes the owner's Home read-only to the connector); it is separate code and only blocks the connector, and the suggested rule (count an own id as shared only for the person's own shares) needs its own check.
- 2026-10-22 (job #148, review 137-A finding H1; owner: "go all"). Opening the app from an older offline copy, the automatic tidy-up after measuring (a block drawn taller here: another A−/A+ text size, a late font) counted as "a change made here that is newer", so the stale copy was uploaded over another device's newer edits. Calls made while building (recommended options, the reviewer's sketch): (1) sync keeps track of whether the person changed their own boards on this page since they were last sent or received; only then does a waiting upload win over an arriving version. Tidying (counted by the store as `tidies`), versions sync itself shows, edits to shared boards, changes to which boards are shared and a board leaving because its share ended don't count; restoring a backup, deleting or adding a board, undo and every other action do. A tidy-only upload that is waiting when a version arrives is dropped and the version shown. The same rule now decides whether a version that waited for a gesture to end is shown (it is also shown when the person's edits during the gesture left their boards as they were). (2) Firestore's offline copy is marked (`cached` on the version sync receives, from `fromCache`); until the server's copy (or word that there is none) has come, tidying alone is not uploaded. If the server's copy is the one the page opened from, the tidying is uploaded then; if it is newer, it is shown instead. The person's edits are uploaded as before. A board just shared on a page opened from the offline copy leaves the person's own online copy with their next edit rather than straight away, if a newer server copy arrives first (it stays readable only by them, as before). (3) Fragile-store F1, the same root cause: a tidy-up queued just before another board opens, or just before boards arrive from another device (the whole copy), is dropped instead of re-arranging the new board with heights from before it. A shared board changed by someone else (`replaceBoards`) still runs it, so a block that just grew here isn't left covering the one below. Left for the owner: fragile-store F2 (on first draw the block measured last keeps its place, so a block taller here moves up instead of the one below moving down) changes how layout behaves.

- 2026-10-22 (owner pick from market research: "Export picture / PDF: save a board as an image or PDF to send or print"; job #163; calls made by the coordinator and worker under rule 4's exception). (1) Two new File-menu rows under "Download as text": "Download as picture (PNG)" and "Download as PDF", on a phone in the File menu under ⋯ like the other downloads. (2) They save the whole content area of the open board (every card and container, with a 40px margin), as it looks now including theme and dark mode, at 2x (lower for a huge board: about 16 million pixels and 16,000 on a side is the most a phone browser draws), without the toolbar, panels, selection, hover buttons, resize handles or the dot grid. (3) Names like the backup's ("BusyAnts - Home - 2026-10-22.png" / ".pdf"). (4) The PDF is always one page the size of the board holding the picture (a JPEG at quality 0.92), not A4 pages: simplest that works, and nothing to cut across. Its text isn't selectable; real text in the PDF would need a PDF-drawing package and a second layout of the board. (5) New package html-to-image 1.11.13 (MIT, no dependencies, about 12 KB), loaded only when a picture is asked for; the browser has no way to draw HTML to a picture by itself. The PDF file is written by a small function, not by adding jsPDF. (6) A picture from another site that can't be read back (no CORS header) is drawn as a light blank panel; a video or web page as a blank 16:9 panel; neither stops the download. (7) The board is copied into a hidden, see-through copy (never the live board, so nothing flickers and nothing about the open board changes), and that copy is drawn. (8) "Preparing…" shows in the notice banner meanwhile. An empty board says so instead of saving a blank picture. (9) Nothing saved or synced changes; no saved board is touched.

- 2026-10-22 (owner pick, option A, from the UX-fix mockups; job #165b, change 4 of 3 PRs; calls made by the worker under rule 4's exception). The Share panel on the home board offers one "Make a board to share" button instead of only telling the person what to do: it adds a sub-board with a card on home (the same action as "Add a sub-board here") and opens it, and the panel stays open and shows "Share this board". - 2026-10-21 (owner request, job #171: "every pull request adds an entry at the top of CHANGELOG.md, so as soon as one merges every other open PR shows merge conflicts"). Calls made (recommended options, rule 4 exception): (1) each pull request writes `changes/<branch>.md` (changelog entry) and, if it made calls, `changes/<branch>.decisions.md`, instead of editing `CHANGELOG.md` / `docs/decisions.md`; appending decisions at the end alone would not help, since two pull requests that both add lines at the end of a file conflict on GitHub too. (2) `node scripts/fold-changes.mjs` folds them: entries to the top of the changelog under its heading, decisions to the end of `docs/decisions.md`, fragments deleted, `changes/README.md` kept. It is run by the crew coordinator at publish time and committed on `build/v1` (added to the crew skill's publish step), not by the Publish job in `checks.yml`: that job would have to commit to `build/v1` and `main` with its own token, and a commit it pushed would not start the checks again. (3) Auto-publish alone leaves fragments waiting in `changes/` until the next manual publish; nothing reads `CHANGELOG.md` at run time, so the live site is unaffected. (4) The `merge=union` lines in `.gitattributes` stay for now, for pull requests opened before this one that still edit the two files; remove them (and `scripts/gitattributes.test.ts`) once none are open. CLAUDE.md rule 5 and the finish-step, crew, spec-check and spec-checker instructions were reworded to match.

- 2026-10-22 (owner picked option B, job 149, review 137-A H5): deleting an account first adds a marker to boards/{uid} (`deleting: 'started'`, the deleting page's `client`, `updatedAt`; the boards stay in it), and firestore.rules refuse board, version and shared-list saves under boards/{uid} while there is a marker; deletes still work. Order: mark, list the shares (after marking, so none can be added meanwhile), delete shares and records, replace the board by `{deleting: 'done', client, updatedAt}`, then version history and the list of shares, then the sign-in. My calls: while 'started', only the page that set it may change it; if a step before 'done' fails, that page takes the mark off (keeping the boards in it) without waiting, so its pages carry on and a shared board already gone is kept in Version history; that alone isn't "part-way". A 'started' marker over 10 minutes old (the page closed part-way) lets any page save again; pages wait 10½ minutes from when the server's copy of it arrived (not from its time, so a device clock that is off doesn't matter; a marker only in the offline copy never starts the wait), and a page opened on one left long ago still waits that long. 'done' comes before the version history goes, so a version another page saved just before is deleted too, and it stays online for good with nothing of the person's in it, so a device reopened later (even offline with saves waiting) can never put anything back; any page of theirs can run deleting again from 'done' (only the sign-in failed); it stays 'done' (the rules allow only 'done' over it). Other pages hold their changes ("Saving…") and show the boards in a 'started' marker; on 'done' they ask Google whether the sign-in is gone (a forced token refresh, again each minute up to 10 times) and sign out with the note only if it is, so another tab of the same browser never signs out the deleting tab, and a page whose deleting stopped at the sign-in can still try again. Known limit: after a failed delete, signing out by hand on such a page shows the "deleted" note. A page that saw the shared boards go while the account was marked removes them without a Version history copy (the rules refuse versions then); on the deleting page the mark is off first, so it keeps one. Pages from before this change take the marker for their boards gone and sign out at once. The Privacy policy and Delete your account page mention the marker.

Each may be left out, as older copies of the app did.

(2) Ctrl+F does nothing while the keyboard shortcuts list is open, so recording a key can't open the search behind it. (3) In the Search box only the zoom keys reach the board; Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y there still undo the search text, not the board (a typo in the search box shouldn't undo the board). - 2026-10-21 (job #160, fragile-store F5; owner said yes to "keep a copy + note"; calls made by the worker under rule 4's exception). (1) A damaged save is copied to `note-board:v1:unreadable` before the page starts fresh. (2) An earlier kept copy is never replaced: a later, different damaged save goes beside it under `note-board:v1:unreadable-2`, `-3` and so on, because the later one usually holds the newer work and the earlier one may not have been looked at yet; the same save is kept only once, so reloading before any change doesn't pile up copies. (3) A save from a newer version of the app is not copied but left in place and never saved over, as the online copy already is (job 98): otherwise the old page's fresh board would replace it and the updated app would open on that, with the real boards only in the copy. (4) If the copy can't be written (storage full or blocked), the damaged save is left in place and that page doesn't save the boards on this device; reloading tries again. Owner may prefer overwriting in that case (new work kept, old save lost): left for the owner. (5) In (3) and (4) saving starts again once something else has replaced the save (another account signing in, job #145). (6) Only a save that can't be read at all counts: a readable save with one damaged board inside is unchanged (that board is dropped, as before). (7) Nothing reads a kept copy back yet; getting it back is a separate job if wanted. Deleting the account removes the copies (they hold that account's boards). (8) The note uses the existing notice banner, set as the page starts.

Firestore rules tests run on the local emulator: `@firebase/rules-unit-testing` 5.0.2 (the major that matches `firebase@12`) is a pinned dev package, but `firebase-tools` 15.32.1 (the emulator, about 500 packages including one that downloads a native binary while installing) is run with `npx --yes firebase-tools@15.32.1` from the `test:rules` script instead of being a dev package, so `npm ci` in the other jobs (the Pages deploy job among them) does not install it (code review finding); `firebase.json` sets the emulator to 127.0.0.1:8180 with its UI off and the project id is `demo-note-board`, which can't reach the real project. The tests live in `rules/` (outside `src/`, so `npm test` doesn't need Java or the emulator) and run with `npm run test:rules`; on GitHub a separate "Firestore rules tests" job (Java 21 via `actions/setup-java@v4`, the emulator cached between runs) feeds into "All tests". New rules tests go in `rules/*.test.ts` and start with `useRulesEnv()` from `rules/env.ts`.
- 2026-10-22 (owner yes; job 169, split Firebase's database code out of sign-in; calls made by the worker under rule 4's exception). `src/sync/firebase.ts` now holds only sign-in (the Firebase app, `watchUser`, sign-in and sign-out, `confirmSignIn`); everything that reads or writes Firestore moved unchanged to `src/sync/database.ts`, which `src/sync/session.ts` loads with `import('./database')` once someone is signed in (and the unused `src/sync/SyncedApp.tsx` is deleted). Measured with `npm run build`: the sign-in screen's scripts went from 1,001 KB (307 KB gzip) to 471 KB (154 KB gzip). Calls: (0) the download starts when Sign in is pressed (while Google's window is open), and at once on a device someone has signed in on before (so a returning person's board doesn't wait for it after the sign-in check); a person who signed out on that device downloads it on the sign-in screen too; (1) while the database code downloads the screen says "Opening your board…" (the existing loading state); if it can't be downloaded (offline and never kept from an earlier visit) the existing load-failed screen shows rather than loading forever; (2) a sign-out or another person signing in while it downloads wins: the start that was waiting does nothing (a counter in session.ts); (3) the rest of session.ts (sync, sharing, version history, account code) stays in the sign-in download: it isn't Firebase's database and moving it would reshape session.ts, where another job (149) has work waiting; (4) the speed test's script budget goes from 1,050 KB to 520 KB, and it now reads every script in full before adding up or checking them (it counted about 100 KB short before, as some were still being read). Left as is from the high review: deleting an account loads the database code again (already downloaded, so instant) rather than keeping it from the start, to keep the delete-account code (job 149's area) unchanged; and the database code is kept for offline use the way the board's code is since job 165a (the offline copy keeps what it fetches once running; on a very first visit, a sign-in before it is running leaves it to the next visit).
