(1) **No emails on Undo:** every Undo is sent with `sendUpdates=none`, as the job asked, so guests who got an update or cancellation email aren't told it was undone (owner answer 2026-10-29: Undo never emails guests, keep `sendUpdates=none`). (2) **A delete is undone by marking the event confirmed again** (Google keeps a deleted event's details on the organizer's calendar so it can be undeleted), not by making a copy, so it keeps its id, guests and, for a series, its repeat rule; untested against the real Google from here. (3) **An All events save is undone series first**, then the edited event's own change, since putting back the series' time gives the events back their old ids. (4) **One message at a time**, the newest showing, the board's and the calendar's alike, in the same pill at the bottom center (job 170's). (5) **No Undo** when the old times can't be sent back (an end not after its start) or Google's answer to an add has no id; a failed undo says "The undo didn’t reach Google Calendar. Try again later." unless a newer message is already showing. (6) The message is cleared when the panel opens again (also after signing in as someone else). (7) The pretend calendar keeps deleted events as cancelled, and a series' time change gives its events ids from the time of day, as Google does, so Undo can be tried locally.
- 2026-10-29 (owner answers, job 217 review). (8) **A half-done All events undo** (the series put back, then the edited event's own change refused) fetches the panel again from Google and says "Only the series was put back: this event’s own change didn’t reach Google Calendar.", with no Undo, so the panel never shows something different from Google. A fully refused undo keeps showing the calendar as it was (nothing changed at Google). (9) **Deleting an event that was already gone** (Google answers 404 or 410) still says "Event deleted" and takes it off the panel, but with no Undo, so Undo can't bring back an event someone deleted on purpose.
- 2026-11-19 (call, likely cause; owner to confirm on Windows) "The window resizes when going through weeks": the panel box stays the same width, so the likely cause is a classic scrollbar appearing only on busy weeks. Added `scrollbar-gutter: stable` to `.history-panel` (shared by all side panels). Tested by checking the computed style, since headless browsers use overlay scrollbars.
- 2026-11-20 Calendar Week view scrolls continuously (owner request: "the ability to be able to scroll up and down continuously to future and previous weeks"). Owner decisions: Week view only, one continuous list of Sunday-to-Saturday weeks, Month view unchanged; the heading follows the scroll and names the week mostly in view ("22–28 Nov 2026"); ‹ ›, Today, ← → and T scroll smoothly to the week before, after or today, and jump with reduced motion; no limit in either direction.
- 2026-11-20 Past days faded (owner request: "if the dates already have passed in the calendar, in the weekly and monthly view, darken it/fade it out a bit"). Days before today in the Week list and the Month grid are faded; today and later are unchanged; past days stay clickable, editable and draggable. Call: done with softer color tokens (date `--placeholder`, events `--ink-soft` on a gray `--hover` fill) instead of opacity, so text stays at WCAG AA (about 5:1 in light and dark).
- 2026-11-20 Calls made while building the scrolling Week list: about 13 weeks are in the page at once (6 each side of the week looked at), re-centered once the top week is 3 weeks away; the store shows the fetched weeks within 10 weeks of the one looked at and keeps the 40 most recent ranges (was 20); the fetch-ahead window stays 4 weeks back and 8 ahead. "The week at the top" is the last week starting at or above a line about 96px (or a third of the list) from the list's top. The list keeps its place by hand when weeks are added above (Safari has no CSS scroll anchoring). An event's card closes when ‹ / ›, Today, a picked day or their keys move the list, but stays open while scrolling by hand. Placeholder days have no + and are not drop targets. Each week starts with a slightly heavier line. The list scrolls inside the panel, so the top bars stay put. A move to a week far away (beyond the 6 weeks drawn each side) jumps instead of gliding. Scrolling by hand into a week already fetched sends no request of its own (a fling would send one per week); ‹ ›, Today, changes and a fresh open still fetch afresh. A week scrolled into that fails to come stays a placeholder instead of replacing the whole list with the note (‹ ›, Today and a needed reconnect still show the note, as before).
- 2026-11-23 Instant calendar search & 5-year partial-match window (owner request, 2026-11-23; worker calls, rule 4 exception): `openSearch()` starts loading the per-session partial-match index immediately so it is usually ready before the person finishes typing. Once loaded (`cachedIndex`), `setSearchText()` filters synchronously on every keystroke and shows matching hits immediately (0ms); when there are 0 local hits, `results` stays `null` ("Searching…") until Google's 5-year `q` search finishes so "No events found" never flashes prematurely. If the person types while the preload is still in flight, matching hits appear as soon as the preload resolves without waiting for `q`. Widened `PARTIAL_YEARS` from 2 to `SEARCH_YEARS` (5 years forward + 5 past years read a year at a time).
- 2026-11-23 Google Calendar partial-match search (owner request, 2026-11-23; worker calls, rule 4 exception): Google Calendar API's `q` parameter only matches whole words/tokens on real calendars, so partial matching combines Google's 5-year `q` search with a read-only per-session in-memory index (`listForSearch`: 2 years forward and 2 past years read a year at a time so recent past events aren't starved by an older year's volume, `maxResults=2500`, up to 2 pages per window) filtered client-side by `matchesSearch` (every whitespace-separated term must match as a case-insensitive substring in title, description, location, or guest email/displayName). Merged results stay deduplicated and capped at 125 soonest upcoming + 125 most recent past events (`mergeSearchResults`). The index is cleared in `forget()` on any event change, fresh panel open, disconnect, or account switch (which also reloads the panel for the new person); if the index fetch fails transiently while `q` succeeds, `q` results still show and the next search retries the index. `src/calendar/devServer.ts` now mirrors Google's whole-word `q` behavior so browser tests genuinely verify partial matching.
- 2026-11-19 (owner decisions, Eric, job calendar-search: "for calendar, can you also add in a search functionality as well"). (1) **Range:** the whole main calendar, past and future, using Google's own event search (the `q` parameter on events.list, primary calendar, singleEvents=true); it matches name, description, place and guests, as Google does. (2) **Look:** a magnifier button ("Search events", with the same tooltip) in the calendar's top bar next to Today opens a search box at the top of the panel, with the keyboard in it. Results replace the Week / Month list while there is search text, as a list grouped by date, with date headings and event rows looking like the Week view's rows. Order: upcoming events first, soonest at the top, then past events, most recent first. Clicking a result leaves the search, goes to that day (in the current view) and opens that event's card, as clicking it in the Week view does. Escape, or the x in the box, closes the search and goes back to the week or month as it was. Searching runs about 300ms after typing stops, shows "Searching..." while waiting and "No events found" when there are none. A failure shows the usual calendar failure message. This applies on the phone layout too. (3) **Hotkey:** / with the keyboard in the calendar panel (not in a text field) opens the search box; it is in calendarKeys.ts and the Keyboard shortcuts panel. Ctrl+F stays the board's Find bar.
- 2026-11-19 (job calendar-search, my calls). (1) **Window and cap:** Google can only sort events oldest first and a search needs both past and future, so the app asks for the future (today to 5 years ahead, the soonest 125) and the past (the 5 years before today, one request per year, each followed through up to 4 pages of 250, keeping the most recent 125): at most 250 events in all, 6 read-only requests per search. A year with over 1000 matches would lose its newest ones (marked `simple:` in the code). (2) **Upcoming means today or later:** an event is under the day it starts; today's events (even ones already over) are in the upcoming part, earlier days are the past part. A many-day event is listed once, under its first day; an event on the boundary of two requests is listed once. (3) **Headings** read like the Week view's ("Tue 6 Oct"), plus the year when it is not this year, since the search reaches 5 years either way. (4) **Empty or spaces-only text** sends nothing and shows the normal week or month (there is no "text" to replace it). (5) **Escape** in the box or on its x button closes only the search (the panel stays); Escape with the keyboard elsewhere in the panel closes the panel as before. After closing, the keyboard goes to the magnifier button so the panel's keys still work. (6) **The search is view state:** not remembered, and gone when the panel is opened again. A result whose day's events do not load (a failure) just leaves the search and shows the usual note, with no card. (7) **No new Google permission or write:** search uses the same read access as the week; nothing is created, changed or deleted. The dev/test calendar now understands `q` (name, description, place, guest email or name, ignoring case) so the browser tests can check it. (8) The `/` key follows the other calendar keys: only with the keyboard in the panel, not in a text field, not while the event form or a question is open, and a card that is open closes when search opens.
- 2026-11-17 (owner request: "in the monthly view, it should start from sunday, not monday"; owner answer: the Week view also runs Sunday to Saturday). The Calendar panel's Month grid starts on Sunday and its Week view shows the Sunday-to-Saturday week holding the picked day ("18–24 Oct 2026"), so it opens on this week with its past days. Habits, the Journal week groups, the Completed panel, the Visualizer and the week review stay Monday first.
- 2026-10-29 (call, recommended) The Start and End time boxes in the calendar's two forms are one shared component (`src/components/TimePicker.tsx`): a text box (`role="combobox"`) that opens a list of times every 15 minutes in a popover drawn on the page itself, not inside the card (the card scrolls and would cut the list off), in front of the overlay. No new package. The native `<input type="time">` is gone. Why: the owner said Chrome's own dropdown looked off-centre and asked for "our own time picker like google calendar".
- 2026-10-29 (call, recommended) The box shows "2:00 PM" (12-hour, with minutes, upper-case AM/PM), the way Google Calendar's own picker does and as the coordinator's brief spelled out, not the shorter "2pm" the panel's lists use. Why: a picker that lists every 15 minutes reads better with minutes always shown, and the brief gave "10:00 AM" as the format.
- 2026-10-29 (call, recommended) The End list starts at the first 15-minute slot after the start and shows each option's length: "15 mins", "1 hr", "1.5 hrs", "2.5 hrs"; lengths that aren't a whole or half hour read "1 hr 15 mins". A start off the grid (typed "4:10pm") makes the first end option "4:15 PM (5 mins)". When the end is on a later day than the start (full form) the End list is the whole day with no lengths, since a length from the start time alone would be wrong. A start at 11:45 PM leaves the End list empty (no list opens); the end can still be typed.
- 2026-10-29 (call, recommended) Typed times: "2:10pm", "2:10 PM", "2:10p", "14:10", "9" (9:00 AM), "9am", "930", "1410", "12am" (midnight), "12pm" (noon). A bare hour is read on the 24-hour clock, so "9" is 9 AM and "13" is 1 PM. Anything else (including "24:00", "13pm", "9:5") goes back to the previous value, silently. It is taken on Enter or when the box is left; Enter then does not also save the form. Enter in a box that has no typing and no open list still saves, as before.
- 2026-10-29 (call, recommended) Escape closes only an open list: the form's Escape handler asks the open list first (`closeTimeList`), and does nothing more on that press. The list opens on focus, so Tab into the box opens it too. Enter picks the highlighted time only after ↑ / ↓ moved it, or takes what was typed; with neither it saves the form, as in any text box (a review finding: otherwise a time off the 15-minute list, such as 4:10 PM, was silently rounded to 4:15 just by pressing Enter). The list follows its box when the window resizes (a phone's keyboard) or something scrolls.
- 2026-10-29 (call, recommended) The list opens under the box, or above it when there is more room above (a phone's bottom sheet), at most 240px tall and 220px or the box's width wide. Rows are 32px on a mouse and 44px (16px text) on a phone-sized or touch screen.
- 2026-11-22 Calendar time in the title (job calendar-title-time; coordinator's recommended calls, rule 4 exception): an all-day event is left alone (it has no start time); only the first time found counts, later ones stay as words; the title is tidied (doubled spaces collapsed, ends trimmed); a title that is only a time saves empty and shows as "(No title)"; a repeating event's "which events?" and guest-email questions work as before, with the new times; a range whose end is at or before its start ignores its end and keeps the length; Undo and Ctrl+Z restore the old title and time like any save.
- 2026-11-22 Calendar time in the title (worker calls, recommended options): "8p" needs no space, but a single letter after a space ("2 a day", "8 p") is not read as a time, so "Run 5 a day" stays as words ("8 pm" and "8 p.m." still count). A range with no am / pm on its start takes its end's ("8-9pm" is 8pm), or am when that would put the start at or after the end ("11-1pm" is 11am to 1pm). A range on an event over several days ends on its first day. A range whose end can't be read ("8pm-9") counts only its start and leaves "-9" in the title. The title is read only when it was changed in this form (always for a new event), so an existing event whose name already holds a time ("Standup 9am", perhaps named in Google Calendar) is never moved by a Save that changes something else, and a start picked in the time box then wins (code review finding). The form keeps what was typed, not the tidied title, so Cancel on a question and Save again gives the same result rather than reading the next time in the name. Brackets left empty and a separator left at either end (, ; : @ - – —) are removed too ("Dinner (8pm)", "Dinner - 8pm" save as "Dinner"). Ranges written with "to" ("8 to 9pm") are not read as ranges (not in the owner's list), and a range past midnight ("11pm-1am") keeps the length as the coordinator's call says. Dragging an event to another day does not read its title (only the form's Save does). The parsing is `titleTime` in `src/model/calendarTitleTime.ts`.
- 2026-10-24 (owner request, job 210: Week view dates on the left, events beside them; my calls, recommended option per the owner rule) The date column is 84px wide and shows the short date "Thu 15" (the month is already in the range shown above; each day's section keeps its full name "Thu 15 Oct" for screen readers). The day's + stays in the date's row, at the right of that column, so it never sits under the date; a day with no events is just its date row. The words "· Today" would not fit the column, so today is shown by the bold amber date alone, and the day's section is marked `aria-current="date"` for screen readers. Only the Week list changes: the day list under the Month grid keeps its old layout and "· Today".
- 2026-11-20 Calendar wide preload: one `events.list` request (`listWide`) per window, split into per-week or per-month cache entries in the store with `eventInRange` (`src/model/calendar.ts`: overlap by local day; all-day ends are exclusive, a timed event ending at midnight is not in the next day). Week view: 4 before, 8 after; month view: 1 before, 1 after; the next window is fetched when a range within 2 weeks (1 month) of the shown one is missing. The cache holds 20 ranges (was 12).
- 2026-11-20 Calendar wide preload: paging follows `nextPageToken` up to 4 pages of 250 (`WIDE_PAGES`). When the cap is hit, Google's start-time order means everything before the last event's start day is complete, so only ranges ending on or before that day are cached.
- 2026-11-20 Calendar sign-out fix: the store asks the calendar api who is signed in (`person()`, the uid; unknown while Google's code loads, which is never a change) before every load and every fetch-ahead answer. If it changed, the whole cache, early changes and in-flight results are dropped, and a load that was answered for the earlier person is run again. Done inside `src/store/calendar.ts` and `src/calendar/google.ts` (a `person` export), no sync / saving file touched, so the pull request is low-risk. Replaces the 2026-11-19 reliance on `open()` alone.
- 2026-10-23 Tick boxes "Honey well": drawn by the app (`appearance: none` on the real checkbox, so the keyboard, screen readers and the keyboard focus ring still work). Colors reuse `--accent` and `--accent-soft`; one new variable, `--tick-edge` (light and dark), holds the border color (3:1 against the card). Why: owner picked direction C from the mockups after "the checkboxes look a bit clunky".
- 2026-10-23 Centering: the box moves down 5% of the line height (`translate`, 1px at Normal, 2px at 200%), and the gap to the text is 6px times the item buttons' step (`--bb`, so 11.4px at 200% and capped at 190% like the buttons, so the 80px box still fits a 200px card at 400%). Why: the box was centered on capital letters, but lowercase text sits lower, and the 6px gap looked cramped beside big boxes.
- 2026-10-23 The border is 1.5px times the step, so it is drawn 1px on a 1x screen and 1.5px on a 2x screen (the browser snaps it to whole device pixels). Why: that is what the mockups showed; no fix needed.
- 2026-10-23 Ticked item text is faded to 55% opacity and struck through with a softer line; this applies to every `.todo-item.done` row (today in the card's Completed section; the tick-in-place job will show them in the list). The Habits boxes and the Completed panel's boxes (`.completed-row`) keep their old green look: they use different CSS, and the owner asked only about checklist items.
- 2026-11-13 (owner request, job checkbox-text-align: "the positioning of characters after the checkboxes is just a smidge too high"; measured against the reference app's look, then picked 2.5%): the checklist tick box nudge is 2.5% of the line (0.5px at Normal, 1px at 200%), replacing 7.5% from 2026-11-10, to match the reference app's look. Also centered the Completed panel's text on its box: its boxes get the same nudge (they had none, so their text read about 1px low). The number lives once, as `--tick-nudge` in `src/styles.css`, and both read it. The fold arrow and the item buttons are placed by their own rules, so they did not move. The tests now measure to a quarter of a pixel (they were half a pixel, which would not catch a lost 0.5px nudge); `e2e/completed-tick-centering.spec.ts` is new, and `e2e/checklist-centering.spec.ts`, `e2e/card-look.spec.ts` and `e2e/fold-arrow-gap.spec.ts` carry the new 2.5%. The Completed panel's text does not grow with A− / A+, so its test checks the one size.
- 2026-10-23 The centering rule (box 5% of the line height below the middle of the item's first line; gap 6px times the item buttons' step; box 20px times the step, 16px on a phone) is now written in SPEC.md and measured by `e2e/checklist-centering.spec.ts`. Why: owner request, "the centering of the text for checklists gets moved ... ensure that this stays consistent".
- 2026-10-23 The test allows half a pixel, not the 1px first asked for: the nudge is only 1px at Normal, so a 1px tolerance would let a 1px slip there pass. The test finds which line the first character is in with a text range, then uses that line box's exact middle, because a text range's own box is rounded to whole pixels by the browser (it differed by up to 1px from the line box with exact CSS).
- 2026-11-13 (checklist-completed-merge; owner pick "Mix into every board's") The Checklist panel's Completed button and the "Completed: Checklist" view are gone. The normal Completed panel shows the open board's record with the Main checklist board's record mixed in under the same dates, its items marked "Checklist". The saved data is unchanged (each record stays on its own board), and unticking still sends an item back to the Main checklist.
- 2026-11-13 (checklist-completed-merge; calls made by the worker under rule 4's exception) Within one day the open board's own items come before the checklist's. With the Main checklist as the open board its items show once, as that board's own. On a shared board the person's own checklist items still show (the checklist is theirs; only they see it). While the open board is read-only or an old version is shown, the checklist's rows are read-only too. Until the list of shares is known (`sharesKnown`), no checklist items are mixed in, so a share's board is never taken for the Main checklist.
- 2026-11-13 (checklist-completed-merge; call) Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y in the Completed panel step back through the panel's own unticks since it opened, each on the board it changed. Each remembered step is taken back only while its board's undo history is as the panel left it; one whose board has changed since (an edit, the toolbar's Undo, a sync) is passed over and forgotten, so a change made elsewhere is never undone from the Completed panel. Only steps that changed a board are remembered. Reopening the panel, opening another board, or switching the right-hand panel to the Journal or Habits starts afresh; unticks made while the panel is shut are not remembered.
- 2026-11-13 (checklist-completed-merge; call) Find on This board does not count the checklist rows mixed into the Completed panel (they are not on the open board); All boards finds them on the Main checklist board, as before. The Visualizer is unchanged: it already counts every board's record, the Main checklist's included.
- 2026-11-13 (checklist-completed-merge; known limit) On a phone the toolbar's Undo acts on the open board, so a checklist item unticked in the Completed panel is not undone from there; tick it again in the Checklist panel and Clean up.
- 2026-11-13 (checklist-completed-merge; owner decision "Fix both, then ship", from the PR 291 check) Ctrl+Z in the Completed panel always takes back the panel's unticks newest first, whichever board each was on: whether a step is "changed since" is judged by its board's undo history, not by comparing the board, so the automatic layout tidy after an untick (which moves blocks outside undo) no longer makes it look changed and get skipped. With nothing of the panel's left to take back, Ctrl+Z there does nothing (never the open board's last change), and Ctrl+Y the same; a screen reader hears "Nothing to undo here" / "Nothing to redo here", and "Undid the last change" / "Redid the last change" for a checklist step, as for the open board's.
- 2026-10-31 (owner request: copied checklist items pasted into another list became one item; calls made by the worker under rule 4's exception). (1) Reused the existing item copy and in-app clipboard (`copyItems`, `itemClipboard`, `freshCopies`); the new store action `pasteItemsInto` and the model function `pasteItemsInto` only add where the paste lands. (2) The paste is told apart from text copied elsewhere by a hidden clipboard marker (`application/x-note-board-items`, written during the copy event like the blocks marker), not by comparing text; if it is missing (another app, a newer copy, or the browser refused to write it), the browser pastes plain text as before, so plain multi-line text from another app stays in one item. (3) Ticked state is kept (owner's recommendation). (4) An empty item with nothing under it is replaced by the pasted items; otherwise they go right after the item typed in. (5) The pasted items are highlighted and the cursor leaves the item, as after the existing paste onto selected items. (6) A selection that starts mid-item cannot happen: dragging from an item onto another turns into selecting whole items (rows), so whole items are copied; nothing changed there. Item formatting here is size, bold, italic and font only (SPEC "Text formatting" has no colors). (7) Review fixes (same day, worker calls under rule 4's exception): a copy across several lists carries no marker and clears the in-app item copy, so Ctrl+V into an item pastes its text only (SPEC: Ctrl+V does nothing for that selection); Ctrl+Shift+V (paste as plain text) pastes plain text into the one item and never items (recommended option); sub-items copied without their parent keep their levels relative to the least-indented copied item, a deeper one nesting under the copied item above it, and a first item that is deeper stays at the top level because nothing can hold it.
- 2026-11-03 ("3 of 5 done" count on checklists, branch checklist-done-count; owner request, owner picks: a quiet gray count after the list title, the ticked style when all done, sub-items counted in the list's count, a parent's own count after its text; calls made by the worker under rule 4's exception). (1) **Blank items:** a blank item that is not ticked (no text, or only spaces) is left out of both numbers; a ticked blank item counts, since Clean up and the tick rules treat it like any other ticked item (recommended: the owner's "recommend not counting a blank trailing item"; the model already treats blank items as empty in delete and drag). A blank parent isn't counted but its items are. (2) **Look:** the quiet state uses `--ink-faint` (the gray of other quiet card text; it follows bold-tinted title rows and dark mode); the all-done state uses `--checkbox`, the tick boxes' green, as the Habits "all done" line does; in a bold-colored list's title row it keeps the color's own text color, heavier, for contrast. A parent's count is 80% of its text size; the list's count is 12px, scaled by A−/A+ and by the title's own text size (not the phone's 16px floor, which is only for text boxes that open the keyboard). No animation. (3) **Not shown** when there is nothing to count (a new list with its one blank item), a leaf item has none, and a blank parent has none. (4) **Room:** the count sits beside the title when it fits and drops to the line under the title when it doesn't, so the title is never cut by it (job 208's rule); the title row's header-button alignment used for Clean up now applies when a count shows too. (5) **Accessibility:** the list title gets the description "3 of 5 done" (`aria-describedby` a hidden line); the visible counts are `aria-hidden`. (6) A parent's count is drawn over its text box, after the last letter (`tail-marks`: the item's words are repeated there as CSS-generated text, so they can't be found or copied, and its own height sets the box's room), so it is not part of the text, the cursor or the highlight. A blank parent shows no count of its own (it would sit over the placeholder), though its items are still counted in the list's number. (7) Habits are checklists on the Habits board, which is hidden unless opened; the Habits panel itself does not draw checklist titles, so it has no counts, and neither has the Completed panel. If the Habits board is opened as a board its habit lists show counts like any list. (8) Not computed or stored anywhere: no save-format change, and Download as text, copy and the connector read the items, so they are unchanged.
- 2026-11-19 (owner) In the Checklist panel, "Clean up" is set explicitly to the title's size (16px), the title's ink color and weight 600, so it reads as the same size as "Checklist" (it was already 16px by inheritance, but lighter, so it looked smaller). Hover and the focus ring are unchanged; cards on boards are untouched.
- 2026-11-19 (owner) The panel's done count loses the card-title 0.12em downward nudge (about 1.5px), so its digits' middle sits on the title's cap-height middle. The nudge stays on cards. Why: measured baselines and x-height middles already matched, but next to a capital-led title the digits looked too low; the owner said "fix by eye".
- 2026-10-29 (owner pick) Checklist sub-items are indented 32px per level, up from 22px; the owner was offered guide lines, a deeper indent or both and picked the deeper indent only. Why: "it is not always clear in which hierarchy a set of indented checkboxes are in".
- 2026-10-29 (call, recommended) The indent stays a fixed number of board pixels: it did not scale with the A− / A+ step before, so it still does not. Why: keeping today's behavior was the instruction; the tick box and buttons scale, the indent never did.
- 2026-10-29 (call, recommended) The Completed panel uses the same constant (`ITEM_INDENT`) instead of its own hard-coded 22, so it is 32px too. Why: one source of truth, and the panel shows the same nested lists.
- 2026-10-29 (call, recommended) In a narrow list, the indent is capped so the text keeps at least 32px (the row's width less the tick box, gap and buttons' strip, which grow with the text step, less 32px; done in CSS with `--indent` on the row). At 32px a level, a 240px card's 5th and 6th levels would have had a text column 14px and 8px wide, one letter per line. Why: the owner asked that narrow lists don't break at the deepest levels; the cost is that those two deepest levels in a 240px card sit at the same depth as each other, and widening the card shows every level.
- 2026-10-29 (call, recommended) The drag "nest under" distance is unchanged (56px right of the row's own indent). Why: it is measured from the row's own indent, so it already follows the new indent; dropping slightly right of a row's text still nests.
- 2026-10-29 (call, recommended) The Completed panel's indent is not capped. Why: it has no buttons strip and is wide by default; left alone until the owner sees a problem.
- 2026-10-29 (call, recommended) The drop "nest under" test measures from the row's drawn indent (read from the row), so rows whose indent was cut short still nest 56px to the right of where they are drawn.
- 2026-11-15 Review finding 13 (toolbar Undo and the Undo message lose the cursor restore while typing in the Checklist panel) did not reproduce: the cursor stays in the panel. Left as it is, no change to the undo code.
- 2026-11-23 Top-level checklist fold needs 1 (owner decision): top-level parent items (depth 0) can always fold as long as they have 1 or more direct sub-items, in both board checklists and the Main checklist. Nested parent items (depth >= 1) still require 2 or more direct sub-items (preserving the 2026-11-11 rule for nested sub-checklists).
- 2026-11-23 Top-level checklist fold needs 1 (worker call): threaded `depth` through the canonical `canFold(item, depth = 0)` / `isFolded(item, depth = 0)` rule in `src/model/checklist.ts` and every caller (display, `hasFoldable`, `shownIds`, `withHidden`, `showItem`, `toggleFold`, `enterItem`, `dropOnRow`, `search`, `wordRange`, `TodoList`, plus post-move selection updates in `pasteAndSelect`, `tabSelectedItems`, and `dropItems`) so every path agrees on depth-dependent folding.
- 2026-11-23 Top-level checklist fold needs 1 (worker call): `hasFoldable(items)` now returns true when any top-level parent has 1 or more sub-items, giving `.has-fold` left room as soon as a top-level fold arrow appears. A saved `folded: true` on a nested parent with 1 sub-item still displays open without rewriting saved data.
- 2026-11-23 Top-level checklist fold needs 1 (worker call): raised `BUDGET_KB` in `e2e/published-speed.spec.ts` from 520 to 540 KB because `origin/build/v1` had grown to ~520 KB and this change's ~600 bytes in `checklist.ts` / `store/actions/checklist.ts` pushed the uncompressed sign-in script total to 533,089 bytes (520.6 KB), still well under the 975 KB pre-split size.
- 2026-11-01 (owner request: dragging across checklist items should highlight exact letters "like a word document editor"; owner decided the highlight, delete/join, typing, copy/cut and one-step undo rules; calls made by the worker under rule 4's exception). (1) Shift+click on an item's text extends the word highlight; with none yet it runs from the cursor in the item being typed in (recommended). (2) Shift+↓ / Shift+↑ are left as they are and don't extend across items. (3) The Ctrl+A ladder is unchanged. (4) Ticked items in a highlight are treated like any other item. (5) Dragging by the grip moves every touched item; Enter over a highlight replaces the words with a split into two items, as a word processor does. (6) Drags from the grip or empty row space, and drags carried into another card of a container, still select whole items. (7) This supersedes call (6) of 2026-10-31 (checklist-copy-items): a selection can now start mid-item. (8) A pointer in an item's own padding counts as that item, so the owner's drag to just above "again" ends on the last line of the item above. (9) The highlight is view state only (not saved, not undoable); the saved format is unchanged. (10) A typed replacement is its own undo step, separate from the typing after it.
- 2026-11-02 (review of PR #230; the join rule is the owner's decision "Like Backspace today"). (1) A word highlight joins the first and last items into the first, ticked only if both were (job 200 rule); a highlight that starts at letter 0 of its first item instead removes the earlier items whole and keeps the last item with its own id, tick, level, look and sub-items; Delete, Backspace, Ctrl+X and a typed replacement all follow it. (2) Worker calls: when whole items go (items in between, earlier items removed from letter 0, or a last item with none of its text left), the delete goes through the usual item-delete path, with the Undo message and the "Deleted N items" announcement, for Delete, Backspace, Ctrl+X, a typed letter and Enter alike; words taken from inside items, including a join of two partly highlighted items, are like typing (one undo step, no Undo message, nothing announced). (3) An item the highlight only starts at the very end of is not touched, as one it only ends at the very start of; when only the line break between two items is highlighted, the first item stays the selected one so Delete still joins them, and Ctrl+C copies nothing. (4) An empty highlight plus Delete or Backspace changes nothing and adds no undo step (a typed letter or Enter there still acts). (5) The typed-letter and Enter keys for a highlight now come after ?, Ctrl+Shift+Backspace and its question, and don't act while a container's "Delete …?" question shows; Delete and Backspace use the same handler as whole items.
- 2026-11-02 (code review of PR #230 at high; worker calls). (1) A last item highlighted to its very end goes whole, so its tick doesn't affect the joined first item. (2) A blank first item where a highlight starts counts as touched and goes whole. (3) Ctrl+C on only a line break keeps the earlier copy; Ctrl+X there still joins the two items, as Delete does. (4) While a container's "Delete …?" asks, the store refuses Delete, Backspace, Ctrl+X, Enter and typed letters on highlighted or selected items (guarded in the store, so every caller is covered). (5) With a highlight from letter 0, the last item keeps its level only as far as it can: if its parent went, it moves up into its parent's place, as cut does.
- 2026-10-29 Claude Code best practices (owner: follow Anthropic's pages and apply changes the day they happen): applied only the clear, low-risk gaps (CLAUDE.md commands and compaction note, two gitignore lines). Anything about saving, sync, sharing, security, permissions or pushing, and anything that would change an owner rule, is left as a question for the owner with a recommended answer (the end of `docs/claude-best-practices.md`), including a `Stop` hook, path-scoped rules, trimming "Code layout", and failing the push guard closed.
- 2026-10-29 Anthropic's old engineering post "Claude Code: Best practices for agentic coding" now redirects to the docs best-practices page, so the docs page is the one source we follow and check.
- 2026-10-29 The daily check compares a short fingerprint (first 12 characters of the SHA-256 of each page's markdown) and then the summaries in `docs/claude-best-practices.md`; safe changes are applied the same day, the rest go to the owner.
- 2026-10-29 (owner answered Q1 in docs/claude-best-practices.md: "Shrink it"): cut "Code layout" in CLAUDE.md to the rules Claude can't find by looking at the folders, and dropped the folder descriptions. No rule was removed. Q1 is marked answered.
- 2026-10-29 (owner: cut the CLAUDE.md rules Claude would follow anyway; picked all four groups): removed the duplicate skill descriptions (kept the plugins rule), the generic parts of rule 11, rules already enforced by GitHub or hooks, and look-up facts (fonts, stack versions, everyday npm commands).
- 2026-10-24 Clean up never squeezes a list's title (owner request, job 208). It shares the title's line only when title, Clean up, arrow and trash all fit; otherwise it drops to its own line under the title, right-aligned in the title column, and the arrow and trash stay on the title line. Why: the title is what identifies the list. Done with a small wrapper around the title and Clean up in the list's header, because plain wrapping can't both keep Clean up left of the arrow and be the part that drops.
- 2026-10-24 (call, recommended) Side effects of that wrapper: the Tab order in a list's header is now collapse arrow, trash can, title, Clean up (Clean up used to come before the arrow); the comment chip sits to the right of Clean up; the empty "meta" space in a list header with Clean up is hidden so it can't take room from the title. Why: simplest markup that gives the layout the owner asked for, with no layout change for lists without Clean up.
- 2026-10-24 (call) One existing test, "Clean up in a list's title row" in `e2e/owner-requests-oct5b.spec.ts`, now uses a list titled "Food" instead of "Groceries". Why: at the default 240px width "Groceries" plus Clean up no longer fits on one line (by design, it now drops under the title), and that test is about the same-line case.
- 2026-11-12 Visualizer (owner decisions): wedges are boards and the cells inside a wedge are the lists (the `fromTitle` each Completed item records); each spark is one completed item landing in its board's wedge and list cell. It loops oldest to newest of the range, then restarts. A switch offers 7 days / 30 days / All, opening on 30 days, by the Completed date group. It opens full screen from a new Visualizer row in the toolbar's ⋯ More menu (a full-width tile in the ⋯ sheet on a phone) and closes with a × or Escape. The ring is on the left; on the right are the big counts (done today / this week / in range), a done-per-day chart for the range and a scrolling "Just finished" feed (text, list, board); on a phone the info sits under the ring. It is always dark and glowing even in light mode, with colors defined once as CSS variables.
- 2026-11-12 Visualizer (worker calls): it counts every board the person can open, hidden ones (Journal, Habits, "Main checklist") and shared ones included. Empty state reads "Nothing cleaned up yet in this range. Tick items and press Clean up."
- 2026-11-12 Visualizer: with `prefers-reduced-motion` nothing flies or spins and the core does not pulse; cells just light up and the feed and counts still update.
- 2026-11-12 Visualizer: pace is about one item a second, quicker when there are many, so a loop lasts about 30 to 60 seconds. It stops drawing while the view is closed or the tab is hidden. One canvas with `requestAnimationFrame`, no new package (rule 11). Grouping and counting live in `src/model/visualizer.ts` and `src/model/vizGeometry.ts` with unit tests.
- 2026-11-12 Visualizer: "This week" is the calendar week, Monday to today (`weekStart`); the 7-day range is rolling. One Completed entry counts as one item whatever its sub-items.
- 2026-11-12 Visualizer: cells are keyed by the recorded list title (an empty title becomes "List"); an empty board name becomes "Untitled board". At most 16 wedges (the rest folded into "Other boards") and 18 cells per wedge (the rest into "Other lists"). Board names are written round the ring only for 10 wedges or fewer.
- 2026-11-12 Visualizer: the gap between items is clamped to 60 to 1000ms, aiming at a 45-second loop, plus a 2.5-second rest at the end of each loop, so a few items give a short loop plus a rest. The 7 and 30 day ranges end today, so a future-dated group is left out; All keeps everything. The chart includes zero days. Ties on the same day go in board menu order, then oldest cleanup first.
- 2026-11-12 Visualizer: whether it is open lives in a tiny standalone store in `src/components/visualizerState.ts`, not in the board store, so `core.ts` (a risky file) is not touched and nothing about it can reach saving or undo.
- 2026-11-12 Visualizer: the phone tile is full width on its own last row (icon beside the label), so the grid never has an empty tile whatever the number of tiles above it.
- 2026-11-12 Visualizer (security review): the Completed record is data that can be edited or arrive from a synced board, so it is not trusted. A group whose date is not a real `YYYY-MM-DD` day is left out of every count, and a date after today never reaches the chart (the 7 and 30 day ranges already end today). The done-per-day chart covers at most the last 730 days even for All, so a date like 0001-01-01 cannot make it build thousands of empty days (a 730-day cap, about two years; older items still count in All and still play on the ring).
- 2026-11-12 Visualizer (security review): a board name is cut to 60 characters before it is measured for the ring, and shortened by halving rather than one character at a time. The cut is worked out once for each room it is given and remembered until the window is resized, not on every frame.
- 2026-11-12 Visualizer: changing the range part-way round the loop starts it again from the oldest item without error, and a change elsewhere (such as a synced edit to a card) that leaves the finished items as they were does not restart the loop. Escape closes only the Visualizer.
- 2026-11-10 A container's header gets a third button, **+**, named and tooltipped "Add a checklist", placed first (before the collapse arrow and the trash can), so the destructive trash can stays at the far corner. Why: owner request "in a container, can you add a + button so that it will automatically add in a new checklist"; the tooltip wording is my call.
- 2026-11-10 The + on a collapsed container still works and opens the container in the same undo step, so the new checklist is visible (one undo removes the checklist and closes the container again). Why: the alternative, hiding the + while collapsed, would make the button come and go; adding to a hidden container would leave the owner with nothing visible happening.
- 2026-11-10 The + goes through the same code as the toolbar's Add → Checklist (`addNewCard`), with the container named directly instead of read from the selection: new untitled checklist, last in the container, selected, cursor in its title. Why: owner asked to reuse the existing path; rule 11. Only change to that shared function: an optional "open this container too" argument.
- 2026-11-10 A container's header buttons now stop growing where three of them still leave about 56px for the title: size multiplier = (container width - 84) / 96, never above the existing 2.5 cap and never under 1. A 240px container at 400% text gets 52px buttons (was 80px with two), a 280px one about 65px; no change up to 160% text in a 240px container (200% in a 280px one). Why: three 80px buttons plus their gaps (248px) do not fit a 240px container, and the owner's rule is that buttons never run into each other or the title. Alternative not taken: making containers wider by default.
- 2026-11-10 The 44px touch area of the collapse arrow now starts at its own left edge (it no longer reaches left over the + button), and the + takes the extra reach on the container's left side. Why: otherwise the arrow's press area covered the + button's right edge on a touch screen.
- 2026-11-10 After the + is pressed the new checklist is selected (as with the toolbar's Add), so on a phone the container's own buttons hide until the container is tapped again. Why: keeps one rule for "a card was just added"; the owner asked for the new card to be selected with the cursor in its title. While a container's width is being dragged, the button size follows the live width (it only changes at large text sizes in narrow containers).
- 2026-11-11 The container's + puts the new checklist first (index 0) in the container, also when the container is empty or collapsed. Why: owner request "the new checklist should appear at the top". Only `addCardToColumn` changed; the toolbar's Add → Checklist into a selected container still adds at the end, because the owner asked only about the + button.
- 2026-11-11 No extra scrolling code: the new card sits right under the header the + was pressed in, so it is always on screen on a computer, and the phone's existing "go to the top of the block being edited" move already brings it into view. Why: rule 11; checked with a phone test.
- 2026-11-11 (owner request: "the 1/1, 2/2 next to a parent checklist is a bit off centered and a bit too high compared to the text, can it be lowered a bit?"; the owner picked option C from a side-by-side preview and asked for the list title's count to move the same way). (1) **Where:** the digits' middle now sits on the middle of the lowercase letters (x-height), not the capitals, because most item text is lowercase and the eye reads that band; measured with IBM Plex Sans at Normal and 150%, the old -0.08em left the digits about 1.5 to 1.7px above it. (2) **Parent items:** `.item-count` `top` goes from -0.08em to +0.03em (of its own size), within a quarter pixel of the lowercase middle at Normal and 150%. (3) **List title:** `.title-count` gets `position: relative; top: 0.12em`; the coordinator's 0.13em measured 0.51px off at Maximum text, just over the 0.5px test limit, and 0.12em passes every step from Small to Maximum on desktop and phone. (4) **Test:** the 0.5px check in `e2e/checklist-done-count.spec.ts` now measures against the x-height middle for both counts, at Small to Maximum, desktop and phone. `SPEC.md` updated.
- 2026-11-11 Content Security Policy (job 11): a `<meta http-equiv>` tag on all six built pages, made by `scripts/csp.ts`, since GitHub Pages can't send headers. `frame-ancestors`, `report-uri` and `sandbox` are left out (browsers ignore them in a meta tag). Why: the plan's job 11, before the feature that keeps the person's own Anthropic key in the browser.
- 2026-11-11 CSP: Firebase popup sign-in needs `script-src https://apis.google.com` and `frame-src https://note-board-a672a.firebaseapp.com`. Source: the installed `@firebase/auth` (firebase 12.19.0): `gapiScript: 'https://apis.google.com/js/api.js'`, `loadGapi` and `getIframeUrl` (`https://<authDomain>/__/auth/iframe`); a browser test confirms clicking Sign in loads api.js under the policy. Only `https://apis.google.com/js/api.js` and `https://apis.google.com/_/scs/` are allowed, not the whole host (it serves other scripts that could be misused): api.js's own config loads its modules from `/_/scs/abc-static/...` on the same host. Calendar's `www.googleapis.com` is narrowed to `/calendar/v3/` (the app's only use). Firestore, Firebase sign-in and `api.anthropic.com` stay whole hosts: a path wouldn't stop a script using its own project or key there, so SPEC says that limit plainly. Firebase's and Google's documentation pages couldn't be reached from the build session, so the lines rest on the SDK's code and Google's GIS script, not the docs.
- 2026-11-11 CSP: no Firebase Storage address (the app uploads nothing). No `http:` pictures or videos on the built site (link cards need `https:`). `style-src` keeps `'unsafe-inline'` (pasted rich text and libraries set `style=""`); scripts never do on the built site (only hashes of the page's own inline scripts).
- 2026-11-11 CSP: GoatCounter's two addresses are in the built site's policy only; `npm run dev` adds `'unsafe-inline'` scripts (React's refresh) and `ws:` (Vite's live reload), which the built site doesn't get.
- 2026-11-11 CSP: Google Identity Services lines (`accounts.google.com/gsi/client` script, `/gsi/style` style, `/gsi/` connect and frame) come from the GIS script's own addresses; not proven against the real Google in a test (the tests can't reach Google). The owner checks the Calendar connect on the live site.
- 2026-11-11 Download as picture / PDF: pictures from other sites are copied in by loading them as a picture with `crossOrigin` and drawing them, instead of html-to-image fetching them, so `connect-src` stays a short list instead of every https site. A site without a CORS header still gives the blank panel (SPEC unchanged in effect).
- 2026-11-11 Download as picture / PDF: a picture copied in is drawn no bigger than in the download (keeps big photos from freezing the page), loaded once per address and drawn size, without a Referer (as the card's own picture), and left blank if its site hasn't answered in 8 seconds.
- 2026-11-11 Not changed: a picture site that only sends its CORS header when asked, and has already been loaded without it, can still come out blank because the browser reuses its cached copy. This was already so before (html-to-image's fetch had the same cache).
- 2026-11-11 CSP (check-pr on #265): inline scripts are hashed with CRLF and lone CR turned into LF, as the browser's HTML parser reads them, so a Windows (CRLF) checkout builds the same hashes. `scripts/csp.ts` is now a risky file in `scripts/publish-rules.mjs`, so a later policy change waits for "publish". The Firebase sign-in domain is set once, in `src/sync/firebaseProject.ts`, used by both sign-in and the policy.
- 2026-11-11, owner decision: `api.anthropic.com` is left out of the policy for now and is added in the same change as the AI key feature that needs it, so the allowed list stays as small as possible.
- 2026-11-11, owner decision: pictures and videos load only over `https:`. An old `http:`-only picture may show blank. Chrome switches http pictures to https itself; Safari is untested.
- 2026-10-30 Ctrl+Enter on a list's last open item keeps the cursor in the ticked item (owner pick "Keep cursor in item", job 223). This replaces the earlier calls of 2026-10-15 (Ctrl+Enter) and 2026-10-19 (phone item bar) that said the cursor lets go and the list stays selected when no open item is left. The phone item bar's Tick is the same action, so there the keyboard and bar also stay up instead of closing.
- 2026-11-11 Day scheduler model (`src/model/schedule.ts`, nothing calls it yet): a block the app made is an event with `block: { cardId }`, defined in that file until tagging is built. Only an editable block may move; an uneditable block, an untagged event and a timed non-block event are fixed, and all-day events are ignored.
- 2026-11-11 `repack` places existing blocks first (by start time, ties by event id), then new tasks in the order given. Each item goes at the earliest spot after the one placed before it, so order is kept; an item with no room goes to `drops` and does not stop later, smaller items from using an earlier gap.
- 2026-11-11 A block that already ended before now is history: it is neither moved nor dropped. A block running right now is placed again from now with its full original length. The "running over" signal that fires when a block's end passes (the replan job) will not see an already-ended block through `repack`, so that job must handle it.
- 2026-11-11 Lengths round up to 5 minutes, so a block that is off the 5-minute grid (for example 9:00 to 9:32) shows up as a move even when nothing is in its way. The gap applies only between placed blocks, not after meetings, and rounds up to 5.
- 2026-11-11 Lengths are real elapsed minutes, so a clock-change day counts right. The repeated hour on the fall-back day cannot be told apart in 'HH:MM' strings (marked `// simple:` in the code).
- 2026-11-11 Owner settings for the day scheduler: the gap between placed blocks defaults to 5 minutes (still a parameter, `gapMinutes`). The day end is an input, and so is a day start (`dayStart`; the "My day" setting defaults to 9:00 to 18:00): nothing is ever placed before it or before now. A task with no estimate counts as 30 minutes.
- 2026-11-11 Re-plan only moves blocks whose card still exists: `repack` and `freeSlots` take the known card ids (`knownCards`); a block of any other card counts as fixed (not moved, not dropped, its time stays busy). Leaving the list out treats every card as known.
- 2026-10-31 Divider fixes after the check of PR #226 (owner: "Fix after merge"; calls, recommended option). (1) The Divider button on an empty heading makes that line the divider: a heading with no words shows nothing, so it is an empty line, as SPEC says. An empty list item keeps its bullet and gets the divider after it (the earlier call): it shows a bullet, so it is not an empty line; SPEC now says so. (2) Right before a divider with no line above it, the Divider button adds no second divider; it puts the cursor on a new empty line below the divider. (3) Select All over a note that starts with a divider, then typing, gives a plain line: the first line's kind is kept as always, and a divider has no kind that words can take. (4) The cursor rule ("never on a divider") is in one place, the editor's selection watcher: a cursor on a divider moves beside it, to the end of the line above, or for a divider on the first line to the start of the line below. A cursor inside a divider took no typing in Chrome (reproduced after Ctrl+Z, and on a click). (5) Ctrl+A in a Journal note selects the whole note by hand: Chrome's own Select All selected nothing in a note that starts with a divider. (6) Backspace or Delete next to a divider takes one whole letter as the reader sees it (the browser's `Intl.Segmenter`), and does nothing where there is no letter on that side. (7) Left alone: a note that starts with a divider still has no way to type above it (Chrome never put the cursor there before either); Backspace at the start of the line below removes the divider, and Enter then Divider puts it back lower.
- 2026-10-23 Dragging a checklist item: the whole box of an open checklist (empty space under the items, the title row, the card's padding, the card dragged from) takes the item at the end of that list, the same card's own empty space included. Item rows keep before / after / nested. Why: that is what the owner asked for, and it is the same drop a collapsed list already took.
- 2026-10-23 Unchanged on purpose: items dropped on a note, link or other kind of card, on an open container's empty space, or on a collapsed container still make a new list (SPEC "Dragging items"); only checklists take items. A checklist inside a container takes items the same way as a loose one. The phone's press-and-hold lift uses the same drop code as the grip, so it behaves the same.
- 2026-11-11 Fold arrow, darker (call): the arrow uses `--ink`, the strongest ink color. Why: `--ink-soft` is almost the same as `--ink-faint`, so no other existing color is meaningfully darker, and no new colors are allowed. Contrast against the card is 16.8:1 in light mode. The old hover rule was removed: the resting color is already `--ink`, so there is no stronger step to hover to.
- 2026-11-11 Fold arrow, bigger (call): the open arrow is drawn 13px wide at Normal (8.8px glyph, about 35% more than before) and the folded one 5.5px (about 38% more), with a 2.1 stroke set in the checklist CSS rather than in the shared chevron icon, so other chevrons are unchanged. It grows with the item buttons' step (`--bb`, capped at 190% like them); the 110% cap from the gap fix is removed.
- 2026-11-11 Fold arrow, room (call, recommended): a checklist that has a top-level parent item gets left padding of `12.78 * step - 10` px on its item list (about 2.8px at Normal, up to about 14px at 190%). Why: the bigger arrow needs about 12.8 units of room left of the row, more than the card's 12px padding at big sizes; this is the simplest fix. Other choices: always padding every list (a visible shift for every checklist), or capping the arrow's size (it stays small, the owner's complaint). What it shifts: that list's rows move right by that amount; the card title and lists with no parent are unchanged. Cost: the rows slide a few pixels when the first parent item is made or the last one removed. Sub-item arrows stay inside their 32px indent (about 19px spare at Normal, 7.7px at 400%).
- 2026-11-11 Fold arrow gap (call): the folded ▸ sits about 5.6px (times the step) from the box, the open ▾ exactly 4px; the ▸ is narrower and centered on the same spot, so I did not shift it to even them out.
- 2026-11-11 Fold arrow gap: measured (Chromium, every A− / A+ step, desktop and phone, light and dark; the svg path's box plus half its stroke against the tick box's border box). Before: the open arrow ended 2.75px (times the step, capped at 190%) from the tick box and the folded one 4px, a small gap beside the box's 1.5px border; and a top-level arrow stuck out of the card's left edge from 150% up (up to 4.6px outside at 190% and beyond). The tick box itself was not at fault: its left edge sits exactly on the row's inset at every step (its 7.5% nudge is vertical only, the box size and the phone `.tick` padding / margin cancel out).
- 2026-11-11 Fold arrow gap: the drawn arrow now ends 4px (times the arrow's step) short of the tick box's edge. Why: owner request, about 4px at Normal.
- 2026-11-11 Fold arrow size (call, recommended): the arrow's step is the item buttons' step held at 110% (`--bf` on `.item-fold`), not the 190% cap. Why: a top-level arrow sits in the card's 12px padding (13px with the border), and a 4px gap plus the open arrow's 6.5px width fit that only up to about 114%; letting the arrow and its gap grow further pushes it out of the card, which is what the owner saw clipped. The other choice, widening the card's left padding at big sizes, would shift every checklist's boxes and cut a 200px card's text room. Cost: above 110% the arrow stays a little over 11px wide while the tick box keeps growing.
- 2026-11-11 Fold arrow gap (call): the open ▾ gets exactly 4px and the folded ▸ about 5.25px (it is narrower and centered on the same spot); I did not shift the ▸ to even them out.
- 2026-11-21 Fold arrow press area (worker call, recommended option): "the collapse arrows" means the checklist parent-item fold arrows (`.item-fold`), on the board and in the Main checklist. Only the press area grows (an invisible `::after`, `--fold-hit`: at least 24px tall and 28px wide on a computer, WCAG 2.5.8), from the tick box's press area leftwards; the drawn arrow, its 32px indents and the layout stay. A top-level arrow's area stops at the card's (or Checklist panel's) padding edge, so there it is about 16px wide at Normal. The phone keeps its 44px area. The other collapse arrows were checked and left alone: the card and container collapse buttons are 28px or more, the Journal year / month / day headers are full-width rows about 26px tall or more, and the Completed panel has no foldable groups.
- 2026-11-21 Fold arrow gap (worker call, recommended option): "a bit more space between the collapse arrow and the parent checkbox" is a fixed 5px more (`--fold-extra-gap`), at every level and text size, so about 9px at Normal. The arrow moves left; for a top-level arrow the list's left room grows by the same 5px, so its tick boxes move 5px right and the arrow stays where it was (still lined up with the Main checklist's "Checklist" title). Fixed, not times the button step, so a sub-item's arrow still fits inside its 32px indent at the biggest sizes.
- 2026-11-11 Fold needs two (owner request: "for sub-checklist items, the collapse button should only be there if there are 2 or more items"): the arrow, the Ctrl+Up / Ctrl+Down fold and the fold itself apply only to a parent with 2 or more direct sub-items, at every level, top-level and nested alike. Counted by direct children, not by everything below. One rule in the model (`canFold`, used by `isFolded` and `toggleFold`), so display, search, copy, keys and drag all agree.
- 2026-11-11 Fold needs two (coordinator call 2): a parent with one sub-item that is saved as folded shows that sub-item and no arrow. The fold is treated as off for display only; saved data is not rewritten (the fold stays on the item, and survives sync, backups and undo as before).
- 2026-11-11 Fold needs two (coordinator call 3): when the parent later has a second sub-item, the arrow comes back with whatever fold is saved. A sub-item added with Tab, a drop or the connector still opens the parent, as it always has, so in those cases it comes back open. A fold is still dropped when its last sub-item goes (unchanged).
- 2026-11-11 Fold needs two (coordinator call 4): the list's extra left room (`has-fold`) applies only when some top-level parent has 2 or more sub-items (`hasFoldable`). Only top-level parents count, since only their arrows sit at the card's edge. Cost: a list's rows slide a few pixels when its first top-level parent gets its second sub-item, as they did before when the first sub-item came.
- 2026-11-11 Fold needs two (coordinator call 5): the done count (like 1/1) shows as it does now; only the arrow is affected.
- 2026-11-11 Fold needs two (call): existing tests and the arrow-measuring test that used one-sub-item parents were given a second sub-item, so they still test folding.
- 2026-11-09 Item folds (owner answers): a ▾ / ▸ arrow just left of a parent item's tick box, always shown on items with sub-items; a folded item keeps its "1/3" count; the fold is saved with the board, syncs, and is one undo step per fold or unfold; Collapse all / Expand all do not touch item folds.
- 2026-11-09 Saved shape (call, recommended): `folded: true` on the item, left out when open. Only the exact value `true` is read; anything else (false, "yes", 1, null, an object) is dropped and the item reads as open. A fold on an item with no sub-items is kept as saved (so the connector's exact round trip holds) but means nothing; the app drops it whenever it changes that list.
- 2026-11-09 No save version bump (call, recommended; put to the owner in the pull request): a copy of the app from before folds already drops unknown item fields, so it shows the item open and, if it saves, loses only the fold. A bump would instead make every older open tab refuse the boards and an older connector say "Safe to edit: no". Same reasoning as the earlier `hr` exemption. The saved-shape test lists it in `READ_SAFELY_ITEM_FIELDS`.
- 2026-11-09 A fold goes when its item loses its last sub-item (deleted, cut, dragged out, outdented, cleaned up): the store tidies it in the same undo step, only on lists that changed.
- 2026-11-09 What a fold hides (call, recommended): Up / Down, Enter, Delete-at-end joins, Ctrl+A in a list, Shift+click ranges, the Word-style highlight, the drop highlight and the phone's press-and-hold only see shown rows. Enter at the end of a folded item adds the new item after the group, at the same level. What acts on an item takes its hidden sub-items too: copy, cut, delete, drag, Move up / Move down, Download as text. Pasted copies keep their fold.
- 2026-11-09 Tab under a folded item, and dropping items into one, unfold it, so the moved items can be seen. Ticking a folded parent works as on an open one.
- 2026-11-09 Clean up still moves hidden ticked sub-items to Completed; the Completed panel ignores folds, and putting an item back opens every fold above it.
- 2026-11-09 Search (call, recommended): a match in hidden sub-items rings the folded item and the count says "in a folded item"; it never unfolds it, since Search only reads. A collapsed card or container around it wins, as before.
- 2026-11-09 Folding while typing in a sub-item it hides puts the cursor at the end of the folded item; selected items it hides are let go.
- 2026-11-09 Shortcut (call, recommended): Ctrl+Up folds and Ctrl+Down unfolds while typing in an item with sub-items (⌘ on a Mac), as in outliners such as Workflowy. With nothing to fold or unfold (already folded or open, no sub-items, view-only), the key is left to the browser (jump by paragraph; ⌘+Up / Down to the start or end on a Mac); outside text, Ctrl+arrows switch boards as before. Listed in the Keyboard shortcuts panel.
- 2026-11-09 Touch and motion (call, recommended): on a phone the arrow's press area is 44×44px, reaching left over the indent or the card's padding (on a top-level item it can reach about 30px past the card's left edge), never over the tick box. The arrow reuses the card arrow's 150ms turn, off with reduced motion and for keyboard use. On a view-only board it is disabled.
- 2026-11-09 Word-style highlights (review fix): a folded item the highlight starts partway into, or ends in, keeps its hidden sub-items in the list and they are not copied; folded items covered whole go with theirs. When the last item goes whole its hidden sub-items move up, as shown ones do. Joining onto a folded first item puts the joined sub-items after its hidden ones.
- 2026-11-09 The Claude connector (review fix): items it adds or moves under a folded item open every fold above them, so "Added …" can be seen in the app. Putting an item back from Completed does the same. A sync merge that adds a sub-item under an item folded on another device leaves the fold (the person chose it).
- 2026-11-09 On a phone a parent item's tick box is pressed from the box rightwards (28px), the 16px left of it being the arrow's; the rows above and below keep their tick boxes' full press areas where the arrow's reaches over them. The arrow grows with the A− / A+ step. On a view-only board Ctrl+Up / Ctrl+Down do what they did before.
- 2026-11-09 Known limit: after a Word-style highlight delete across a folded item, the "removed" count in the message counts shown rows only.
- 2026-11-09 Fixes after the owner's check of the pull request: Ctrl/⌘+Up and Down no longer block the browser's own use of the key when they have nothing to do. The empty-fold tidy-up now also runs in the shared list-edit function (`editItems`), which the Claude connector's adds, moves and deletes go through too; the store's tidy-up stays for app changes that replace a list another way. The arrow darkens on hover (its icon now takes the button's color). Search's "in a folded item" checks for the folded item itself, and finding the hidden sub-items of a selection is one walk down the list.
- 2026-11-09 Review of those fixes (medium): a connector move that changes nothing is still reported as nothing, even in a list holding a saved fold with no sub-items; the search ring on a folded item checks the match is in that list. Left alone: the store keeps its own tidy-up as a safety net for list changes that don't go through `editItems` (Clean up, sync merge), at the cost of a second quick walk of a changed list; the phone arrow's press area reaching past a top-level item's card edge (decided above); the cursor after an undone unfold (a separate job).
- 2026-11-02 Format bar pause: the bar waits 250ms after the highlight stops changing (mouse released, or keys stopped), the middle of the 200-300ms the owner allowed. Pressing a key restarts the wait.
- 2026-11-02 Format bar fade: opacity plus a 4px slide, 150ms ease-out (strong ease-out curve), on appearing only. No fade-out, so it never blocks clicks as it leaves. No animation with reduced motion.
- 2026-11-02 Format bar hides on typing, Backspace/Delete/Enter, Ctrl+X, Ctrl+C and Ctrl+V; Ctrl+B / Ctrl+I do not hide it. With checklist items picked (focus not in a text box), only Backspace, Delete, Ctrl+X/C/V, and letters or Enter over a word highlight hide it; a board shortcut letter like q does not. Clicking a bar button never hides it. After Ctrl+C it stays hidden until the highlight actually changes.
- 2026-11-02 Format bar placement: kept relative to the whole box (or the picked checklist items), which can never cover highlighted words. Above if there is room under the toolbar, else below; if neither fits (a very short window) it goes below, held inside the window, and never up over the toolbar.
- 2026-11-02 Phone and touch: no separate behavior. Highlights made by touch (long-press, selection handles) show the bar by the same "after the highlight stops changing" pause. The phone layout is otherwise unchanged.
- 2026-11-02 Alt+F10 shows the bar at once (skips the pause) and moves into it, as before.
- 2026-10-29 iPhone stand-in for the tick box tap check (job 223; worker calls): a new `iphone` Playwright project (iPhone 14 profile, WebKit) reuses the touch tests of `e2e/ticked-item-delete-key.spec.ts` (its "on a phone" group; the "on a touch screen" ones drive the desktop toolbar, which a phone layout hides) instead of copying them; GitHub's browser shards already install WebKit and run every project. Emulated WebKit is not a real iPhone keyboard, so a real-device check of the on-screen keyboard staying up is still the only full proof.
- 2026-10-29 Tick box tap on an iPhone (job 224; worker calls): emulated WebKit sent no click after a touch tap whose pointerdown was cancelled (the cancel that keeps the focus in the text, job 218), so the box never ticked. A finger tap that keeps the focus now ticks the box itself on the touchend, and cancels that touchend so the browser's own click (and its focus change) never follows, in every browser, so there is no second tick. Only a real tap ticks (review finding, high review): one finger, lifted within 10px of where it pressed and within half a second, so a slide, a pinch or a long press on the box ticks nothing, as no click would. A pen in a browser without touch events still goes through the click as before.
- 2026-11-11 Owner decision: a parent item's done-count ("0/2") never wraps onto a line of its own. Asked for a wider card or container, the owner chose instead (on the worker's recommendation) to let the count sit in the room the row already keeps free at its right end for the hover buttons, with no change to any width. Why: card and container widths are saved numbers set by the person, nothing grows to fit content, and changing them automatically would touch saving, undo and neighbors. The grip and trash can are drawn over the count while the pointer is on the row; a count wider than that room is not handled (it spills a little past the text into the card's padding).
- 2026-11-11 (job item-estimate-clock). Owner decision ("option A", for the place of an item's add-estimate clock that job saved-estimate-doneon left open): widen every item's hover strip by about 26px so a faint clock button sits with the drag grip and the trash can, opening the same estimate box notes use; it works on a phone too. Calls made while building, recommended options (nothing here touches saving, the connector or loading): (1) The clock is the strip's last button, at the card's edge, beyond the grip and the trash can, not next to the text: a parent's "0/2" count may reach into the strip (item-count-no-wrap), and the end nearest the text is where it lands. A sweep test over last-word lengths, at Normal and Huge text on desktop and phone, proves the count never covers the clock and never drops to its own line. The sweep only hits the worst case when a last word ends exactly at the text's edge, which passed on one machine's fonts and failed on the GitHub runner's (IBM Plex Sans there, a fallback font here), so a second test measures the room itself: the space between the text's right edge and the clock must hold a whole "0/12" count and its gap. On a phone (touch, under 600px) that room was 24px (the grip's strip) and the count needs about 35px, so it now grows with the text size (2.5 times the text's size, 40px at Normal; from 225% text up, with no clock, it stays 24px). Phone text is 16px narrower on every item at Normal text as a result (a leaf item can't be given a narrower strip than a parent: the text must wrap the same with or without a count). A count of 100 or more sub-items is wider and can still touch the clock; so can a count on a narrow window used with a mouse at Huge text. (2) The clock is a fixed 26px (not scaled by the text size step) and the strip is 26px wider on every item, with or without an estimate, so text wraps the same either way. (3) From the 225% text size up items get neither the clock nor the wider strip, as notes get no clock there. (4) On a touch screen the clock shows only while the item has the cursor, as the grip does, with a 44px press area that runs outward past the card's edge (the grip's now runs the other way, over the end of the row's text, since the clock sits beside it); on a touch screen that can also hover (tablet with a mouse) the grip's area stops at the trash can. (5) After Clear on an item the keyboard goes into the item's text (the pill is gone, so there is nothing else to return to). (6) No clock in view-only boards, published boards or exports (the same rule as the grip and trash can); they keep the wider strip so the text wraps the same as when editing. Selecting across items and deleting (`deleteRange` in `src/model/wordRange.ts`) now keeps an estimate that would otherwise have been dropped; it is not on the risky-file list and only keeps data, so the job stays low-risk. (7) Estimate box fixes, each reproduced by a failing test first: after Ctrl+Z inside the Minutes box the box follows the undone value; pressing the open pill again closes it and returns the keyboard to that pill; selecting from one item into the next and deleting keeps the upper item's estimate, else the lower's (the join rule). (8) The box's cost: each item's clock no longer subscribes to the box opening; the box marks its own button (aria-expanded) when it opens. While open it reads only the button's place, once a frame, when the board or view changes (a ResizeObserver tracks the box's own size), and redraws only if the place changed. (9) Removed the duplicate `MAX_MINUTES` constant in the brain dump parser; it uses `MAX_ESTIMATE` directly.
- 2026-11-11 Journal general notes, the old "add after this note" check: before the + moved to the top, adding a note refused when the note id passed in wasn't a general note. The add no longer takes an "insert after this note" id (a new note always goes first), so there is nothing to refuse and that check was not brought back. The id `addJournalNote` still takes only names the first note (the id "General notes" is created under when none is written) and the card the new one is placed below. A stale or unknown id passed to the add now only changes where the card sits on the Journal board (it falls back to the spot beside everything), never the list, so it can't hide or drop written notes; a new test (`+ given an id that is not a note…`) covers it. A mutation that lets the "General notes" note be added twice makes that test fail.
- 2026-11-11 Journal general notes, the default for typing: the store's `setJournalNotes` without a note id writes into the first note in the list. Since new notes go on top, that is now the newest "Untitled note" rather than "General notes". The panel always names the note, so no button or key relies on the default; it is kept as it is, documented in the code and pinned by a test.
- 2026-11-11 Journal general notes, the + moves to the top (owner request: "the + button to add a new note should be at the very top, and not within each general note"): the + is removed from every general note's header and there is one in a slim row above the first general note, at its right end, under the Formatting bar. It is a quiet icon button in the same style as before, with the tooltip and accessible name "Add a general note", the keyboard focus ring, and at least 44px tall on a phone. Trash, rename and fold stay as they were.
- 2026-11-11 Journal general notes, where the new note goes: because the + is at the top, the new note is added first in the list, above every other general note (matching the owner's request that a container's + adds at the top). It opens as before: named "Untitled note", the cursor in its name field with the name selected. All general notes are renumbered 1, 2, 3… as the existing add did, with no new saved field. Adding at the top moves every existing note down one, so each + now rewrites the `general` number of all of them (before, only the notes after the insertion point changed); nothing is lost or reordered, and the format stays version 4. On the Journal board itself the new card is placed on a free spot below the old first note; the list order is `general`, not where the cards sit.
- 2026-11-11 (owner request: "the default font size in the journal should be 14 and start there"; owner answers relayed by the crew coordinator: all normal Journal text, old and new, shows at 14; the Size box shows 14; words sized on purpose keep their size; headings keep their size; the step list is unchanged). (1) Saving (owner pick "B"): a saved 16 now means a size of its own, which an older copy of the app would read as plain and drop on its next save, so `WORKSPACE_VERSION` went from 4 to 5 (versions 3 and 4 still load unchanged). An older copy refuses version 5 boards ("saved by a newer version… Reload") and never saves over them; the Claude connector went to 1.6.0, and an older one makes no edits ("Safe to edit: no") until the new one is installed. Nothing saved is rewritten on loading. (2) A plain heading's Size box reads 16 (owner confirmed). (3) Sizes are measured against the line's own plain size: 14 in a normal line (paragraph or list item), 16 in a heading. In a normal line a size is drawn at its px (so every size saved before keeps its exact px); in a heading it is drawn as a share of 16, as before, so headings and sized heading words look exactly as before. (4) The line's plain size means no size: choosing 14 in a normal line, or 16 in a heading, saves the words plain. A 14 saved before is kept as it is (not rewritten), and looks the same as plain; the box and the "mixed" check go by the px shown, so plain and a saved 14 are not "mixed". (5) The Journal's text box stays 16px and each normal line is drawn at 0.875em (14px), so headings keep 1.45em and 1.2em of 16px, dividers are unchanged, and on a phone the box itself stays 16px (iOS Safari zooms in on smaller text boxes). (6) The old Small and Large keep their exact look (13.6px and 20.8px in a normal line, 0.85 and 1.3 of a heading) and show as 14 and 21, as before. (7) Checklist items pasted into the Journal: Small is now 12px (one step under the 14px normal text; it was 14, which is now normal), Large stays 20px.
