Journal lines pasted into a checklist are Small or Large when their size is under or over the line's plain size (14px, or 16px in a heading); so a 16 in a normal line now pastes as Large, a saved 14 as a normal item, and an old Small word (13.6px) still as Small.
- 2026-11-12 Panel widths: Habits now opens 420px wide (`habits: PANEL_DEFAULT.week`), so the Calendar (Week), the Journal and Habits all open the same. Owner decision (option A), after the request "the default journal width should be the same as calendar and tracking daily habits": the Journal was already 420px like the Calendar's Week view, but Habits was 300px. Widths people already saved on their device are kept; the phone is unchanged (full screen).
- 2026-11-11 Journal divider keys (owner request, "change it to be -- instead of ---", "alt + I + R like Google Docs"): only the typing trigger changes. A divider's plain text stays the line `---`, so the board card, search, Download as text, copying and the Claude connector are unchanged, and there is no save-format change (call, from the coordinator).
- 2026-11-11 `---` + Enter no longer makes a divider, and neither does Enter at the end of an old plain `---` line (call). A typed `---` and an old plain `---` line are the same saved line, so the old rule could not be kept without keeping the typed one; it was dropped. Old `---` lines stay words, nothing is lost.
- 2026-11-11 Alt+I, then R (call, from the coordinator): a sequence, not a chord. R must come within 1.5 seconds, Alt held or released; any other key cancels it, pressing a modifier does not. Matched on the key's place (KeyI, KeyR), never the character, so other layouts and a Mac (Option+I is a dead key) work; both keys are stopped. It only acts inside a Journal note, never on the board behind. It does what the Divider button does, as one Ctrl+Z step. Listed in the Keyboard shortcuts panel and the Divider button's tooltip.
- 2026-10-31 Journal divider (owner decision, "Button + type ---"): a thin gray line across the note from the Divider button in the Formatting bar or from `---` + Enter on an empty line. Backspace or Delete next to it removes it, and Ctrl+Z undoes that. It is saved, synced, kept in Version history and read by the connector. Plain-text and markdown exports show it as `---`.
- 2026-10-31 Divider, how it is saved (call, recommended): a formatting line of type `hr` whose words are `---`. The note's plain text keeps the line `---`, so the board card, search, Download as text, Version history previews and the connector (which reads the plain text) need no new code. No new save version (it stays 4). An older copy of the app reads an unknown line type as a plain line and keeps its words and the rest of the note's formatting, so an old tab shows the words `---`. If that tab saves, the divider stays those words; nothing else is lost. A version bump would instead have made old tabs show "Your board couldn't be loaded" (as version 4 did). The saved-shape guard test (`persist-roundtrip.test.ts`) lists `hr` as a value older copies read safely, so any other new value still needs a version bump.
- 2026-10-31 Divider: a Claude connector installed before this change (version 1.4.0 or older) refuses to make any edits while any journal holds a divider ("data this connector doesn't know"), because its round-trip check covers the whole workspace and sees the new line type. Nothing is changed or lost. It works again once the newest connector (version 1.5.0, built with `npm run mcp:pack`) is installed (check of PR #226).
- 2026-10-31 Divider button placement (call, recommended): it goes after the current line. On an empty line, that line becomes the divider. A selection is replaced. The cursor never rests on a divider: typing, Enter, Style and the list buttons skip it.
- 2026-10-31 `---` + Enter only converts a plain line, not a heading or a list item (call, recommended). In those, `---` is likely meant as words.
- 2026-10-31 Backspace on a list item right after a divider outdents the item first, as Backspace at the start of an item always does. It removes the divider only once the line is plain (call, recommended).
- 2026-10-31 Dividers are not counted in the Journal's word count. A plain `---` line written before is not counted either (call, recommended).
- 2026-10-31 Download as text writes a divider as a raw `---` line, as the owner asked. In Markdown viewers, a line directly above it can show as a heading (Markdown's own rule). Left alone, because a plain `---` line in a note already exported that way.
- 2026-10-31 Divider, after the high review: Enter at the end of a plain `---` line written before the divider existed also makes it a divider (call, recommended). That line looks exactly as if it had just been typed, and the owner's rule is "`---` on an empty line, then Enter". Opening the note never changes it. Ctrl+Z puts it back as words.
- 2026-10-31 Divider: Backspace right after pressing Divider removes the line but leaves the new empty line below it, like deleting one character (call, recommended). Ctrl+Z undoes the whole button press in one step. A folded day's first line skips a divider.
- 2026-11-21 Journal fold all (worker's recommended call, from the brief): the button reads "Unfold all general notes" only when every general note is folded; otherwise "Fold all general notes". Icon: the toolbar's collapse-all arrows (in when it folds, out when it opens). Not an undo step (view state).
- 2026-11-21 Journal rearranging (worker's calls): the grip sits just left of the trash, only while there are two or more notes, and is not in the Tab order (the keyboard uses Alt+Up / Alt+Down on the note's arrow or name button). On a phone the grip itself is the touch handle (no long-press on the header), so a long press keeps its usual meaning and scrolling the panel still works everywhere else. A drag of under 4px is a press and moves nothing. The others slide aside in 200ms ease-out (none with reduced motion); the dropped note takes its place at once. The sheets' places are read when the drag starts, so scrolling the panel mid-drag is not followed. A move numbers every general note 1, 2, 3… again (as add and rename do) and changes nothing else; the announcement is "Moved “name” to place 2 of 3".
- 2026-10-29 Journal general notes, how they are recognized: by the note's first line being exactly "General notes" on the Journal board, the same way days are found by their date line. No new saved field, so sync, import, copy and older copies of the app all carry it as an ordinary note, and a date line never reads "General notes", so it is never taken for a day. If the board holds two (a copy, say), the one with the smaller id is shown everywhere; the other stays an ordinary note.
- 2026-10-29 Journal general notes and the hidden rule: the Journal board stays hidden from the Boards menu while it holds only dated notes plus one general notes card. A second "General notes" card, or any other card, lists it as before.
- 2026-10-29 Journal general notes are made lazily, on the first typing that leaves text or formatting, never on opening the panel. That typing and the new card are one undo step on the Journal board, so one Ctrl+Z removes both.
- 2026-10-29 Journal general notes placement: the card goes to the right of everything on the Journal board, level with its top, on a free spot. Today's note ignores the general notes card when it works out "straight above, level with the leftmost", so it lands exactly where it did before.
- 2026-10-29 Journal general notes are not part of the groups, entry counts, word counts or the folded timeline; search, Download as text and Version history treat them as the ordinary note they are.
- 2026-10-29 Journal general notes fold: open by default and open again every time the panel opens, even if folded last time (view state, not saved or synced). Today's note keeps the cursor when the panel opens.
- 2026-10-29 Journal general notes look: a quieter sheet above today's page (card background, thin edge, no shadow), a 15px semibold "General notes" header button with the group headers' arrow, `aria-expanded`, a focus ring, and at least 44px tall on a phone; the empty text box shows "Notes to keep, not tied to a day…".
- 2026-10-29 Journal general notes, known limits (from the high review, left as they are): a note the owner already wrote on the Journal board whose first line is exactly "General notes" becomes the panel's general notes (if the board holds nothing else but days, it now leaves the Boards menu, and the first edit in the panel adds a blank line under that first line); nothing is lost. A copy of the general notes card made on the Journal board (Ctrl+D, paste) may take over from the original, since the smaller id wins and ids are random; the other stays an ordinary note with its text. Both are rare, and a timestamp field would be a new saved field for little gain.
- 2026-11-10 Several Journal general notes, how they are saved (worker's recommended call, put to the owner in the pull request): each is a note on the Journal board whose first line is its name, as before, and a new optional `general` number gives its place in the list. The one "General notes" note saved before this has no number, is found by its first line as before, and is listed first, so boards already saved load unchanged. No format version bump (as with folding checklist items): an older copy of the app drops `general`, so it shows only the note still named "General notes" and the others become ordinary notes on the Journal board, with their words kept (that board then shows in the Boards menu there). An older connector won't edit until updated. Bumping the version would instead make older copies show "Your board couldn't be loaded".
- 2026-11-10 Several Journal general notes: a renamed note's name is its first line, so search, Download as text, Version history and the connector all show it by name. Spaces and line breaks in a typed name become single spaces, and a name is at most 200 characters.
- 2026-11-10 Several Journal general notes: the trash shows only while there are two or more (the last has none). Delete has no question, only the Undo message, as the owner asked. After a delete the keyboard goes to the panel, so Ctrl+Z works straight away.
- 2026-11-10 Several Journal general notes: each note's fold is view state, open every time the panel opens, as before. Adding, renaming and deleting are one undo step each on the Journal board. Typing in the name field is undone by the browser until it is saved.
- 2026-11-10 Several Journal general notes: a new note is placed on the Journal board just below the note it was added under (or beside everything, if that spot isn't known). It never counts as a day and is never taken for today's note, whatever its name.
- 2026-11-10 Several Journal general notes, from the high review: a copy of a general note (Ctrl+D, copy and paste) is an ordinary note, not another general note. Once any note has a place, another note named "General notes" stays an ordinary note, so a stray copy can't jump into the list. The note not yet written is shown under the id it will get, so the cursor stays in it on the first letter typed.
- 2026-11-10 Several Journal general notes, after an older copy of the app (follow-up to the #246 review, worker's recommended call): an older copy drops each note's place. Each device now remembers, in its own browser, the card ids of the general notes it last saw with places (ids only, never words). When a board comes back with no places at all, those notes are listed again in that order, and the next add, rename or delete in the panel writes the places back. Nothing is changed or deleted when the panel only opens. Options not taken: treating every plain note on the Journal board as a general note (it would pull notes the owner put there by hand into the panel), or a new saved field the older copy keeps (there is none it keeps).
- 2026-11-10 Several Journal general notes, date-like names: a general note named like a date line ("Tue 10 Nov 2026") is never taken for that day, with its place or remembered. A copy of such a note keeps its place, so it shows as another general note, not as a day. Other copies are ordinary notes, as before.
- 2026-11-10 Several Journal general notes, name field: the Enter that picks a character in an input method doesn't save (the same check as checklist items). If a note goes while its name is typed (undo, sync), the typed name is kept and comes back with the cursor when the note does. A deleted note's places are renumbered, so the list stays 1, 2, 3.
- 2026-11-10 Several Journal general notes, after the high review of the follow-up (worker's recommended calls): the notes a device remembers are listed after those with a place, even when another device has placed only some; a deleted note's id stays remembered, so Undo brings it back in its old place in the list. The list is read from the browser each time, so two tabs agree. A name field that comes back (redo) doesn't take the cursor from a field outside the panel. Left as known limits: a copy of a remembered note that has no place yet and is named like a date line becomes a day (it is a plain note on the board then); the Boards menu still shows the Journal board on a device that only remembers its notes.
- 2026-11-10 Several Journal general notes, re-check of #246 (worker's calls): deleting the account also clears the general notes this device remembers (they are ids of that person's notes). The panel reads that list again when another tab changes it. A name saved from the panel's name field is cut to 200 characters (counted in characters, so an emoji isn't split), so a paste can't save a longer one; a longer first line typed on the Journal board itself is kept, and leaving the field unchanged never cuts it. The remembered list is one per browser, not per account (ids only): signing in as someone else on the same device leaves it, and it only matters if an older app has dropped the places.
- 2026-11-21 Journal list markers are drawn in the note's text color (they inherit the line's color, which is --ink), never gray, for every list style and level. A highlight sits on the words, not the line, so it never colors the marker. (Worker's call, as recommended.)
- 2026-11-22 Typed lists on list items (owner decision, "yes follow google docs way"): typing `1. `, `1) `, `- ` or `* ` at the very start of a list item's words switches that item to that kind (bulleted, numbered 1., numbered 1)) and keeps its indent level. The typed characters are removed.
- 2026-11-22 Typed lists on list items (worker calls, recommended options from the coordinator): only the item typed on switches, not the items around it; its number then follows the existing numbering rules for its neighbors; it is one Ctrl+Z step (Ctrl+Z right after brings back the typed characters as words); it works the same in general notes and day entries; the saved format is unchanged (no new version number).
- 2026-11-22 Typed lists on list items (worker call): typing the kind the item already has (`- ` or `* ` on a bulleted item, `1. ` on a 1. item, `1) ` on a 1) item) keeps the characters as words. What Google Docs does there could not be checked from this session, so the coordinator's fallback was used.
- 2026-11-20 (call, recommended) A list item's bullet or number takes the size of the item's first character (as Google Docs does), not the biggest or the line's own size. Why: it is the simplest rule to predict, and a plain item stays at 14. It is drawn only (a style on the rendered line, nothing saved), so saved notes and older copies of the app are unaffected.
- 2026-11-20 (call, recommended) The marker now sits in the item's first line (inline) instead of floating over it, so it stays on the text's baseline at any size. Its width and indent are the same as before.
- 2026-10-29 (owner: "Merge + fix wording" for #211): the spec no longer says both devices' General notes survive a sync; the Journal board syncs whole, so the last save wins, as for day notes, and Version history may bring the other back.
- 2026-11-01 (owner request: pasting into the Journal should keep the copied text size and the indents of a list; the owner's answers were relayed by the crew coordinator; the calls below were made by the worker under rule 4's exception). (1) The app's own copies are recognized by a hidden clipboard type written during the copy event (`application/x-note-board-journal` for Journal lines; the existing `application/x-note-board-items` for checklist items, whose value is now the items as JSON instead of `1`). The formatted content travels as JSON in the type's value, so it also works across browser tabs; it is read back with `parseRich` / a checked reader, and anything damaged, or the old value `1`, means no marker, so the plain text is pasted. The plain-text clipboard is unchanged. Nothing new is saved (the Journal stays at version 4). (2) Ticked checklist items pasted into the Journal arrive as plain words with no tick (the recommendation; the Journal has no ticks). (3) Headings pasted into a checklist become bold items, Heading 1 also Large; blank lines and dividers are skipped. (4) Item sizes map to Journal sizes Small = 14 px and Large = 20 px (both in the size box's list), and back: under 16 px is Small, over 16 px is Large. (5) A Journal line's looks become the item's look only when every letter of the line has them; underline, strikethrough and highlight are dropped in a checklist, which has none. (6) A copy's first line stays a list item or heading only when the selection starts at the start of that line; otherwise it is plain words, as Google Docs does. (7) Several pasted lines join the first to the words before the cursor and the last to the words after it, as Google Docs does; a divider is never pasted into, it goes beside the line. (8) Journal lines paste into a checklist only while typing in an item (as #228 does for items); with items highlighted, Ctrl+V keeps using the in-app item copy. (9) A rich paste is one Ctrl+Z step of its own (like a formatting change); typed text and plain pastes merge into typing steps as before. (10) Items copied across several lists still carry no marker (as in #228). Items pasted from checklist to checklist still use this tab's own copy; only the Journal paste reads the JSON, so it works from another tab.
- 2026-10-30 (owner request: Journal size picker, "Number box, like Google Docs"; high-risk pull request, waiting for the owner; calls made by the worker under rule 4's exception). (1) A run's `size` is saved as a number of px; any number from 10 to 36 is kept on loading (other numbers, strings like "20" and anything else are dropped, as unknown looks were); the old `'small'` and `'large'` are still read and kept as they are, not rewritten, so boards already saved load and save back exactly as before and keep their look (0.85em and 1.3em, shown in the box as 14 and 21; − and + step from the number shown, so Small goes to 16 or 12 and Large to 24 or 20). A saved 16 is dropped on loading, as choosing 16 does. (2) The saved shape changed (an older app's loader keeps only 'small' / 'large' and would silently drop a number, then save that loss), so `WORKSPACE_VERSION` went from 3 to 4, as the rule from review 137-A M1 requires; version 3 saves still load. An older copy of the app refuses version 4 boards ("saved by a newer version", never saves over them), and an older Claude connector can neither read nor change the person's own boards ("Safe to edit: no") until the newest `busyants.mcpb` is installed (it is built by hand with `npm run mcp:pack`). (3) A size is drawn as a share of the line's size (`size / 16` em), like Small and Large were, so in a heading 16 is the heading's usual size; the box shows the number chosen, not the heading's px. (4) Choosing 16 takes the size off (no mark), so plain text stays saved plain. (5) − and + on a selection of more than one size step each part from its own size (as Google Docs does); with nothing selected they set the size for what is typed next, like the other looks. (6) The number can't be typed into: a typed box would take the cursor out of the note and needs a combobox; the list and − / + cover every size. (7) Card and container text sizes (Small / Normal / Large in "Text formatting") are unchanged. (8) The connector (version 1.4.0) still edits boards saved as version 3 and saves them as version 4, and a note of changes held back while the account was being deleted elsewhere (review 181-A H1) is worked out as if saved by version 3, so a note left before this update still matches the same boards (code review).
- 2026-10-30 (owner request, same pull request: "once a given size is selected, when a new list or item is entered, the nextline should continue to keep the previous size"; calls made by the worker). (1) Enter (and Shift+Enter) carries the size at the cursor onto the new line when that line starts empty: what is typed next is in that size, in a paragraph or a list. Enter in the middle of a line needed nothing new: the words moved down keep their own size. (2) Only the size carries. Bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, font and highlight did not carry onto an empty new line before and still don't (the request named the size only; carrying them all, as Google Docs does, is a choice left to the owner). (3) Enter on an empty line keeps the size it had (chosen there or carried from the line above); Enter on an empty list item, which ends the list, keeps it too. (4) After a Heading 1 or 2 the new line is a normal paragraph at 16, unless the heading's words were given a size, which then carries like any other. (5) The carried size waits for the next letters like a size chosen with nothing selected: clicking elsewhere drops it, and an empty line is saved with no size.

- 2026-11-20 Speed on big boards (job perf-fewer-elements): the hover-only icons on checklist rows (Move up / down, trash, grip, estimate clock) and on card and container headers (collapse arrow, trash, estimate clock, resize corner) are drawn by CSS (a mask on the button's ::after) instead of inline SVG, rather than adding or removing the buttons as the pointer moves. Why: it halves the board's elements with no change to look, hit areas, tab order or touch behavior, and no extra re-rendering on hover. The drawings are hand copies of the ones in icons.tsx (a `simple:` note there). The item fold arrow, the container's add button and the comment chip keep their SVGs (few of them).
- 2026-11-21 Big boards (worker calls, job perf-offscreen): only loose cards and containers are left undrawn when far (a card in a container goes with its container); "far" is more than one screen beyond what is shown on every side, so panning never shows a card before it is drawn. The browser's `content-visibility: auto` is used, only on far blocks, not `hidden`, so far text stays in the page for Search, the browser's find, Alt+arrows and screen readers; applying it to every card would clip menus and edges of cards on screen. A far block is drawn again when it or a card in it changes, when the text size, view-only or phone layout changes, when a web font arrives, and before a search jump or a picture/PDF download. A far block's height changing for another reason (another board's name in a board card, a picture loading in a far link card) is measured once it comes near, a screen before it shows. Heights are never recorded while a block is far.
- 2026-11-21 Big boards (worker call): the first pass that marks far blocks runs just after the board is first shown, not before, so the board appears as fast as before; it costs a short moment of work right after.
- 2026-11-21 Panning a big board (job perf-paint-chunks, worker calls, recommended options): a faded-out hover-only button keeps its box but stops drawing its icon (`visibility: hidden` on its `::after`, inside `@media (hover: hover)`), and the Move up / down pill drops its shadow while hidden. The icon is held for the 120ms fade-out with a visibility transition, so the fade looks the same. Visibility, not `display`, because a `display` transition does not hold in every browser. The collapse arrow is left out (it has its own turning transition on its icon), and touch screens keep the old rules.
- 2026-11-21 Panning a big board: not done, because measured or seen not to be worth it: placing the checklist tick box with a margin instead of `translate` (a small gain, but the box edges render a pixel differently); `will-change` on the board (moves the cost to the compositor and blurs zoomed text, as in closed PR #328); dropping the hidden buttons' shadows or opacity (no gain).

- 2026-10-23 (owner request, job 198: on a phone, tapping into a card or checklist puts it near the top, clear of the keyboard; my calls, recommended option per the owner rule) The block's top goes 12px below the top bar (the same margin the keyboard rule uses). It is done in the one place that already runs when the cursor comes into a block from outside (the phone's fill-the-width step, SPEC "Lists fill the screen on a phone"), which now puts the block's top at that spot instead of keeping the tapped line at its height. So the zoom is whatever that step gives (the block fills the screen's width, as before, rather than "unchanged"), and the left edge is already at the margin, so there is no separate sideways rule. The block is the card tapped, or the container when its title is tapped; for a card inside a container, the card goes to the top while the container sets the width.
- 2026-10-23 The board slides there in 200ms (`--ease-out`, a CSS transition on the board's `transform` switched on only for that move by `data-glide` on the page element; none with reduced motion). The keyboard rule (ItemBar) waits for the slide to end before measuring, then moves the board just enough and at once as before, so a card taller than the room above the keyboard keeps its top at the top unless that hides the line being typed in, and the line wins. A touch on the board, or the next tap into a card, ends a slide at once at its final place, so a swipe is never held back and the next move measures real positions (code review).
- 2026-10-23 Only coming into a card or container from outside moves the board (as the fill-the-width step already did): moving between the items, title and note of the same card, or between the cards of one container, keeps today's just-enough behavior. A card made with + goes to the top too (it is a cursor coming in from outside), so adding several cards in a row leaves each earlier one above the screen; tests that made two cards and tapped the first now slide the board down first. It follows the phone layout (under 600px), as the keyboard rule and the fill-the-width step do, not "touch" separately; a narrow window used with a mouse behaves the same.
- 2026-10-23 Left alone (code review): on a very tall card the tapped item can sit below the keyboard for the length of the slide before the keyboard rule brings it back into view (the owner's recommended order: top first, the typed line wins); on iPhone Safari the page's scroll offset is read when the cursor comes in, before the keyboard moves it, and the keyboard rule's later check corrects any difference. Browser tests that made two cards and tapped the first, or tapped the empty board at the top, were changed for the new place of new cards (they slide the board or tap lower, and the multi-move test presses Escape and Fit to screen instead of clicking at the top and zooming out twice).
- 2026-10-23 (job 197, owner report "mobile bug with unload dishwasher"; recommended options, rule 4 exception). (1) **Holding the text being typed in** (the field that has the cursor) never lifts an item or picks up a card, on any touch screen, phone or tablet: the hold belongs to the phone's own cursor and selection. A swipe on it still moves the board, and a tap still narrows a bigger selection. Holding an item that isn't being typed in still lifts it (job 114). (2) **A lost finger ends its gesture as cancelled**, never as a drop: a waiting hold, an item lift or grip drag, a card drag and a resize each end the way a cancelled touch does, so nothing moves, when a new first finger touches the screen (or the same pointer comes down again), when the phone takes every touch over (a touchcancel with no finger left), or when the page is hidden. Losing focus alone (an iframe, a system overlay) does not end them. A second finger does not either, so a pinch still works; if an iPhone ever counts the lost finger as still down, the next touch is a second finger and only hiding the page ends it (noted in the code as a known limit). One helper, `onLostFinger` in `touchHold.ts`, used by `holdOrPan`, `trackItemDrag`, `trackDrag` and `beginResize`. (3) **Low risk**: no saving, sync, undo or deleting code changes, and the stuck lift never removed the item from the board (it was hidden while lifted, and saved all along), so a reload already brought it back.
- 2026-10-23 (owner request, job 199: on a phone, tapping into a card zooms in so the text is not too small; owner pick "text at least 16px"; my calls, recommended option per the owner rule) The zoom used when the cursor comes into a block from outside is now the larger of the fill-the-width zoom and the "readable" zoom, 16px divided by the block's base text size on the phone (14px times the A− / A+ step, never under 16px: 100% at Small and Normal, less at bigger steps), within 30%–250%. A per-text size (Small, Large) inside a card is ignored; the base size is the one the page's 16px phone minimum already uses (`phoneReadableZoom` in `src/model/font.ts`).
- 2026-10-23 When the readable zoom wins (a wide card) and the person is already zoomed in further, their zoom is kept rather than zoomed out. A narrow card that fills the width at a bigger zoom than that is unchanged (it can still zoom out to fit, as before).
- 2026-10-23 A wide card's left edge goes to the 16px margin (the existing rule); keeping the typed line in view sideways is left to the keyboard rule that already does it (ItemBar, "between the sides, 12px to spare"), which a new browser test covers, so there is no second sideways rule.
- 2026-11-21, owner request: "no need to ask me to publish going forward, can go do so without my permission". Owner picked "Publish only": the crew coordinator moves `main` up to `build/v1` once "All tests" passes there, without asking; a `high-risk` pull request still waits for the owner's yes before it is merged. CLAUDE.md rule 9 updated.
- 2026-10-23 A list made from dragged-out items copies the source list's own width (`w`) only when the source is loose on the board. A source inside a container, or at the default width, gives the default width (`w: null`). Why: a container's lists have no width of their own (the container sets it), and the code has no clean way to read a rendered width in the model; copying a stale saved width would be wrong. Upgrade if the owner wants a container's width carried over.
- 2026-10-23 A new list made at the end of a container always has `w: null`, so a later drag out of the container does not bring back an old width. Dropping items onto an existing list never changes that list's width.
- 2026-11-10 (owner request, "yes re-tick": removing the blank sub-item that unticked a parent should tick it again; calls made by the worker under rule 4's exception). (1) **One shared place:** `removeItemInPlace` now says which item the removed one was nested in, and a new `tickCompleteParents` ticks any item left with sub-items that are all ticked, going up while each next item is complete too, the same rule as `setItemsDone` uses for the last open sub-item. `removeEmptyItem` (Backspace) and `deleteItems` (the trash can, Delete on selected items and on a ticked item) both use it, in the one commit of the removal, so one undo restores both. (2) **No sub-items left:** the parent keeps its tick as it is. (3) **The "last open item" guard:** Backspace's refusal to delete a list's last open top-level item is judged before the re-tick, otherwise Backspace on the blank sub-item in a one-parent list (the very case asked for) would do nothing. The list can end up fully ticked, with the cursor in the sub-item above. (4) **Left alone:** cut (`removeExactly`) and the highlighted-words delete (`wordRange`), because the latter moves sub-items and changes ticks after removing, so ticking inside it would be done too early; they keep the old behavior. Say if cut should re-tick too. (5) Any removed open item re-ticks the parent when the rest are ticked, not only blank ones. Removing a ticked item does not: a parent the owner unticked by hand, with every sub-item ticked, would otherwise tick itself when an unrelated ticked sibling is trashed (found in review).
- 2026-11-23 (my call): Gave the central Escape stack four priority tiers (`capture` > `modal` > `overlay` > `panel`), with LIFO order within each tier, so overlays opened before a side panel (such as the Find bar or Format bar) still take Escape before persistent panels without CSS-class sniffing.
- 2026-11-11 (job saved-estimate-doneon: an optional time estimate on notes and checklist items, and the day a checklist item was ticked; the job, as given by the crew coordinator, asked for the version bump and connector 1.7.0; the rest are calls made while building, recommended options, and the pull request waits for the owner). (1) Saving: `WORKSPACE_VERSION` went from 5 to 6, as the job asked, because an older copy of the app would drop the new fields on its next save; versions 3 to 5 still load unchanged, an older copy refuses version 6 ("saved by a newer version… Reload") and never saves over it, and the Claude connector went to 1.7.0 (an older one says "Safe to edit: no"). (2) An estimate is whole minutes from 1 to 1440, the same cap as a brain dump's minutes; a loaded or imported value outside that, or a `doneOn` that is not a real "YYYY-MM-DD" day, is dropped and the rest of the item kept. (3) A `doneOn` left on an open item (written by hand or by another tool) is kept on load but counts for nothing; it is only read while the item is ticked. (4) The day is this device's local date, made where the tick happens (the app, or the connector's computer). Every way of ticking stamps it, including a parent ticked by its last open sub-item; a parent ticked again because its open sub-items were removed gets no day (a stale day left on it is removed), and ticks in the Habits panel get none. (5) Enter splitting an item keeps the estimate on the first half only, and a ticked item's day on both halves; joining two items with Backspace or Delete keeps the upper item's estimate, or the lower one's when the upper has none. (6) The connector sets a note's estimate only when adding it (`add_note`), since it can't edit notes. (7) Look and placement (owner may want to see it): a gray pill (the app's hover color, 12px, tabular numbers) at the end of an item's first line, before the hover buttons, and in a note's header; with no estimate a note shows a faint clock only on hover or keyboard focus. Items have no add clock (asked of the owner): a clock over the end of an item's text broke Shift+click and drag selection there (the end of an item's text is kept clear, 2026-10-22), one in the row's flow on the item being typed in reflowed the text mid-drag and can't be reached by Tab (Tab nests the item), and widening the hover strip would narrow every item's text; an item's estimate comes from the connector until the owner picks a place, and its pill changes or clears it. On a touch screen a note's clock leaves a 10px gap before collapse, whose 44px press area fills it; on a phone it shows only on the selected note (like collapse and delete, job 113); the clock keeps its drawn 26px as its press area (a wider one would reach over the comment chip before it). After Clear on an item the keyboard goes into the item's text. The picker is a small box under the pill with quick choices 15m, 30m, 45m, 1h, 2h, a Minutes box, Set and Clear; it fades in over 120ms (none with reduced motion), and closes on Escape, a press outside it, or focus leaving it. A collapsed note has no clock; from 225% text up no note has the clock, a collapsed note has no pill, and an open note's pill comes after its two buttons: at 400% the clock pushed the trash button off a 200px note.
- 2026-11-11 (call, recommended) While this person's own leave of a shared board is on its way, the server saying they are off it is that same leave: no second leave is sent and no "no longer shared with you" note shows. "Was deleted by the person who shared it" still shows if the owner deletes it meanwhile, and a leave that fails still says "That didn't work" as before. A list of their shared boards sent before the leave doesn't open the board again; once the server's list has stopped naming it, or they open its link here, it is treated as usual (so being added back on another device still brings it here). Why: a flaky browser test showed join, leave, leave; this is the smallest fix that keeps every note the spec promises.
- 2026-11-11 (owner decision on pull request #269) A board whose own leave is still on its way isn't closed by the server's list of shared boards leaving it out; it waits for the leave, so a leave that fails changes nothing (the spec's "if it doesn't work nothing changes"). Every leave the shared-boards code sends (the Share panel, a damaged link, the one retry after a failed leave, the clean-up after being removed or the board being deleted) goes through one place. The person's own leave is sent once; the one retry is for a leave whose boards have already gone from here (as before, plus a damaged link's), and no retry goes out after signing out. The clean-up joins their own leave if one is on its way and is otherwise sent once, as before.
- 2026-11-11 (call, recommended, from the code review) Opening a board's link right after clicking Leave is not made to wait for the leave (a leave can wait offline for the connection); as before, that leave can still take the board away once it answers.
- 2026-11-11 (call, recommended, from the code review) The clean-up leave after someone is removed (or the board is deleted) is not treated as their own leave, since that would hide a later "no longer shared with you" note or keep a board they are added back to from coming back. It is sent as before, and an out-of-date list that still names the board can show that note a second time, as before this change. Why: guarding that reliably needs its own job (see pull request #269).
- 2026-11-12 (sign-out-confirm; calls made by the worker under rule 4's exception) Sign out reuses the app's existing question card (`ConfirmDialog`, the same one Delete board and Leave board use), not `window.confirm` and not a new dialog. Wording: title "Sign out?", body "Your boards stay saved online. You can sign in again any time.", buttons "Sign out" and "Cancel". When the save note by the zoom control says anything but Saved (Saving…, offline, "Couldn't save online", or held while an account is deleted elsewhere), the body instead says "Some of your latest changes haven’t been saved online yet, so they may be missing when you sign in again." (code review: the reassurance would be false just when a warning matters). Read from the existing save note only, so `session.ts` stays untouched; the buttons are the same.
- 2026-11-12 (sign-out-confirm) The question is asked in one place, `App.tsx`, and handed to the toolbar button, the folded ⋯ row and the phone ⋯ row, so all three behave the same. The sign-in screen's Sign out buttons (no access, couldn't be loaded) are not wrapped and still sign out at once. `src/sync/session.ts` and `firebase.ts` are untouched.
- 2026-11-12 (sign-out-confirm) Like every question in the app, it opens with Cancel focused (so a stray Enter never confirms); Tab then Enter, a click, or Space on Sign out confirms. Not changed for this one question.
- 2026-11-12 (sign-out-confirm) From the folded ⋯ menu the menu closes as before and focus goes back to the ⋯ button (the row it was pressed on goes away with the menu), the same as Escape does there. On the phone the ⋯ panel stays open behind the question and focus returns to the Sign out row.
- 2026-11-12 (sign-out-confirm) The "error screens still sign out at once" check is a unit test (happy-dom), not a browser test: those screens only show on the published build with a real Firebase account, which the browser tests can't sign in to.
- 2026-11-12 (sign-out-confirm, code review) "Saving…" gets the warning too, not the reassurance: signing out sends the waiting changes but does not wait for them to arrive. A board with no save note at all (the demo page) isn't synced online, so it gets the warning as well. Only "Saved" says "Your boards stay saved online".
- 2026-11-15 (owner approved the fix from the week's code review) The Sort with AI labels are dropped by the sign-in watcher the same way as the AI key (`dropAiLabelsUnlessFor` in `src/model/aiLabelsName.ts`, next to `dropAiKeyUnlessFor`): always on sign-out, and on a sign-in unless they were saved for that person (damaged ones go too). The same person after a reload keeps them, so nothing is sent and billed again. Only the `note-board:ai-categories` entry is ever removed.
- 2026-11-15 Delete account removes `note-board:ai-categories` (`accountKeys`) when the labels are that account's, or damaged; another person's stay ("never remove anything that isn't the account's own AI labels"). On the published site the watcher has already dropped anyone else's, so in practice they always go; the check matters for two demo people sharing one browser in local runs. The label cache, the watcher and Delete account read the saved form with one function (`readAiLabels`), so they agree on what is damaged.
- 2026-11-15 A failed label save now pauses Sort automatically only for the person it failed for (found in the high review: the watcher removes the labels without telling the label cache, so the next person on the same page would otherwise stay paused).
- 2026-11-15 The Brain dump text is emptied and its dialog closed on every sign-in change in `watchUser` (src/sync/session.ts), next to `feedback.reset()`. On a page load the box is already empty, so nothing changes there. The demo people (local runs) have no sign-out, so nothing was needed there.
- 2026-11-15 The privacy page says nothing about the Sort with AI labels kept in the browser (before this change too); left as is for the owner to decide.
- 2026-10-31 (owner decision, Eric: "Only size"): on Enter only the text size carries onto the new line; bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, font and highlight don't (settles point (2) of the 2026-10-30 Enter decision, which left it to the owner).
- 2026-10-31 (follow-ups to the check of #224; calls made by the worker under rule 4's exception). (1) The size list presses the size the box shows, rounded: 14 for the old Small (13.6px); the old Large (20.8px, shown as 21) presses nothing, since 21 isn't in the list, and − and + step from 21 as before. (2) A new Version history copy isn't made when the newest one holds the same boards saved by an older version of the app (only the version number differs): the fingerprint is compared with the boards as each version from 3 up would have saved them (code review: also for versions saved before fingerprints existed, and for the Claude connector's safety copy, which takes effect once the newest `busyants.mcpb` is installed). A pressed 14 for old Small words, picked again, makes them 14px (from 13.6px), as picking any size does. (3) The held-back-changes note (review 181-A H1) is still written as if saved by version 3, so notes already on devices keep matching, but the version number is now set by reading the saved boards, not by matching the start of the text. (4) The greyed − at 10 and + at 36 already kept the cursor and selection in the note in Chromium (a browser test now guards it); the code review pointed out that other engines (Safari) may not pass a press on a disabled button on to the toolbar, so they are now marked unavailable (`aria-disabled`) instead of disabled, and a press does nothing. (5) The "stray size mark that drops words back to 16" didn't reproduce. What did: deleting every letter of a line (Backspace, Ctrl+Backspace) and typing again gives plain text, for every look (bold too), as before the size box; left as is (Google Docs keeps the look there; a choice for the owner).
- 2026-10-23 (owner request, job 193: "could I change it the other way around, left click to drag, and holding down both left and right + middle click to select around"; recommended options, rule 4 exception; reverses the mapping of job 188 / PR #183: the 2026-10-22 entry's "mouse pan" part is superseded, and its file `usePanButtons.ts` is now `useBoxButtons.ts`). (1) **Left-drag pans:** a left press on empty board starts a pan (mouse only; the glove, `.canvas.panning`, shows from the first move, not on a plain click). Blocks, items and handles still take their own presses. (2) **Middle / both buttons draw the box:** `usePanButtons.ts` became `useBoxButtons.ts`, with the same window-level detection, cancelling of a begun left drag, refusal of click / menu / middle-click autoscroll / new tab, and ending on release, blur or cancel; instead of panning it calls three small functions given by `Canvas.tsx` (begin, move, end) that reuse the existing selection-box code, including edge panning. The box clears the selection unless Ctrl / Shift / ⌘ was held when the buttons went down. (3) **Shift and Ctrl with a left-drag on empty board keep drawing the add-to-selection box** (the old behavior; the spec only named Ctrl, the code also took Shift and ⌘) rather than panning, so adding to a selection keeps a left-button way. (4) **Read-only board:** middle / both buttons do nothing there (it has no selection box, job 161; the hook does not start, so a left-drag that is panning carries on), and a left-drag pans as before. (6) A left press on empty board still clears the selection at once, as the old Hand tool did, so a pan also clears it; not changed. (5) The cursor is the arrow everywhere except the glove while panning with the left button; the shortcuts list has a row for each. Touch is unchanged. The e2e file `pan-buttons.spec.ts` became `mouse-buttons.spec.ts`.
- 2026-11-09 (job sync-keeps-cursor) The guard sits in the one keyboard handler every Delete / Backspace block delete goes through (`useShortcuts`), fed by `src/components/lostCursor.ts`: it notes the block text box the cursor is in and forgets it only when the person leaves it on purpose (a click or tap that moves the focus, Tab or another text box, or a key the app answers by taking the cursor out: Escape, an arrow onto a card with no text box, Ctrl+A in a list, pasting copied items). Done on the phone item bar counts as leaving on purpose too. A focus move within 0.6 s of the person's own press, drag, release or click counts as theirs (a tap moves the focus a moment after it is let go), unless the box was taken off the page. A checklist item's tick box and buttons are tracked like its text box. While the cursor has fallen out of that box any other way, Delete, Backspace (and the board's own Delete key) and the arrows only let the selected blocks go (as undo-keeps-cursor does when no item is left): they delete nothing, nudge no block and pan nothing, for every press until a click, tap or focus move, so a held Backspace can't delete the block either. Ctrl+A and arrow keys pressed on the page don't turn it off, since someone who kept typing may press them too. The tracking listeners are started by the board's shortcuts and stopped with them. A Version history restore on this device needs no guard (it starts with a click, and lets the selection go already); one made on another device arrives as a sync.
- 2026-11-09 (job sync-keeps-cursor, code review) A row moved by another device (Ctrl+Shift+Up there) keeps the cursor in it here, so that case needed nothing; a browser test now checks it.
- 2026-11-09 (job sync-keeps-cursor, owner decision on PR #242) Only Delete, Backspace, the board's own Delete key and the arrow keys (plain or with Shift) are stopped while the cursor is lost. Every other key works exactly as without the guard and neither lets the selection go nor ends the lost state: Shift, Ctrl, ⌘ or Alt alone, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+D, Ctrl+A, Ctrl+arrows (switching boards) and Ctrl+Shift+Backspace (which asks first). The first version let the selection go at any board key, which broke Ctrl+C and Ctrl+D after a sync.
- 2026-11-09 (job sync-keeps-cursor, code review of the narrowing) A click made by a key (Space or Enter on a tick box or button) no longer counts as the person's press. Switching boards with Ctrl+arrows forgets the lost box, so the new board's keys work at once. The guard now sits after the "Delete completed items?" question's own key rule, so it never closes that question. The rule for which keys are stopped lives in `src/model/hotkeys.ts` with the other key rules. Left alone: the person's own undo that takes the box away also stops these keys until a click (it lets the selection go already, so nothing is lost), and the box is remembered until the next click or focus move.
- 2026-11-03 Tap limits (follow-ups to job 224; worker calls, owner approved the follow-ups): the tick box tap's own numbers (10px, 500ms) meant the same as `DRAG_THRESHOLD` (5px: a press that moves further is a drag or pan) and `HOLD_MS` (450ms: a press that rests longer is a long press), which the zoom taps on the empty board already use through `pressKind`, so the tick box now calls `pressKind` with those two constants instead of its own. A tap is a bit stricter (5px, 450ms); a thumb that wobbles more than 5px was a pan on the board and now ticks nothing on the box too.
- 2026-11-03 Pinch edge case: reproduced with a two-finger touch test: a second finger anywhere while the first rests on a tick box, then the second lifting first, ticked the box (the box only counted the fingers left on screen at the lift). The box now listens for any non-primary pointer down during its press and treats the press as a pinch. The board's other taps (zoom taps, narrowing a selection) already cancel on a second finger; their tests were added as guards and passed unchanged.
- 2026-11-19 (job text-size-box) Owner decisions for the size box on whole-box formatting ("in journal there are many different font sizes that you can go up and down to, can you add that into the main checklist as well"): (1) Where: every whole-box text format, not only the Checklist panel. The small format bar's Small / Normal / Large becomes the Journal's number size box, for checklist items, notes, card titles and container titles, on boards and in the Checklist panel. (2) A size still covers the whole box or item; no per-word sizes. (3) Old app versions: accepted. Numeric sizes are saved as numbers; an older app version shows them as normal (and would save them so), and the owner reloads devices. No backward-compatible duplicate fields.
- 2026-11-19 (job text-size-box, call) The saved version number stays 6: the new numbers are recorded as read safely under 6 in `persist-roundtrip.test.ts`, as owner decision (3) above allows, with a test of what an older copy does (it keeps the box's words, bold, italic and font; only the size goes back to normal). Raising it to 7 would have made every older tab refuse the boards and needed a new Claude connector install. The Claude connector reads and keeps numbered sizes once rebuilt from this code; an older installed connector would reset them to normal if it edits that board.
- 2026-11-19 (job text-size-box, call) The px shown is the box's real size at the A− / A+ Normal step: 14 for note text, items and container titles, 15 for a checklist's or link's title (its usual size). A size is saved as absolute px and drawn as px ÷ the box's usual px, so it still scales with A− / A+. A title's 15 is not in the list: − and + go to 14 and 16; to get 15 back, undo.
- 2026-11-19 (job text-size-box, call) Old words map to the nearest whole px of how they are drawn (0.85 and 1.3 of the usual size): Small 12 and Large 18 (13 and 20 on a title). They keep drawing exactly as before until changed; − / + step from the shown number, as the Journal does.
- 2026-11-19 (job text-size-box, call) Journal ↔ checklist paste: a size is the same px on both sides (an item and normal Journal text are both 14px). The Journal's old Small / Large words become 12 / 20 on an item (the same as an item's Small / Large going into the Journal); a Heading 1 with no size becomes 18, the nearest to the Large it used to become.
- 2026-11-19 (job text-size-box, call) The tick box keeps its current rule: it grows with A− / A+ and stays centered on the first line at any per-item size, as with Large before.
- 2026-11-19 (job text-size-box, call) The Journal's size box was moved into a shared component (`SizeBox.tsx`) used by both bars; in the small format bar it is drawn flat to fit its 28px row. Escape in the bar closes an open size list first.
- 2026-11-19 (job text-size-box, call, from the review) On a phone, text boxes keep their 16px floor, so sizes 10 to 14 all draw at 16px there while the box shows the number; the spec says so. Lowering the floor would make phone browsers zoom into the text box.
- 2026-10-23 Text size: three new steps after `maximum`, saved as `colossal` (300%), `immense` (350%) and `ultimate` (400%). Existing names and values are unchanged, so values saved on a device still load; unknown names still fall back to Normal. Why: owner request "the font text size can still be increased", pick "A+ up to 400%".
- 2026-10-23 Tick box: 20px at Normal (was 16px), times the same step as before (never under 1), so 50px at 250% and 80px at 400%; the tick mark and the press area (4px of padding at Normal) scale with it, and it stays centered on the first line. Why: owner request "the checkboxes can be still a bit bigger" (about 1.25 times). The phone layout keeps its 16px box and its 44px-wide press area (its text size still follows the step); making the phone 20px was possible but the phone's touch layout is built around the 16px box, so not changed without the owner asking.
- 2026-10-23 Card and container header buttons (collapse arrow, trash can) stop growing at 250% (80px, 40px icon); 300%, 350% and 400% keep that size. Why: two 128px buttons (the 400% size) are wider than a 200px card, so they would run off its edge or cover the title; the owner's rule is that nothing overlaps or runs off the edge. Simplest fix: one cap on the shared step variable.
- 2026-10-23 Checklist item hover buttons (trash, grip, move arrows) and the strip kept free for them stop growing at 190% (strip 87px, was up to 115px at 250%); their height keeps following the item's first line. Why: at 400% the tick box is 80px, and tick, its 6px gap and a 115px strip do not fit the 174px row of a 200px card, so the box would cover the trash can. 87px is the largest strip that still fits. The item buttons at 200%-250% are therefore a little smaller than PR #191 made them. A 240px card has about 50px of text room left at 400%; a 200px card none (the text box may shrink to nothing, so it never covers a button): widen the card. Cards are not widened by themselves (kept from PR #191).
- 2026-10-23 The item text box's grid track may shrink below one letter (`minmax(0, 1fr)`), so a very narrow row no longer pushes the text over the item buttons. Why: found in the 400% check in a container; the text box was one letter wide at minimum and overlapped the trash can.
- 2026-10-23 Everything else that grows with the step (Completed header, Clean up, Uncheck all, link and board card controls, the delete questions, the drag label) is unchanged: it follows the step up to 400% by the existing rules. The header buttons still drop under a card's title from 225% up (PR #191).
- 2026-11-13 (owner request, job tick-animation: "a small animation that feels good when you complete a checklist item"). Coordinator calls: (1) about 200ms, ease-out: the box scales 0.85 to 1 while its tick grows in (`background-size`, a tiny paint on a 20px box; a real draw-along stroke would need an extra element), the text fade and the strike color ease in; no confetti, sound or layout shift. (2) Only on the person's own tick: the row sets a local `tick-pop` class from the box's change handler and from Ctrl+Enter, cleared after 260ms and ignored once the item is not ticked; so undo, redo, sync, loading, Clean up and a parent ticked by its children never play it, and only the clicked item does. (3) CSS only (transform, opacity, color). (4) Same on desktop and phone; no hover or tap-highlight changes. (5) `SPEC.md` ticking and "Honey well" lines updated. No change to board data, saving or undo.
- 2026-11-13 (owner request, job tick-animation: "I'd like it to override a device's reduced motion setting"). The tick animation plays the same with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` (like the Visualizer). It is scoped to the tick animation only: its rules sit outside any reduced-motion media query, and the app has no global rule that cancels animations. Tested in both settings in `e2e/tick-animation.spec.ts`.
- 2026-10-23 Ctrl+Shift+Backspace deletes every ticked item on the open board (top-level and sub-items, with everything under them), after asking as before. Before, it only deleted the Completed sections' items. Why: owner's pick for job 200, now that there is no Completed section.
- 2026-10-23 The per-list `completedOpen` flag stays in the saved data and new lists still set it, but nothing reads it. Why: removing it would change the saved format, so older copies of the app might refuse new boards. No migration runs and nothing is reordered on load.
- 2026-10-23 (call, recommended) The tick's leave-and-arrive animation (280ms ease-out, then a 450ms amber tint) is removed. A tick applies at once, also with motion allowed. Why: nothing moves any more, and the strike-through shows the change straight away.
- 2026-10-23 (call, recommended) A ticked item is struck through and faded, using the "Honey well" look from job 201 (a softer strike line and the text at 55%), for items and sub-items alike.
- 2026-10-23 (call, recommended) Clean up in the title row shows while anything in an open list is ticked, not only on hover. It is hidden on a collapsed list (Arrange > Clean up still reaches it), on view-only boards and in exported pictures. On a phone it shows only on the selected list, like the collapse arrow and trash can (job 113), so a tap on another list can't clean it up by accident. On a computer it stays visible, not only on hover like the collapse and trash buttons (owner decision 2026-10-16), because it is the list's only way to clean up and shows only when there is something to clean. Why: the owner said "shows only while something is ticked", and a hover-only button can't be found on a touch screen.
- 2026-10-23 (call, recommended) The rules that kept ticked and open items apart now treat them alike: Up/Down and Ctrl+Shift+Up/Down pass through ticked items, Delete at the end of an item joins the next item even if it is ticked (the joined item stays ticked only when both were, and the 2026-10-22 rule unticks anything an open item would end up under, so Clean up never sweeps away text that was never ticked), Ctrl+A and range selection take ticked items, and Tab can nest an item under a ticked one (which unticks it, as the 2026-10-22 rule already says). Backspace still can't delete a list's last open item, so the cursor always has an open item to go to.
- 2026-10-23 (call, recommended) Ctrl+Enter (and the phone's Tick) moves the cursor to the nearest open item below in the list, now including an open sub-item of the item just ticked, since that is the next row down.
- 2026-10-23 (call, recommended) Screen reader: a click on one tick box says nothing extra, because the checkbox already announces its new state. Ctrl+Enter and the phone's Tick say "Ticked: Buy milk" / "Unticked: Buy milk", because the cursor moves on and no box is focused. Several items at once say "Ticked: N items" / "Unticked: N items". The old "Moved to Completed" message is gone. A refused Ctrl+Enter (read-only board, an old version shown) leaves the cursor where it was.
- 2026-10-23 (call, recommended) Restoring an item from the Completed panel is unchanged.
- 2026-10-23 (left for later) The website's About page demo video and its sample checklist still show ticked items moving under "Completed". Re-recording the video needs ffmpeg and a separate job.
- 2026-10-23 (call, recommended) Ctrl+Shift+Backspace deletes a ticked sub-item with everything under it, as Clean up already moves it with everything under it. Open sub-items can only be under a ticked item if the item was ticked itself (the 2026-10-22 rule unticks parents when an open item is moved there), and the question shows the full count first.
- 2026-11-10 (owner request, job tick-lower: after looking at previews of 5%, 7.5% and 10%, picked 7.5%, "B"). The checklist tick box is nudged down 7.5% of its item's line height below the middle of the item's first line (1.5px at Normal, 3px at 200%), replacing 5% (1px at Normal, 2px at 200%). The fold arrow and the item buttons are placed by their own rules, not by the box's `translate`, so they did not move. Changed in `src/styles.css`, `SPEC.md` (the A− / A+ section and the "Honey well" centering rule), `e2e/checklist-centering.spec.ts` (still within half a pixel) and `e2e/card-look.spec.ts`.
- 2026-11-09 (owner request: "ctrl + enter, and completing a checklist item, shouldn't move you down to the next line automatically") After any tick the cursor stays in the item just ticked, at the same place in its text. This replaces the 2026-10-15 call (job #43: the cursor moves to the nearest open item below, else above) and the 2026-10-19 "audit job A" call (the phone item bar's Tick moves it the same way). The "stay put" path is the one that already ran when no open item was left (job 223, 2026-10-30), so the keyboard stays up on a phone.
- 2026-11-09 (call, recommended) The next-open-item helper (`nextOpenItem` in `src/model/checklist.ts`) and its tests are deleted, since nothing else used it (rule 11).
- 2026-11-09 (call, recommended) The screen-reader message "Ticked: Buy milk" / "Unticked: Buy milk" for Ctrl+Enter and the phone's Tick stays as is. Why: the cursor still stays in a text box, so no tick box is pressed to announce it; the message is still the only spoken sign of the change.
- 2026-11-09 (call, recommended) Clicking or tapping a tick box needed no change: it already leaves the cursor where it was (job 218), so only Ctrl+Enter and the phone's Tick changed.
- 2026-11-09 (call, recommended) With several items selected, Ctrl+Enter still ticks them all and the cursor stays in the item it was pressed in.
- 2026-10-29 Delete in a ticked checklist item (owner report, job 218; coordinator's calls): with the cursor anywhere in a ticked item's text and nothing selected, Delete removes the item and everything nested under it, exactly as its trash can (same function, one undo step), instead of deleting a letter or joining the item below. The cursor goes where the phone's trash leaves it: the item above, else the one below. Ticked text is editable, so Backspace keeps editing it. Open items are unchanged; Delete at the end of an open item still joins a ticked item below it.
- 2026-10-29 Root cause of the card being deleted: clicking a tick box leaves the keyboard on the box (a checkbox, not a text field), so the board's "not while typing" check let Delete / Backspace through to "delete the selected card". Fixed at the board's delete key: when the focus is anywhere inside a checklist item row (tick box, trash, arrows), Delete / Backspace (or the person's own Delete key) remove that item, open or ticked, never the card.
- 2026-10-29 With letters of a ticked item selected, Delete deletes just those letters, and Shift/Ctrl/Alt/⌘+Delete are left to the text (worker calls: a selection or a modifier shows the person means the text). Only a fresh press removes an item: after Delete or Backspace removed one, the held key's repeats do nothing in the item the cursor moved to, until the key is pressed again, so it can't remove or join more items (worker call after the high review).
- 2026-10-29 A clicked tick box now takes the keyboard in every browser (Safari and Firefox on a Mac don't focus a clicked checkbox, so the keyboard went to the page and Delete would still have deleted the card there). With the focus on a tick box, the board's own Delete key, if the person changed it, removes the item too. If other blocks are selected as well (Ctrl+A with the focus on a box), Delete deletes the selection as before (worker calls after the high review).
- 2026-10-29 Not changed, for the owner to decide: Ctrl+Enter on a list's last open item still leaves the field with the list selected (as the spec says), so a Delete right after it still deletes the whole list (undoable with Ctrl+Z).
- 2026-10-29 Tapping a tick box with a finger or pen while typing in a text box of the same card leaves the cursor there, so an iPhone's or Android phone's keyboard stays up (owner: "fix iPhone first", job 218 round 2). That holds on the box and on the wider pressable area around it. Otherwise the box takes the keyboard, as a mouse click does, so Delete from a tablet's hardware keyboard removes the item, not the card; a tap on a box in another card moves the focus to that box (worker calls after the high review). No focus ring after a click: `focus({ focusVisible: false })` (Chrome 145, Firefox 104, Safari 18.4; older browsers ignore it). On Android Chrome a tap while typing no longer moves the focus to the box, which it did before this change (same rule on every phone). Not done: the reviewer's claim that Safari before 18.4 carries a text box's ring over to the box could not be checked here (no WebKit), so no workaround was added.
- 2026-10-29 An item's trash can moves the cursor to the item above, else below (owner-approved follow-up to job 218, job 222; replaces the 2026-10-19 job 115 line "The trash can on a computer is unchanged and does not move the cursor"), on every screen, mouse or keyboard: it now runs the same code as the phone's item bar and Delete in a ticked item (the "keep typing" switch is gone; it was on everywhere but the trash can). Worker calls: when the list is left empty, the cursor goes into the one blank item the list always keeps (the existing rule, and the same as the phone's Delete), not to the card's title or the card itself, since a focused card would let Delete delete it. A mouse click on the trash while not typing also puts the cursor in the item above (no exception, so the focus is never on the page). With items selected across several lists, the trash removes them all as before and the cursor goes to the item above in the trash's own list.
- 2026-10-29 Found in the high review (job 222, worker calls): Delete or Backspace with items selected had the same bug (focus left on the page, a second Delete deleted the card), so it now runs the trash can's code and moves the cursor the same way. Not changed: Ctrl+X on selected items still leaves the focus on the page (a cut, not a delete; a possible follow-up). Left as is, because they are how Delete already works where the cursor lands (job 218), and Ctrl+Z undoes them: a Delete right after the trash, with the cursor at the end of the item above, joins the next item into it; and if the item above is ticked, a Delete removes it. A held Enter on a keyboard-focused trash can removes one item, and its repeats do nothing in the item the cursor moved to (as held Delete since job 218).
- 2026-10-29 Job 222 rework after /check-pr (worker calls): the trash can (and Delete with items selected) moves the cursor only when nothing is being typed in, or the item being typed in is one of those removed; while typing in any text box that stays (another item of the same list or another list, a title, a note) the cursor stays there, as it did before job 222 (the coordinator offered "same list moves too"; the high review showed that pulls the cursor out of the item being typed in, so the narrower rule was taken). Enter with Ctrl, Alt, Shift or ⌘ on a focused trash can does nothing (the browser would otherwise press it, without the held-key guard). A held Delete or Backspace with items selected removes once and its repeats do nothing in the item the cursor moved to (as held Delete since job 218). With items selected in several lists, the cursor goes to the list the selection started in: the list a drag started from, or the list Ctrl+A was first pressed in (the Ctrl+A ladder now keeps that list as the selection's start instead of the column's first list, so that list's card stays the selected one).
- 2026-11-09 Undo or redo while typing in a checklist item (job undo-keeps-cursor, owner OK 2026-11-09; worker calls): the fix is in the one place every undo and redo of the open board goes through (Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y, the toolbar and phone buttons, the Undo message, the phone item bar), and acts only when the cursor was in a checklist item. While the item is still drawn (even moved by the undo, in its list or to another list), it is asked to take the cursor again, at the place in the text the cursor had (highlighted letters stay highlighted), because the browser loses the cursor when React moves or redraws its row (code review); when the step changed that item's text, at the end of the text instead, as the browser puts it when a text box's text is replaced (owner, 2026-11-09: reading the place before the step put the cursor after "hello" on a redo of " world"). Else the cursor goes to the nearest item still drawn in the same list, above, else below; if none of the items it had is left but the list still draws items (its contents were replaced), the list's first item. When the list draws no item (the undo collapsed the card or its container, or took the card away), the whole selection is let go, not only that card, because any selected block with the cursor on the page is what Backspace deletes (code review; without typing, an undo that takes a card away keeps the other selected blocks selected). "Drawn" is one function (`drawnItems` in src/model/undoCursor.ts, built on `visibleItems`), so folded items (PR #239) hide their sub-items here too once `visibleItems` skips them. The phone item bar's Undo now uses the same code, so it no longer sends the cursor to an item hidden in a collapsed card. Left as is: `focusAfterRemoving` (the trash can's cursor rule) is not reused, since it doesn't know which rows are drawn after the change. Clicking or tapping Undo or Redo while typing in a checklist item, a note or a title (the toolbar's, the phone top bar's, and the Undo message's Undo) leaves the cursor there (the button doesn't take the focus from it on a press, as the phone item bar's buttons don't), so the same cursor rule applies and a Backspace after it edits text (owner, 2026-11-09: "Fix all 3, then live"; it used to move the keyboard to the button with the card still selected, so a Backspace deleted the card). Reached with Tab, the buttons still take the focus and show their ring. Notes and titles get it too (code review: the same trap there); a text box outside the board (a panel's, the board name) loses the cursor as before, since some save on leaving. When the undo moves the item being typed in to another list, that list becomes the selected card, as while typing in it (code review). An item counts as moved only into a list that didn't have it before, so the cursor never jumps to another list's item that merely shares its id (code review). Left for later (code review, the wider cause): any change that takes a focused text box off the screen with its card still selected (a synced change, a Version history restore, or an undo that hides a note) still drops the cursor to the page; a guard in the one Backspace / Delete handler would cover them all. The phone top bar's buttons sit under the item bar while typing, so that case has no browser test.

Any sort stops after the first batch it cannot save ("Can't save sorted labels on this device."), so at most one batch is billed for nothing.
- 2026-11-13 (worker call on the owner answer, job "viz-ai-autosort"). The 2,000-label cap is kept (the labels share localStorage with the boards) and Sort automatically never pushes a label out: it pauses when the labels kept plus the ones it would add pass 2,000 ("Sort automatically is paused: this device keeps at most 2,000 sorted labels."). Only a press drops the oldest. This is the simplest option that cannot loop: an automatic run only ever adds labels, each item at most once, so it can send at most 2,000 items in all before it pauses. The other option (only send items finished since the oldest kept label) needs a date per label, a change to the saved format.

- 2026-11-13 (owner answer, job "viz-ai-autosort"). `docs/play-store/data-safety.md` now says the text is sent when an AI button is pressed or when the Visualizer sorts by itself, with a sub-item's parent text. The recommended Play Console ticks stay the same; the owner re-checks the Play Console form against it.

## The boards keep their room in storage (2026-11-13, coordinator re-review of PR #300)

The sorted labels and the saved boards share the space the browser gives the app, so filling it with labels could
make a board save fail. Decision: no sort, pressed or automatic, writes labels unless at least 1,000,000 letters
stay free, or twice what the boards take now if that is more (`BOARDS_ROOM`), checked by writing and removing a
test of that size. 1,000,000 is about a fifth of a usual 5 MB allowance and far more than a typical board save;
twice the boards' size covers a board save rewriting the whole save and growing. A press that would use that room
writes nothing and says so in plain words; an automatic run pauses, as it already did when it could not save.

## Only an unsaved label pauses Sort automatically (2026-11-13, coordinator re-review of PR #300)

Labels that cannot be saved come back as their own kind of failure (`labels-not-saved`), so the AI-use counter's
storage failure, which has its own message, no longer shows as "Can't save sorted labels on this device" and no
longer pauses Sort automatically.

## Whether an automatic run may send is decided in one place (2026-11-13, coordinator re-review of PR #300)

`autoSortDue` now takes the label store's answer (`room`) and whether this run is already paused, and answers
"no", "send", "full" or "cant-save". The Visualizer row only shows what it says, so the rule is in the pure model
layer where unit tests reach it (CLAUDE.md: no rules logic in components).

Up to about 8 items it stays one at a time. At most 8 beams and fountains are drawn at once (the oldest are cut short; every item still lands, lights its dash and joins the feed). Why: the old loop already allowed overlap but ran at about one a second, so it never looked busy; waves look like the second reference clip and the cap keeps the frame cost bounded.
- 2026-11-13 A fountain has 10 sparks, shared out so about 60 are in the air together (never under 4; about 70% on a stage under 520px wide). The count is worked out once per loop from the busiest moment, so no spark pops in or out. Why: the frame budget with up to 8 fountains at once; fewer sparks per fountain when crowded looks the same as a dense burst but costs much less.

Why: a card per item would flicker and bury the disc; the brief overlap is a known limit, small enough not to need a third place.

(b) keptToken() reads getAuth().currentUser while the Visualizer reads collab.me(); collab.me() is set only inside start(user), which runs only from the onAuthStateChanged handler with the Firebase user, so currentUser is always set first — no timing gap. (c) each range switch refetches (one fetch per open and range, as decided); the model re-filters by range, so the widest-window-once alternative is only an efficiency option, not a correctness fix. (d) `vizWindow` has its own small local-midnight helper, like the Calendar panel's in src/store/calendar.ts; sharing one is a tidy-up for later, not a behavior change.
