- 2026-11-13 The names stand above the disc's back and below its front, never beside it, and the disc is sized to the stage's width (the largest disc for which every name fits at every turn, worked out by `fitDisc` in `src/model/vizNames.ts`; the old width-reserve for the side names and `LABEL_RADIUS` are gone). Why: the owner said "make the disc bigger, move the names out"; beside the disc the names were what held its width back.
- 2026-11-13 A name whose wedge crosses the disc's far left or right edge hops from the back to the front, and fades out and back in over about 1.5 seconds each side of the swap (about 7 degrees of turn, at one turn per 80 seconds). Why: when the disc fills the width there is no room beside it, so the hop cannot be made continuous; a fade looks calmer than a jump. Known limit: a short name flickers out briefly twice a turn.
- 2026-11-13 Names that would touch are moved apart, up or down, so a crowded corner stacks them in a short column; they never overlap. Why: with up to 17 names this is the simplest rule that keeps every name readable.
- 2026-11-13 On a short stage or a narrow phone with long names the disc shrinks (steps of 7%, down to a floor of 40px of half width, where names may then touch on a tiny stage) until every name fits at 72 sample angles round a whole turn; it is checked on the pure model, so it never needs the browser. Why: "pick the largest disc that still keeps the names readable". Known limit: the sample is 72 angles, so a name could touch another by a pixel between samples on a very crowded stage.
- 2026-11-13 A name is cut (ending in …, keeping its first word) only if wider than the stage; before, it was cut whenever the old fifth-of-the-width reserve was too small. The tilt (0.62) is unchanged. The canvas publishes the disc's size as `data-disc` (middle x, middle y, half width, stage width, stage height) for the browser tests.
- 2026-11-13 Each item's sparks follow a path worked out by `flowAt` in `src/model/vizGeometry.ts` (pure, tested): they leave the cell, run inward on the disc's tilt along the cell's wedge (swinging sideways at most 0.4 radians and never out of the wedge), then lift off the hub into the core, slow at first and quickest at the end. Why: the owner asked for the energy to flow from the categories into the center; it replaces the old arc from the cell to the core. The same ten-per-item sparks and sixty-in-the-air cap apply, so the flow adds no particles.
- 2026-11-13 No steady idle flow along lit categories. Why: the reference's calm moments show only the discrete bursts and a still disc; a constant drift would add motion the reference does not have and more particles to the budget. Easy to add later (a few capped drifting sparks per lit wedge using `flowAt`) if the owner wants it.
- 2026-11-13 Arc height: the fountains' arc is `(0.2 + 0.3 * lift) * disc half width * sin(pi * q^0.8)` above the path (q is how far along the spark is, so the top comes about 2/5 of the way). That puts the top 0.075, 0.103 and 0.131 of the disc's width above the path for the lowest, middle and highest spark; I measured the reference at about 0.07 to 0.14 (mean about 0.10, the near cells rising about 0.25 of the width almost upright), and the old arcs at 0.021, 0.049 and 0.077 (the first flow version 0.012 to 0.032). The same ten sparks and sixty-in-the-air cap, so no more particles. Why: the owner asked for the arcs to match the video. Known limit: measured by eye from stills, so within about 0.02.
- 2026-11-13 Cards: four places (upper right, lower left, upper left, lower right), found by `cardRoom` / `cardSlotsFor` / `pickSlot` in `src/model/vizBurst.ts` (pure, tested). Each is held inside the same ring the rim names stay outside of, which is what guarantees no card touches a name; a card is cut to the room its place has (up to 240px). The gap is 0.3 seconds, the life 2 seconds, and a list of 8 or fewer gets a card for every item (`SMALL_LIST`). A new card takes the place of the oldest still on screen; the old one fades out in 0.15 seconds before the new one fades in. A place needs 80px of room, so a phone (and a small laptop window under 600px wide) uses only the lower pair; the cards there are narrow and their text is cut with an ellipsis. Why: the owner's request for cards on more items without overlap. Known limit: on a phone only two cards show at once, so in a long list items beyond that wait for a place (a new card takes the oldest's).
- 2026-11-13 Beam width: a beam is drawn as a pink glow sprite 0.029 of the disc's half width wide (at least 8px) and a white-hot core 0.011 of it (at least 2.4px), from `beamWidths` in `src/model/vizGeometry.ts` (pure, tested); the glow is drawn at 0.75 and the core at 0.9 (was 0.5 and 0.85). I measured the reference's beams at 4 to 6px across at half brightness on a disc about 930px wide, about 0.0055 of its width (flat-topped pink, about 8px at the base); a sprite is half bright at 0.38 of its width. Before, the glow was 7 * unit px (3.2px at half brightness: 0.0034 of the disc's width on a big window, 0.0065 on a phone) and the core 1.8 * unit (0.8px). After: about 5.2px glow and 2px core at half brightness on a 469px half width (0.0055), 3px and 1px on a phone (0.0124, held up by the floors so a beam is seen). Why: the owner asked for thicker beams. Known limit: measured by eye from stills, within about 1px. The landing column and the core's pillar are unchanged.
- 2026-11-14 (owner: "both middle and lowest level items can count") Every ticked descendant of a Completed entry counts in the Visualizer, middle levels too. This was already how it worked; now recorded in SPEC.md and pinned by a unit test.
- 2026-11-14 (owner: "today, now, meow, now, etc. or any variations of these or any seeming words that don't really have any inherent meaning to them should not be counted") `isTimeWord` became `isEmptyWord` (`src/model/categorize.ts`). Same whole-text match, case-insensitive, trailing punctuation/emoji ignored, 40-character early return. Effects as for time words: never a Visualizer item, a vague parent, never sent by Sort with AI; stats outside the Visualizer unchanged.
- 2026-11-14 The exact list is a worker call, kept conservative: the old time words; short forms rn, tn, tdy, tmr, tmrw, 2day, 2nite, 2morrow, tonite, to-day; fillers meow, test, testing, asdf, qwerty, sdf, lol, idk, hmm, ok, okay, yes, no, misc, stuff, thing, things, task, tasks, item, items, list, untitled, new item, new task, done, blah, placeholder. Real short tasks (gym, eat, run, mop, tax, vet) are not on it and have tests.
- 2026-11-14 Variants without a fuzzy library: a run of three or more of the same letter shrinks to two or one ("sooon" to "soon", "nowww" to "now"), and two at the very end shrink to one ("yess"). One ASCII letter alone, or one letter repeated ("x", "aaa", "xx"), and text with no letters at all (digits, symbols, emoji, empty) count as empty. A single non-Latin character (such as a one-character word in Chinese) is not treated as empty.
- 2026-11-14 Known limit: a real word that collapses to a listed word (for example a trailing double letter) would be skipped; none of the listed words has a common real task that does, and the list can be pruned if one turns up. Some existing test fixtures used single letters as item text and now use "a-1" style texts.
- 2026-11-14 A ticked sub-item that is an empty word is skipped, but if every ticked sub-item of an entry is one ("Groceries" with ticked "1" and "ok"), the parent still counts as itself, so nothing the owner finished disappears. "C++", "A+" and "C#" are not single letters. Calendar events are not run through the empty-word check (the spec only covers board items).
- 2026-11-13 Visualizer motion: the owner decided "Always full motion". The Visualizer no longer reads `prefers-reduced-motion`; a Calm button in its own "Motion" group beside the range and group switches turns the still drawing on. It is saved as `note-board:viz-calm` (`1` on, `0` off; anything unreadable means off) in localStorage on this device only, not board data. The rest of the app keeps following reduce motion.
- 2026-11-13 Visualizer pacing: waves are about a third of the list (up to 16, 60ms apart), at most 16 in the air, spark budget about 100. Trip is 2.66s (0.7s beam drop, 1.82s spark flight). Tension: for a moderate list the wait between waves is long (about 12s at 36 items), because the loop length target of about 45s is kept; a shorter pause would mean a shorter loop.
- 2026-11-13 Core glow: the flare and the per-landing pulses share one soft cap (0.5 x tanh of the sum), so overlapping pulses never stack past it. A pulse rises in 0.15s and eases back over 0.4s.
- 2026-11-13 Name cards grow up to 1.4x only while the same number of places still fit; otherwise they shrink toward the first size. Rim names are 1.3x. On a phone the ring is 70dvh with the controls first (display: contents on the info column) and the × fixed.
- 2026-11-12 Visualizer names: the disc shrinks so the longest name fits beside it (at most a fifth of the width reserved, so a phone keeps a usable disc); a cut name keeps at least its first word (up to 8 letters). The hub label may still be cut, since the hub is small.
- 2026-11-12 Visualizer look (owner request: "make it exactly like the video I sent"): matched a reference clip for look and motion only; no text, labels, data or code from it. Kept our own categories, board names and counts.
- 2026-11-12 Lit cells are all the same magenta (the reference look) instead of each category's color; the category's color stays only as the small dot beside its name on the rim.
- 2026-11-12 The disc keeps turning slowly (the reference disc turns) and the hub shows the name of the wedge that landed last; kept the hub text short rather than adding any data panel.
- 2026-11-12 Particle paths are a pure function of the item's position (`particleSeed`/`particleAt` in `src/model/vizGeometry.ts`), so a loop always replays the same and the paths are unit-tested.
- 2026-11-12 Static parts of the picture are painted once into a cached layer rebuilt only on resize, to keep the frame budget.
- 2026-11-15 (owner choice) When the calendar fetch hits its 5-page cap, the events left out are the oldest, not the newest. Google can only list events oldest-first (`orderBy` is `startTime` or `updated`; there is no descending order), so when the first read of the whole range hits the cap the range is read again in 30-day pieces, newest piece first, until 5 extra pages are spent (a piece's first page is free, so quiet months cost nothing). A piece the pages cannot finish is left out whole (unless it is the newest), so what is kept is an unbroken run up to today. A calendar that fits in the cap costs the same one fetch as before; only a very full one costs up to about 30 more requests. Why: the recent days matter most in the Visualizer.
- 2026-11-15 (call, recommended) The Visualizer closes when the board goes away (a sign-out), by closing from an effect of the always-present Visualizer component after a zero-delay timer that React's mount, unmount, mount development check cancels. Why: the open flag is kept outside React and outlived the board, so the next sign-in in the same tab reopened it.
- 2026-11-15 (call, recommended) In Calm the Visualizer's newest-cell flash is not drawn at all, so the single picture drawn is the final one. Why: the flash fades over 0.7 s and would have been frozen half-faded once the loop stopped.
- 2026-11-13 (call, owner) Visualizer trips are 1.5x slower: about 4.0s in all (beam drop 1.05s, spark flight 2.73s, same ratio). The ripples and column keep their lengths. Why: owner said "can make it slower and more continuous, but with more things coming in".
- 2026-11-13 (call, owner) About 20 trips in the air (STREAM_FLYING 20), so the beat is trip/20, about 0.2s. MAX_FLYING goes from 16 to 24 for headroom, and the spark budget from 100 to 200 so each fountain keeps its ten sparks. The 0.3s card gap, the core pulse cap, the look and Calm are unchanged.
- 2026-11-13 (call, recommended) Frame cost is up: about twice as many sparks drawn at once (up to about 200). Not measured on a real phone; if it stutters there, lower STREAM_FLYING first.
- 2026-11-13 Visualizer softer cards (owner: "the boxes that come up are a bit too intense and bold and take away from the experience"; calls made with the job): card size 1.4x down to 1.15x of the first size (`CARD_SCALE_MAX`); glow about half as strong (0.45 to 0.22) and half as wide (reach 1.1 to 0.55 of the card height); fill opacity 0.92 to 0.64 and border 1 to 0.7 (about 30% less); title weight 600 to 500 (the muted second line was already regular and is unchanged); fade-in stays 250ms but is now ease-out. The values live together in `CARD_LOOK` in `src/model/vizBurst.ts`. Text stays AA against the card on the dark stage; only over a pure-white spark flash behind the card does the muted second line dip below AA for a moment. Speed, beams, sparks, ripples, core, card gap, rim names and Calm are unchanged.
- 2026-11-13 Visualizer sparks: the owner said the sparks were too small; I made heads bigger with a glow halo and tails long and tapering (length grows with speed), kept 5 particles and the ~0.9 s flight, and let the sparks hold back up to 22% of the run (was 15%) so the cluster fans out and the heads read as separate beads. On a phone the sparks scale with the stage (down to 0.4 of full size) so they stay crisp. Beam, ring, flare and timing of the other phases are unchanged.
- 2026-11-13 (viz-subitem-beams, owner: "the parents, if it has completed sub-items in there, doesn't need to be counted... the parent can be used as context") A Completed entry with completed sub-items is replaced, in the Visualizer only, by those sub-items (nested ones too, each ticked one; an unticked sub-item is ignored). The parent sends no beam and is not counted. An entry with no ticked sub-items is one item as before. Why: the owner asked; the stats on other screens (week review and so on) read the Completed record themselves and are unchanged.
- 2026-11-13 (Owner request, replaces the earlier order: "if a parent item is descriptive enough, then all of its sub-list items should fall under that parent item's categorization... if it is something like today, now, etc., then it should be taken at its own context".) A sub-item's built-in category is the nearest ancestor whose words give a real category, else its own text, then the list title, else Other, all inside `categoryOf` (which takes an optional `context`). The sub-item's cell is the entry's list title. Why: "Today" or "Now" matches nothing, so those sub-items go by their own words; the built-in words have no "gaming", so the unit tests use "Clean the garage" as the parent that carries its sub-items. A single ticked sub-item that itself has ticked sub-items is also an item (every ticked descendant is one), which can count a middle level as well as its children.
- 2026-11-13 Sort with AI: a sub-item is looked up by its own text only, but a descriptive parent's built-in category wins over its AI label (`parentFirst` wraps the AI categorizer in Visualizer.tsx, so `aiCategories.ts` is untouched); otherwise the chain above. Known limit: such a sub-item's label still counts toward the top AI names. Sending the parent's text to the AI as context is a follow-up for the worker who owns that file.
- 2026-11-13 A ticked sub-item with no text is skipped (it would show as "Untitled item" and hide the parent's words).
- 2026-11-13 (owner: "checklist items like today, now, daily, tonight, etc. should not be considered as part of the visualization as a completed item") An item whose whole text is just a time word or phrase is not a Visualizer item (its completed sub-items still are), and as a parent it gives no category. The word list is a coordinator call, kept in `TIME_WORDS` in `src/model/categorize.ts` (`isTimeWord`): today, tonight, now, right now, daily, tomorrow, this morning, morning, this afternoon, afternoon, this evening, evening, this week, weekly, this weekend, weekend, later, soon, asap, todo, to do, to-do, next week, monday to sunday; matched case-insensitively on the whole text with trailing punctuation or emoji ignored.
- 2026-11-13 (owner request) Visualizer and Feedback swap places: on a computer Visualizer gets its own toolbar button in the slot Feedback's button had (the existing Visualizer icon, title and label "Visualizer"), and Feedback becomes a row in ⋯ More, in the slot Visualizer's row had. Each keeps its own visibility rule (Feedback signed in only).
- 2026-11-13 (call, recommended) On a phone the two rows trade positions inside the ⋯ sheet: Feedback takes Visualizer's full-width row under the tiles, Visualizer takes Feedback's row before About BusyAnts. Nothing is added to the phone bar. The More button's tooltip text was updated to match.
- 2026-11-13 (call, recommended) Closing Send feedback no longer gives focus back to a Feedback button, because the row it was opened from leaves with the ⋯ menu (the same as every other ⋯ row). Why: that is how the panels opened from ⋯ already behave.
- 2026-11-11 (plan job 7, `week-review-model`; calls made by the worker under rule 4's exception). (1) **"Done this week" waits for `doneOn`:** `src/model/weekReview.ts` reads an optional `doneOn` ("YYYY-MM-DD") on to-do items if one is present and the item is ticked; items without it (every item today, since the field is plan job 6 and isn't saved yet) are not counted. The model does not add the field to `TodoItem` or `persist.ts`; once job 6 lands the same code counts them with no change. (2) **No streak:** habits give the days ticked in the week (each day once, so a day ticked on two devices counts once) and their count, per the owner's 2026-10-20 "no streak counter". (3) **Shapes of "nothing":** `habits` is null with no Habits board of the person's own (a shared one doesn't count), `nextWeek` is null with no calendar access, `journalCount` is 0 with no Journal board; the screen can tell "no board" from "no ticks". (4) **Habit lists aren't "done" items:** a habit's day items are skipped when counting done items. (5) The day given may be any day; the week is its Monday to Sunday, and next week is the seven days after, so the caller must pass events covering both.
- 2026-11-02 (leftovers of PR #230; the owner said "fix the small leftovers too"; worker calls). (1) A highlight from letter 0 of its first item to the very end of its last item, with nothing typed, removes the last item whole too (matching the joining case); its sub-items move up as usual. With a typed letter the last item stays, holding the letter. A list emptied this way keeps one blank item (as cut does). The cursor goes to the start of the next item, else the end of the item before. A blank last item with the highlight ending at its start is removed too, as in the joining case. (2) Escape with items selected while a container's "Delete …?" asks closes the question and leaves the items selected; Tab is left to the browser so it moves between the question's buttons. The store's item guard (the same one as Delete, Cut and Enter) now also covers Tab and both paste routes. (3) "!" (Shift+1) over a word highlight replaces the words; the other bare printable shortcut, ?, keeps opening the shortcuts list (owner rule). (4) Not changed, noticed: Enter pressed while a button (for example the container's trash can) has the keyboard focus and a word highlight is up still replaces the words with a split before the button sees the key.
